46/// Cap for the caller-supplied `client_id` recorded in a failed-client-auth
47/// audit row (SEC-087). Server-generated ids are `oa_`/`sa_` + a 32-char
48/// encoding, so this leaves generous headroom for a legitimate value while
49/// bounding what an anonymous caller can push into the log.
50const MAX_CLIENT_ID_LEN: usize = 128;
51
52/// Truncate to at most [`MAX_CLIENT_ID_LEN`] **bytes**, cutting on a character
53/// boundary so the result is still valid UTF-8.
54///
55/// §22.3 residual 3: the previous `chars().take(N)` capped code points, not
56/// bytes, so a multibyte id could still store 4× the intended size. The cap
57/// exists to bound what an anonymous caller can push into an append-only log,
58/// and a bound expressed in the wrong unit is not the bound that was intended.
59fn truncate_bytes_on_char_boundary(s: &str) -> String {
60    if s.len() <= MAX_CLIENT_ID_LEN {
61        return s.to_owned();
62    }
63    let mut end = MAX_CLIENT_ID_LEN;
64    while end > 0 && !s.is_char_boundary(end) {
65        end -= 1;
66    }
67    s[..end].to_owned()
68}

74/// Query parameters for the authorization endpoint.
75#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, utoipa::IntoParams)]
76pub struct AuthorizeQuery {
77    /// Required unless `request_uri` is present (B5 / RFC 9126 §4).
78    pub response_type: Option<String>,
79    pub client_id: String,
80    /// Required unless `request_uri` is present.
81    pub redirect_uri: Option<String>,
82    pub scope: Option<String>,
83    pub state: Option<String>,
84    pub code_challenge: Option<String>,
85    pub code_challenge_method: Option<String>,
86    pub nonce: Option<String>,
87    /// B5 — a `urn:ietf:params:oauth:request_uri:` value obtained from
88    /// `/oauth2/par`. Mutually exclusive with the inline parameters above.
89    pub request_uri: Option<String>,
90    /// X7 G12 — RFC 9101 `request`, a request object by value. AXIAM does not
91    /// accept one; the field exists so the refusal can name the right error
92    /// code (`request_not_supported`) instead of the parameter being dropped
93    /// by serde and the request quietly succeeding without it.
94    pub request: Option<String>,
95    // X7.1 — the nine OIDC authentication-request parameters (OIDC Core
96    // §3.1.2.1, §5.2, §5.5). Declared so they can be *parsed*; whether any of
97    // them is acted on is the client's `authn_request_params` registration,
98    // and in this wave the answer is "none of them, for anybody".
99    //
100    // Adding them here is the one change in this file that alters what serde
101    // accepts. It cannot change what a request *does*: previously they were
102    // dropped as unknown parameters, and the golden-path tests (P1) assert
103    // that a standard client sending all nine still gets a byte-identical
104    // redirect, token response and ID token.
105    pub prompt: Option<String>,
106    pub max_age: Option<String>,
107    pub acr_values: Option<String>,
108    pub claims: Option<String>,
109    pub id_token_hint: Option<String>,
110    pub login_hint: Option<String>,
111    pub display: Option<String>,
112    pub ui_locales: Option<String>,
113    pub claims_locales: Option<String>,
114    /// RFC 9449 §10 — the JWK thumbprint of the key the client will prove
115    /// possession of at the token endpoint.
116    ///
117    /// Declared here as well as on the PAR body because §10 defines it as an
118    /// *authorization request* parameter, and Figure 25 shows it on a plain
119    /// `GET /authorize`. It is read only on the inline branch: beside a
120    /// `request_uri` the pushed copy wins, like every other parameter, so a
121    /// browser cannot re-pin somebody's pushed request to a key of its own.
122    pub dpop_jkt: Option<String>,
123    /// T21.3 / RFC 8707 §2 — the target service this authorization is for.
124    ///
125    /// Read only on the inline branch: beside a `request_uri` the pushed copy
126    /// wins, exactly as `state`, `nonce` and `dpop_jkt` do, so a browser
127    /// cannot re-address somebody's pushed request at a resource of its own.
128    ///
129    /// A single value. RFC 8707 §2 permits the parameter to repeat; AXIAM
130    /// issues a token for one target service, so a second value is
131    /// `invalid_target`. That refusal is answered before this struct is built
132    /// — see `duplicate_resource_response` below — because the deserializer
133    /// rejects a repeated key first.
134    pub resource: Option<String>,
135    /// W3 — which tenant this authorization request is for, read **only** when
136    /// the request carries no authenticated principal (plan §4.0).
137    ///
138    /// Every session, client and code in AXIAM is tenant-scoped, and until this
139    /// wave the tenant came exclusively from the caller's own access token. An
140    /// anonymous browser has no token, so the login hop cannot even look up the
141    /// client whose `browser_sso` field decides how to answer it — hence a
142    /// parameter, the same way `/oauth2/end_session` and `/oauth2/token` already
143    /// take one, and the same way the tenant-scoped discovery document is
144    /// fetched.
145    ///
146    /// **Ignored whenever a principal was resolved from a token**, which is
147    /// every request that works today: the token's tenant wins, unconditionally,
148    /// exactly as before. A request that omits it and has no principal is
149    /// answered with today's 401 — so a client registered today, which has never
150    /// sent it, cannot observe that it exists.
151    pub tenant_id: Option<Uuid>,
152    /// W3 — the login hop's loop guard (`axiam_login_hop`).
153    ///
154    /// Set by this server inside the `return_to` it builds, and read here to
155    /// recognise the return leg. See `axiam_oauth2::login_hop` for why its
156    /// presence bounds the hop at one redirect, and for why a browser that
157    /// strips it harms only itself.
158    #[serde(rename = "axiam_login_hop")]
159    pub login_hop: Option<String>,
160    /// W7 — the **consent** hop's loop guard (`axiam_consent_hop`).
161    ///
162    /// A second marker rather than a reuse of the one above, because the two
163    /// ceremonies answer different questions and a request can need both. See
164    /// `axiam_oauth2::login_hop::CONSENT_HOP_MARKER`.
165    #[serde(rename = "axiam_consent_hop")]
166    pub consent_hop: Option<String>,
167    /// The sign-in page's Cancel, coming back (`axiam_user_declined`).
168    ///
169    /// Read on the anonymous path only, and answered with `access_denied` to a
170    /// registered `redirect_uri`. See
171    /// `axiam_oauth2::login_hop::USER_DECLINED_MARKER` for why refusing is a
172    /// protocol outcome rather than an abandoned tab.
173    #[serde(rename = "axiam_user_declined")]
174    pub user_declined: Option<String>,
175    /// G-B (#817) — which federation provider the sign-in page should start
176    /// with: a provider's UUID, its `provider_slug`, or a branded provider
177    /// kind name (`google`, `github`, `facebook`, `apple`, `microsoft`; ASCII
178    /// case-insensitive) when exactly one provider of that kind is offered.
179    ///
180    /// `axiam_`-prefixed by the vendor-namespace rule `axiam_login_hop` states
181    /// (`axiam_oauth2::login_hop::LOGIN_HOP_MARKER`); there is no
182    /// `kc_idp_hint` alias (D-N3). Read on every lane, because it is not one
183    /// of the OIDC parameters `authn_request_params` governs and it changes
184    /// nothing that is issued. Resolved on the server against the request
185    /// tenant's effective, enabled provider list — the list
186    /// `GET /api/v1/auth/federation/providers` already shows — and only the
187    /// matched provider's id reaches the sign-in page (`idp=<uuid>`). A hint
188    /// that matches nothing, or matches more than one provider, is ignored:
189    /// the response is byte-identical to a request without one. Beside a
190    /// `request_uri` the pushed copy is used and this one is ignored. Moot when
191    /// the browser already holds an OP session and no sign-in is needed.
192    pub axiam_idp_hint: Option<String>,
193}

198    pub tenant_id: Uuid,
199}

212/// Read the authentication behind this request's session (X7.2, plan §4.3).
213///
214/// The evidence is snapshotted onto the authorization code, so it has to be
215/// read here, while the session still exists: refresh rotation replaces the
216/// row, and by the time the code is redeemed the session it was minted from
217/// may be a different row with a different id.
218///
219/// # It never fails the request
220///
221/// A session that cannot be read yields empty evidence and a `debug!` line,
222/// not an error. Two reasons, and both are invariant 4: an authorization
223/// request that works today must not start failing because of a column added
224/// this wave, and the principal on this path is not always a browser session
225/// at all (a bearer token minted for a service, a session already rotated
226/// away). Empty evidence is also the strict reading — a code with no
227/// `auth_time` can never present as fresh.
228async fn resolve_session_evidence<C: Connection + Clone>(
229    state: &AppState<C>,
230    tenant_id: Uuid,
231    session_id: Uuid,
232) -> SessionEvidence {
233    match state.session_repo.get_by_id(tenant_id, session_id).await {
234        Ok(session) => SessionEvidence {
235            auth_time: Some(session.authenticated_at),
236            amr: session.amr,
237        },
238        Err(e) => {
239            tracing::debug!(
240                error = %e,
241                %session_id,
242                "no session row behind this authorization request; the code is issued \
243                 without authentication evidence"
244            );
245            SessionEvidence::default()
246        }
247    }
248}
249
250/// G-5 (D-53 (1)): remember a step-up the honour lane is sending `user_id` to
251/// perform — the session they hold and the `acr` it achieved — for the return
252/// leg to consume. One record per `(tenant, user)`, the latest replacing the
253/// earlier, ten minutes.
254///
255/// Written only when some stream would carry `assurance-level-change` (a tenant
256/// with SSF off, or nobody subscribed, keeps nothing), and never fails the
257/// request: a record that could not be written costs one event, not a sign-in.
258async fn ssf_step_up_begin<C: Connection + Clone>(
259    state: &AppState<C>,
260    tenant_id: Uuid,
261    user_id: Uuid,
262    session_id: Uuid,
263    previous: axiam_oauth2::acr::Acr,
264) {
265    use axiam_core::models::ssf::{SsfEventType, SsfStepUp};
266    use axiam_core::repository::SsfStepUpRepository as _;
267
268    if !state
269        .ssf
270        .emitter
271        .carries(tenant_id, SsfEventType::AssuranceLevelChange)
272        .await
273    {
274        return;
275    }
276    let record = SsfStepUp {
277        tenant_id,
278        user_id,
279        previous_session_id: session_id,
280        previous_acr: previous.as_str().to_owned(),
281    };
282    if let Err(error) = state
283        .ssf
284        .step_up_repo
285        .put(&record, chrono::Utc::now())
286        .await
287    {
288        tracing::warn!(
289            target: "axiam::ssf",
290            %tenant_id,
291            %user_id,
292            %error,
293            "a step-up could not be remembered; its assurance-level-change will not be sent"
294        );
295    }
296}
297
298/// G-5 (D-53 (1)): the return leg of a login hop. Consume the user's step-up
299/// record (single use, whatever comes of it) and, when this request's session is
300/// a **new** one whose `acr` differs from the recorded one, tell SSF receivers
301/// the assurance level changed (`previous_level` the recorded class,
302/// `change_direction` from the published order, `initiating_entity: user`).
303///
304/// Nothing is told for: no record (never written, another user's, expired or
305/// already consumed), the same session coming back (nothing was stepped up, and
306/// the record is left for the real return leg), a request with no readable
307/// session (the new level is unknown), or an equal `acr`. Nothing travels in
308/// `return_to`, so there is nothing for a relying party to forge or replay; the
309/// marker query parameter only says a return leg is being made, the record it
310/// can consume is the caller's own, and the caller runs this only for a request
311/// the authorization service accepted (F4 W4 P23W4-02).
312///
313/// Never fails the request.
314async fn ssf_step_up_return_leg<C: Connection + Clone>(
315    state: &AppState<C>,
316    tenant_id: Uuid,
317    user_id: Uuid,
318    session_id: Uuid,
319    current: Option<axiam_oauth2::acr::Acr>,
320) {
321    use axiam_core::models::ssf::SsfEventType;
322    use axiam_core::repository::SsfStepUpRepository as _;
323    use axiam_oauth2::ssf::AssuranceLevel;
324
325    if !state
326        .ssf
327        .emitter
328        .carries(tenant_id, SsfEventType::AssuranceLevelChange)
329        .await
330    {
331        return;
332    }
333    // Taken only by a return leg in **another** session than the one the
334    // step-up was asked of (F4 W4 P23W4-02): the same session coming back has
335    // stepped nothing up, and must not spend the record the real return leg
336    // will need.
337    let record = match state
338        .ssf
339        .step_up_repo
340        .take(tenant_id, user_id, session_id, chrono::Utc::now())
341        .await
342    {
343        Ok(Some(record)) => record,
344        Ok(None) => return,
345        Err(error) => {
346            tracing::warn!(
347                target: "axiam::ssf",
348                %tenant_id,
349                %user_id,
350                %error,
351                "a step-up record could not be read; its assurance-level-change will not be sent"
352            );
353            return;
354        }
355    };
356    let (Some(current), Some(previous)) = (current, AssuranceLevel::from_urn(&record.previous_acr))
357    else {
358        return;
359    };
360    if record.previous_session_id == session_id {
361        // Unreachable through `take`, which leaves such a record in place; kept
362        // so the rule does not depend on the repository alone.
363        return;
364    }
365    state
366        .ssf
367        .emitter
368        .assurance_level_changed(
369            tenant_id,
370            user_id,
371            previous,
372            AssuranceLevel::from_acr(current),
373        )
374        .await;
375}
376
377/// Record the outcome of a `prompt=none` authorization request (W4, plan §4.2).
378///
379/// `prompt=none` is a **silent-authentication oracle**: a registered relying
380/// party learns, without any interaction, whether this browser is signed in.
381/// That is inherent to the parameter and it is accepted — it is bounded to
382/// clients registered in this tenant with exact `redirect_uri` matching, and
383/// nothing but one bit crosses — but "accepted" and "invisible" are different
384/// things. One audit row per outcome is what makes abuse of it something an
385/// operator can see and count rather than something they have to be told about
386/// by the party doing it.
387///
388/// Three actions are emitted: `oauth2.prompt_none.code`,
389/// `oauth2.prompt_none.login_required`, and `oauth2.prompt_none.refused` for
390/// every other refusal. The plan names a fourth,
391/// `oauth2.prompt_none.consent_required`, which is **not** written: W4 could
392/// not raise `consent_required` at all (it needs a consent-gated scope), and
393/// since W7 defined `address` and `phone` a silent request for them without a
394/// recorded consent *is* refused `consent_required` — but it is audited as
395/// `oauth2.prompt_none.refused`, the error code in the row's `error` metadata
396/// being what distinguishes it. A dedicated action would be a one-line match
397/// arm below, and has not been needed.
398///
399/// Never fails the request. An audit sink that is down costs a row, not a
400/// login (T-15-04).
401async fn audit_prompt_none<C: Connection + Clone>(
402    state: &AppState<C>,
403    http_req: &HttpRequest,
404    tenant_id: Uuid,
405    client_id: &str,
406    refusal: Option<&OAuth2Error>,
407) {
408    let (action, result) = match refusal {
409        None => ("oauth2.prompt_none.code", AuditOutcome::Success),
410        Some(e) => (
411            match e.error_code() {
412                "login_required" => "oauth2.prompt_none.login_required",
413                _ => "oauth2.prompt_none.refused",
414            },
415            AuditOutcome::Failure,
416        ),
417    };
418    if let Err(e) = state
419        .audit_repo
420        .append(CreateAuditLogEntry {
421            tenant_id,
422            actor_id: Uuid::nil(),
423            actor_type: ActorType::System,
424            action: action.into(),
425            resource_id: None,
426            outcome: result,
427            ip_address: peer_ip(http_req),
428            metadata: Some(serde_json::json!({
429                // The `client_id` is the point of the row: the oracle is
430                // per-client, so counting it per client is what makes a
431                // relying party polling every few seconds visible.
432                "client_id": truncate_bytes_on_char_boundary(client_id),
433                "error": refusal.map(OAuth2Error::error_code),
434            })),
435        })
436        .await
437    {
438        tracing::error!(
439            error = %e,
440            %tenant_id,
441            "could not record a prompt=none outcome; the authorization request is unaffected"
442        );
443    }
444}
445
446/// Decode an `id_token_hint` presented at the authorization endpoint (W4,
447/// plan §4.2).
448///
449/// One decoder, shared with `/oauth2/end_session`
450/// ([`axiam_oauth2::logout::decode_id_token_hint`]): the signature is checked,
451/// the expiry deliberately is not — an `id_token_hint` has routinely expired,
452/// which is exactly why an RP is sending one rather than a live token — and
453/// `aud` is read out rather than validated against, because who the token was
454/// for is part of what the caller wants to learn. A second decoder here would
455/// be a second opinion about what a hint proves.
456///
457/// Returns `None` when no hint arrived and when one arrived and did not
458/// verify. The caller distinguishes them by
459/// [`AuthnRequestParams::id_token_hint`], and treats the second as a hint that
460/// names somebody else rather than as an absent one — dropping an unverifiable
461/// hint would let any string turn a `prompt=none` refusal into a code.
462///
463/// Run for every request carrying the parameter, not only for the honour lane.
464/// It costs one Ed25519 verification of a value the request already carried,
465/// it cannot change any response on the `ignore` lane (nothing reads the
466/// result there), and making it conditional would put the lane decision in a
467/// second place.
468fn decode_authorize_id_token_hint<C: Connection + Clone>(
469    state: &AppState<C>,
470    params: &AuthnRequestParams,
471) -> Option<axiam_oauth2::logout::IdTokenHint> {
472    let raw = params.id_token_hint.as_deref()?;
473    axiam_oauth2::logout::decode_id_token_hint(raw, &state.auth_config.jwt_public_key_pem)
474}
475
476/// Classify a request object on an authorization request (X7 G12, plan §4.10).
477///
478/// AXIAM accepts neither form. `request` is RFC 9101's request object by
479/// value; a `request_uri` that is not a `urn:ietf:params:oauth:request_uri:`
480/// handle is a request object by reference, which would have the authorization
481/// server fetch an attacker-chosen URL — an SSRF primitive that PAR (RFC 9126
482/// §1) made unnecessary and that FAPI 2.0 does not ask for.
483///
484/// `request` is checked first: when both arrive, the by-value object is the
485/// one the server would have had to *read*, so it is the one the refusal
486/// should name.
487///
488/// Returns `None` for a request carrying no request object at all, including
489/// one whose `request_uri` is a genuine PAR handle — that path is untouched.
490fn classify_request_object(
491    request: Option<&str>,
492    request_uri: Option<&str>,
493) -> Option<RequestObject> {
494    // A blank value is a client library filling in a template, not an object.
495    // Refusing it would break a request that works today and carries nothing.
496    fn present(v: Option<&str>) -> Option<&str> {
497        v.map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
498    }
499
500    if present(request).is_some() {
501        return Some(RequestObject::ByValue);
502    }
503    match present(request_uri) {
504        Some(uri) if !uri.starts_with(axiam_oauth2::par::REQUEST_URI_PREFIX) => {
505            Some(RequestObject::ByReference)
506        }
507        _ => None,
508    }
509}
510
511/// What [`resolve_authorize_principal`] answers with when there is no principal
512/// to return: a finished response, boxed.
513///
514/// Boxed because `HttpResponse` is a large value (well over
515/// `clippy::result_large_err`'s 128-byte threshold), and a `Result` whose error
516/// variant is large makes *every* caller pay that size on the success path too.
517/// Do not unbox it back for tidiness — CI's clippy is the thing that notices.
518type AuthorizeRefusal = Box<HttpResponse>;
519
520/// The principal an authorization request is acting for (W3, plan §4.0).
521///
522/// Three fields rather than an [`AuthenticatedUser`] because the two ways of
523/// arriving here produce different amounts of context and only these three are
524/// used past this point: an access token carries an organization scope and an
525/// active-tenant header, an `axiam_op_session` cookie carries a session row.
526/// Modelling the union would invite a later reader to ask a question of the
527/// cookie path that only the token path can answer.
528struct AuthorizePrincipal {
529    tenant_id: Uuid,
530    user_id: Uuid,
531    session_id: Uuid,
532}
533
534/// Is this `return_to` a path on an origin this deployment owns?
535///
536/// The second of the two server-side checks the plan asks for. The first is
537/// [`axiam_oauth2::login_hop::validate_return_to`], which is syntactic and
538/// demands the exact authorize path; this one *resolves* the candidate against
539/// the issuer and hands the result to
540/// [`require_deployment_spa_origin`](crate::handlers::federation_login::require_deployment_spa_origin)
541/// — the rule that already decides where a federation SSO handoff code may be
542/// sent, rather than a second rule that could come to disagree with it.
543///
544/// While the syntactic check demands `/oauth2/authorize?…` this is redundant,
545/// and it is kept for the case that stops being true: `Url::join` is what turns
546/// `//evil.example` into a different host and `/a/../../b` into `/b`, so it is
547/// an independent opinion on the two attacks that are about *resolution*
548/// rather than about spelling.
549/// The authorization endpoint path **this** request arrived at (T21.6).
550///
551/// `/oauth2/authorize` for every request on the deployment-wide endpoint, which
552/// is every request on a deployment with `AXIAM__AUTH__TENANT_ISSUER_PATHS`
553/// unset — so a login or consent hop builds and validates exactly the value it
554/// built and validated before tenant paths existed.
555///
556/// `/t/{tenant_id}/oauth2/authorize` on a per-tenant path, built from the
557/// *parsed* tenant id the scope middleware recorded rather than from the path
558/// string the request carried, so the result cannot be anything the UUID parse
559/// did not already accept.
560fn authorize_path_of(req: &HttpRequest) -> String {
561    match crate::middleware::tenant_path::binding_of(req) {
562        None => axiam_oauth2::login_hop::AUTHORIZE_PATH.to_owned(),
563        Some(binding) => axiam_oauth2::login_hop::tenant_authorize_path(binding.tenant_id),
564    }
565}
566
567/// The authorization request's query string as the client sent it — what a
568/// `return_to` must carry back (T23.1.8).
569///
570/// On the deployment-wide path that is `req.query_string()`. On a per-tenant
571/// path it is the query **before** `TenantPathScope` appended `tenant_id`:
572/// the browser presents the `return_to` to the tenant scope again, and that
573/// scope refuses a `tenant_id` parameter outright, so a `return_to` built from
574/// the rewritten query made every return leg on a tenant path an
575/// `invalid_request`. The path already names the tenant, which is the whole
576/// point of the path form.
577fn client_query_of(req: &HttpRequest) -> String {
578    match crate::middleware::tenant_path::binding_of(req) {
579        None => req.query_string().to_owned(),
580        Some(binding) => binding.client_query,
581    }
582}
583
584pub(crate) fn return_to_is_on_this_deployment<C: Connection + Clone>(
585    state: &AppState<C>,
586    return_to: &str,
587    authorize_path: &str,
588) -> bool {
589    // The deployment ROOT, never the tenant issuer: this resolves a path
590    // against an origin, and a tenant issuer carries a path of its own that
591    // `Url::join` would then treat as a base directory. `authorize_path` is
592    // where the tenant, if any, is expressed — and it is supplied by the caller
593    // from the request it is answering (T21.6), never read out of the candidate.
594    let Ok(base) = url::Url::parse(state.auth_config.root_issuer()) else {
595        return false;
596    };
597    let Ok(resolved) = base.join(return_to) else {
598        return false;
599    };
600    if resolved.path() != authorize_path {
601        return false;
602    }
603    crate::handlers::federation_login::require_deployment_spa_origin(state, resolved.as_str())
604        .is_ok()
605}
606
607/// Resolve who this authorization request is acting for (W3, plan §4.0).
608///
609/// Two ways in, in this order:
610///
611/// 1. **An access token** — the `axiam_access` cookie or a `Bearer`/`DPoP`
612///    header, via [`AuthenticatedUser`]. Unchanged, and it wins whenever it
613///    succeeds. Every request that works today takes this path and cannot tell
614///    that the other exists.
615/// 2. **The `axiam_op_session` cookie**, and only for a client registered
616///    `browser_sso`. This is the path that makes AXIAM usable as an OP for a
617///    third-party relying party at all: `axiam_access` is `SameSite=Strict`, so
618///    the cross-site top-level navigation an RP redirect *is* never carries it,
619///    and until this wave a signed-in user arrived here anonymous.
620///
621/// # Order of operations, and why the client is loaded first
622///
623/// The client must be read before the cookie is, because the client's
624/// registration is what decides whether the cookie is consulted. That is the
625/// reordering plan §4.0 describes, and it is not a new trust decision — the
626/// authorize service already loads this row before any redirectable error, so
627/// the lookup moves one step earlier rather than appearing.
628///
629/// # Every anonymous refusal is today's refusal
630///
631/// No tenant, no such client, a client that did not opt in, a repository that
632/// failed: all four return the **same** 401 the extractor would have produced,
633/// byte for byte, by returning the extractor's own error object. That is
634/// invariant 4 and it is also what keeps the endpoint from becoming an oracle:
635/// an anonymous caller cannot learn from the response whether a `client_id`
636/// exists in a tenant, only whether it opted into the hop.
637///
638/// # Errors
639///
640/// Returns a complete [`HttpResponse`] — the 401, the 302 into the login page,
641/// or the loop guard's terminal `login_required` — because each of the three
642/// is finished at the point it is decided and none is expressible as an
643/// [`OAuth2Error`] the caller could usefully re-render. Boxed: see
644/// [`AuthorizeRefusal`].
645async fn resolve_authorize_principal<C: Connection + Clone>(
646    state: &AppState<C>,
647    http_req: &HttpRequest,
648    q: &AuthorizeQuery,
649    maybe_user: MaybeAuthenticatedUser,
650) -> Result<AuthorizePrincipal, AuthorizeRefusal> {
651    use actix_web::ResponseError;
652
653    let auth_error = match maybe_user.into_result() {
654        Ok(user) => {
655            // Path 1. `tenant_id` on the query is ignored here, deliberately:
656            // the tenant a token was minted for is the tenant it acts in, and
657            // letting a query parameter move that would be a tenant-crossing
658            // primitive handed to whoever holds the browser.
659            return Ok(AuthorizePrincipal {
660                tenant_id: user.tenant_id,
661                user_id: user.user_id,
662                session_id: user.session_id,
663            });
664        }
665        Err(e) => e,
666    };
667
668    // ---- Path 2: anonymous ------------------------------------------------
669    let Some(tenant_id) = q.tenant_id else {
670        return Err(Box::new(auth_error.error_response()));
671    };
672    let Ok(client) = state
673        .oauth2_client_repo
674        .get_by_client_id(tenant_id, &q.client_id)
675        .await
676    else {
677        return Err(Box::new(auth_error.error_response()));
678    };
679    if !client.browser_sso {
680        // **T0.1 / T0.5 / M7's I4 twin.** Every client registered today lands
681        // here, including one whose browser happens to be carrying an
682        // `axiam_op_session` cookie: the cookie is not read, because reading it
683        // is what `browser_sso` opts into.
684        return Err(Box::new(auth_error.error_response()));
685    }
686
687    // The person said no.
688    //
689    // Answered here, before the session cookie is consulted, because the answer
690    // does not depend on it: somebody who cancels the sign-in page has by
691    // definition not signed in, and asking whether they might have a session
692    // from some earlier request would let a stale cookie turn a refusal into a
693    // grant. The two conditions above still apply first — an unknown client and
694    // a client that never opted into the hop are answered exactly as they are
695    // today, because this endpoint must not become an oracle for either.
696    //
697    // Delivered by redirect on the same terms as every other authorization
698    // error (RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1): only to a `redirect_uri` this client
699    // registered — as `any_redirect_uri_matches` reads "registered", so a
700    // desktop client's ephemeral loopback port is one — with the request's own
701    // `state`. An unregistered or absent one is answered directly, because a
702    // refusal is still not a licence to send a browser somewhere the client
703    // never named.
704    if axiam_oauth2::login_hop::user_declined(q.user_declined.as_deref()) {
705        let refusal = OAuth2Error::AccessDenied(
706            "the end user declined the authorization request at the sign-in page".into(),
707        );
708
709        // Where to answer, and with whose `state` — and for a pushed request
710        // neither comes from the query string.
711        //
712        // RFC 9126 §1: the pushed copy is the request. A FAPI client sends
713        // `client_id` and `request_uri` to the authorization endpoint and may
714        // send nothing else, so reading `state` off the query answered a
715        // refusal with no `state` at all — which the suite reports as
716        // `CheckStateInAuthorizationResponse: State was passed in request, but
717        // is missing from response`, and which a relying party would be right
718        // to discard as an unsolicited response (RFC 6749 §10.12).
719        //
720        // Consuming the `request_uri` here is correct rather than merely
721        // convenient: it is single-use, and this request has just been answered
722        // terminally. Leaving it spendable would let the same pushed request be
723        // presented again after its user said no.
724        let pushed = match q.request_uri.as_deref() {
725            Some(uri) => state
726                .oauth2
727                .par_service
728                .consume(tenant_id, &q.client_id, uri)
729                .await
730                .ok(),
731            None => None,
732        };
733        let (redirect_uri, echo_state) = match pushed.as_ref() {
734            Some(p) => (Some(p.redirect_uri.as_str()), p.state.as_deref()),
735            None => (q.redirect_uri.as_deref(), q.state.as_deref()),
736        };
737
738        return Err(Box::new(match redirect_uri {
739            Some(uri) if any_redirect_uri_matches(&client.redirect_uris, uri) => {
740                build_error_redirect(uri, &refusal, echo_state, &issuer_config(http_req, state))
741            }
742            _ => authorize_error_response(http_req, &refusal),
743        }));
744    }
745
746    // The cookie, and whether it still names a live session in this tenant.
747    let presented = http_req.cookie(crate::middleware::csrf::COOKIE_OP_SESSION);
748    let resolved = match presented.as_ref() {
749        Some(cookie) => {
750            let digest = axiam_auth::token::hash_browser_session_token(cookie.value());
751            match state
752                .session_repo
753                .get_by_browser_token_hash(tenant_id, &digest)
754                .await
755            {
756                Ok(found) => found,
757                Err(e) => {
758                    // A read failure is not a principal. Logged without the
759                    // cookie value or its digest — the first is a credential and
760                    // the second is enough to recognise one.
761                    tracing::warn!(
762                        error = %e,
763                        %tenant_id,
764                        "could not resolve the OP browser session; treating the \
765                         request as anonymous"
766                    );
767                    None
768                }
769            }
770        }
771        None => None,
772    };
773
774    // T23.1.3 — the account, re-read. A session row says who authenticated and
775    // when; it says nothing about whether that account may still act, and the
776    // cookie naming it lives as long as the session does. An administrator who
777    // locks or deactivates a user does not revoke their sessions — the refresh
778    // path re-reads the account instead (`check_user_status`) — so this is
779    // another place a session becomes a principal, and it applies the
780    // existing-credential rule (`axiam_auth::service::account_may_act`, shared
781    // with the OAuth2 grants and federated sign-in since F4 P23W1-01/-04):
782    // suspended statuses are refused, `PendingVerification` is not (P23W1-03 —
783    // every federated account holds it for life, so refusing it past the grace
784    // period ended browser sign-on for all of them a day after provisioning).
785    // A session whose account fails it is treated exactly as a session that no
786    // longer exists: the cookie is stale, the browser is asked to sign in, and
787    // the sign-in page is where the account's state is explained.
788    let resolved = match resolved {
789        Some(session) => match state.user_repo.get_by_id(tenant_id, session.user_id).await {
790            Ok(user) => match state.auth_service.check_session_holder(&user) {
791                Ok(()) => Some(session),
792                Err(reason) => {
793                    tracing::info!(
794                        %tenant_id,
795                        session_id = %session.id,
796                        reason = %reason,
797                        "an OP browser session names an account that may no longer \
798                         sign in; not resolving a principal from it"
799                    );
800                    None
801                }
802            },
803            Err(e) => {
804                tracing::warn!(
805                    error = %e,
806                    %tenant_id,
807                    session_id = %session.id,
808                    "could not read the account behind an OP browser session; \
809                     treating the request as anonymous"
810                );
811                None
812            }
813        },
814        None => None,
815    };
816
817    if let Some(session) = resolved {
818        return Ok(AuthorizePrincipal {
819            tenant_id,
820            user_id: session.user_id,
821            session_id: session.id,
822        });
823    }
824
825    // ---- No principal: refuse silently, hop, or stop ---------------------
826
827    // #524 (P23W2-03) — do not send a user to sign in for a request that
828    // `AuthorizeService::authorize` will refuse whoever signs in.
829    //
830    // A client registered `require_par` (every `fapi2` client is) may not send
831    // its parameters through the browser, and whether it did is decidable from
832    // the registration and the query alone: no principal and no pushed
833    // request. Until #524 an anonymous browser was sent through the login hop
834    // first and the return leg was refused, so a person typed a password for
835    // nothing and a FAPI reviewer saw a sign-in page before the error page
836    // (`REVIEW-JUDGEMENTS.md`, open point 6).
837    //
838    // First among the no-principal arms rather than beside the `response_type`
839    // pre-check, because the `prompt=none` arm before that one would redirect
840    // on the query's `redirect_uri` — the channel this setting forbids — and the
841    // service checks PAR before `response_type` too. Not before the session
842    // lookup: a browser that holds a session still reaches the service, whose
843    // step 1b is the gate the return-leg tests pin. Same wording, same
844    // non-redirecting answer as the handler's `ParRequired` arm.
845    if client.require_par && q.request_uri.is_none() {
846        return Err(Box::new(authorize_error_response(
847            http_req,
848            &OAuth2Error::ParRequired(axiam_oauth2::authorize::PAR_REQUIRED_DESCRIPTION.into()),
849        )));
850    }
851
852    // W4 (plan §4.2, T1.1/T1.7). `prompt=none` says: answer without showing
853    // the end user anything. There is nothing to answer with — no session
854    // resolved — so the answer is `login_required`, **redirected to the
855    // relying party** rather than to a sign-in page it forbade.
856    //
857    // Read from the query string only. A pushed request's `prompt` cannot be
858    // seen here at all (the handle is consumed later, inside the handler), so
859    // a PAR client's `prompt=none` takes the hop first and is refused on the
860    // return leg instead, by the marker rule in `axiam_oauth2::honour` — the
861    // interaction is never converted into a code either way.
862    //
863    // Honour lane only, and that is what keeps T0.1 exact: a `browser_sso`
864    // client registered `ignore` — which is every client that exists — sending
865    // `prompt=none` gets today's answer, byte for byte.
866    //
867    // W5 (plan §4.5/§4.6) reads the same bundle for the cosmetic four, which is
868    // why it is parsed once here with every field rather than only `prompt`.
869    // Query string only, as before: a pushed request's parameters cannot be
870    // seen at this point (the handle is consumed inside the handler, after a
871    // principal exists), so a PAR client's presentation reaches the page on the
872    // *interaction* arm below instead. Both arms build their URL through the
873    // one builder, so the two carriers cannot come to disagree about what a
874    // `/login` URL looks like.
875    let honour = client.authn_request_params.is_honour();
876    let authn_params = honour.then(|| {
877        AuthnRequestParams::parse(&RawAuthnParams {
878            prompt: q.prompt.as_deref(),
879            // #835 (D-N28) — read for the hop's re-authentication marker only
880            // (`demands_fresh_authentication`); nothing on this arm decides on
881            // it, and a malformed one is the return leg's to refuse.
882            max_age: q.max_age.as_deref(),
883            login_hint: q.login_hint.as_deref(),
884            display: q.display.as_deref(),
885            ui_locales: q.ui_locales.as_deref(),
886            // `claims_locales` is deliberately not read: it selects the
887            // language of *claim values*, which AXIAM does not localise, and
888            // it must not reach the page's language. See
889            // `axiam_oauth2::login_hop::Cosmetic::from_params`.
890            ..Default::default()
891        })
892    });
893    if let Some(params) = authn_params.as_ref()
894        && params
895            .prompt
896            .contains(&axiam_oauth2::authn_params::Prompt::None)
897    {
898        let refusal = OAuth2Error::LoginRequired(
899            "no end user is authenticated at this authorization server, and prompt=none \
900                 forbids asking them to sign in"
901                .into(),
902        );
903        audit_prompt_none(state, http_req, tenant_id, &q.client_id, Some(&refusal)).await;
904        // RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1: an error is redirected only to a
905        // `redirect_uri` this client registered, and "registered" means what
906        // `any_redirect_uri_matches` means by it — string equality, plus RFC
907        // 8252 §7.3's port allowance for a registered `http` loopback URI.
908        // The same comparison `AuthorizeService::authorize` makes, which is
909        // the point: until T21.8 this arm compared exactly while authorize
910        // used the matcher, so a desktop client that registered
911        // `http://127.0.0.1/callback` and listened on an ephemeral port got
912        // its successes redirected and its refusals rendered as a page it
913        // could not read. An unregistered or absent URI is still answered
914        // directly.
915        return Err(Box::new(match q.redirect_uri.as_deref() {
916            Some(uri) if any_redirect_uri_matches(&client.redirect_uris, uri) => {
917                build_error_redirect(
918                    uri,
919                    &refusal,
920                    q.state.as_deref(),
921                    &issuer_config(http_req, state),
922                )
923            }
924            _ => authorize_error_response(http_req, &refusal),
925        }));
926    }
927
928    // The loop guard. A request that already carries the marker is one this
929    // server sent to the login page and got back; sending it there a second
930    // time is the non-terminating case, so it is answered instead. See
931    // `axiam_oauth2::login_hop` for the full argument.
932    if axiam_oauth2::login_hop::is_return_leg(q.login_hop.as_deref()) {
933        tracing::warn!(
934            %tenant_id,
935            client_id = %q.client_id,
936            "an authorization request returned from the login hop still \
937             carrying no OP session; refusing to redirect again"
938        );
939        return Err(Box::new(authorize_error_response(
940            http_req,
941            &OAuth2Error::LoginRequired(
942                "the sign-in did not establish a session for this tenant at this \
943                 origin; sign in again from the relying party, and check that the \
944                 browser accepts the axiam_op_session cookie"
945                    .into(),
946            ),
947        )));
948    }
949
950    // Two refusals stand here, and they are mutually exclusive by
951    // construction: the `response_type` check below runs only when there is NO
952    // `request_uri`, and the `peek` after it only when there is one. They can
953    // never both fire, so their order carries no meaning — the cheaper,
954    // datastore-free one is simply first.
955
956    // Do not send a user to sign in for a request that cannot succeed.
957    //
958    // `response_type` is the one parameter whose validity is decidable here,
959    // with no principal and without touching the pushed request: RFC 6749
960    // §3.1.1 makes it REQUIRED, AXIAM supports exactly `code`, and neither
961    // fact depends on who is signing in. Everything else the authorization
962    // endpoint refuses needs either the client's registration read against a
963    // resolved tenant or the pushed parameters, and the `request_uri` those
964    // live behind is single-use — consumed once, in the handler, after a
965    // principal exists. That constraint is why this check is narrow rather
966    // than a second copy of `AuthorizeService::authorize`'s validation.
967    //
968    // Skipped entirely when a `request_uri` is present: with PAR the real
969    // `response_type` is the pushed one, `ParService::push` already refused
970    // anything but `code` at push time, and the query string's copy is not
971    // authoritative — RFC 9126 §4 says the pushed parameters are used and the
972    // query's are ignored, so rejecting on the query's absence here would
973    // refuse a perfectly good pushed request.
974    //
975    // The refusal follows RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1 exactly as the `prompt=none` arm
976    // above does, and for the same reason: an error goes back to a
977    // `redirect_uri` only when the client registered it, as
978    // `any_redirect_uri_matches` decides "registered", because an unvalidated
979    // one is an open redirect. Anything else is answered in place, which is
980    // also the error page a certification reviewer is asked to see for
981    // `oidcc-response-type-missing` — unchanged, because that module registers
982    // an `https` URI and the matcher is string equality for those.
983    if q.request_uri.is_none() && q.response_type.as_deref() != Some("code") {
984        let refusal = match q.response_type.as_deref() {
985            None | Some("") => {
986                OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest("response_type is required (RFC 6749 §3.1.1)".into())
987            }
988            Some(_) => OAuth2Error::UnsupportedResponseType,
989        };
990        return Err(Box::new(match q.redirect_uri.as_deref() {
991            Some(uri) if any_redirect_uri_matches(&client.redirect_uris, uri) => {
992                build_error_redirect(
993                    uri,
994                    &refusal,
995                    q.state.as_deref(),
996                    &issuer_config(http_req, state),
997                )
998            }
999            _ => authorize_error_response(http_req, &refusal),
1000        }));
1001    }
1002
1003    // The handle, before a person is asked to sign in for it.
1004    //
1005    // `/oauth2/authorize` cannot consume a `request_uri` here: it is single-use
1006    // and is spent inside the handler, after a principal exists — which is what
1007    // the `prompt=none` comment above means by "a pushed request's parameters
1008    // cannot be seen at this point". The consequence was that a browser
1009    // presenting an already-used, expired or wrong-client handle was sent to
1010    // `/login` without the handle being looked at at all, the user typed a
1011    // password, and the request was refused on the return leg. The OIDF module
1012    // `fapi2-security-profile-final-par-attempt-reuse-request_uri` reports that
1013    // as a screenshot of a sign-in page against a condition asking for an error
1014    // page about an invalid `request_uri`.
1015    //
1016    // `peek` is a READ. It does not consume, does not authorize, and carries
1017    // nothing forward: the authoritative single-use decision stays in
1018    // `ParService::consume`, in the handler, in one statement (see
1019    // `par.rs`'s §2 rule 1). A handle that passes here may still lose the race
1020    // to a concurrent authorize request, and must.
1021    //
1022    // It therefore refuses exactly what was already dead and nothing that is
1023    // merely unfinished — which is the property
1024    // `par-ensure-reused-request-uri-prior-to-auth-completion-succeeds`
1025    // depends on: a `request_uri` re-presented BEFORE the first authorization
1026    // completed is still unconsumed and unexpired, so the login page is shown,
1027    // as that module requires.
1028    //
1029    // Only a genuine PAR handle is asked about. A `request` object by value, or
1030    // a `request_uri` that is not a PAR handle, is a different refusal with its
1031    // own OIDC Core §3.1.2.6 code, owned by `classify_request_object` in the
1032    // handler; turning either into `invalid_request` here would lose the code a
1033    // conformance suite matches on.
1034    let mut pushed_idp_hint = None;
1035    let mut pushed_fresh_authentication = false;
1036    if classify_request_object(q.request.as_deref(), q.request_uri.as_deref()).is_none()
1037        && let Some(request_uri) = q.request_uri.as_deref()
1038        && let Err(refusal) = state
1039            .oauth2
1040            .par_service
1041            .peek(tenant_id, &q.client_id, request_uri)
1042            .await
1043            // G-B — what `peek` hands back: the pushed provider hint, and
1044            // (#835) whether the pushed request demands a fresh
1045            // authentication, for the sign-in page this browser is about to
1046            // be sent to.
1047            .map(|peeked| {
1048                pushed_idp_hint = peeked.axiam_idp_hint;
1049                pushed_fresh_authentication = peeked.fresh_authentication;
1050            })
1051    {
1052        // RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1, answered exactly as the `prompt=none` arm above
1053        // does: redirected only to a `redirect_uri` this client registered,
1054        // compared by `any_redirect_uri_matches`, and answered directly
1055        // otherwise. The pushed copy's `redirect_uri` is not available and
1056        // must not be — resolving it is the thing that just failed.
1057        let reported = request_uri_error_for_client(&refusal);
1058        let reported = reported.as_ref().unwrap_or(&refusal);
1059        return Err(Box::new(match q.redirect_uri.as_deref() {
1060            Some(uri) if any_redirect_uri_matches(&client.redirect_uris, uri) => {
1061                build_error_redirect(
1062                    uri,
1063                    reported,
1064                    q.state.as_deref(),
1065                    &issuer_config(http_req, state),
1066                )
1067            }
1068            // Rendered for the person, with the wording `ParService` produced.
1069            // The OIDC code above is what a *relying party* can act on; a page
1070            // saying `invalid_request_uri` to somebody who did not send the
1071            // parameter tells them nothing they can use.
1072            _ => authorize_error_response(http_req, &refusal),
1073        }));
1074    }
1075
1076    // T21.6 — the browser comes back to the endpoint it left. On the
1077    // deployment-wide path that is `/oauth2/authorize`, exactly as before; on a
1078    // per-tenant path it is `/t/{tenant_id}/oauth2/authorize`, which is what
1079    // makes the authorization response the return leg produces carry the tenant
1080    // issuer in its RFC 9207 `iss` — the issuer the client discovered.
1081    let authorize_path = authorize_path_of(http_req);
1082    let Some(return_to) =
1083        axiam_oauth2::login_hop::build_return_to_at(&authorize_path, &client_query_of(http_req))
1084    else {
1085        // Nothing safe to come back to. Answer as if the request had been
1086        // anonymous with no `browser_sso` at all rather than send a browser
1087        // somewhere on a value that did not validate.
1088        return Err(Box::new(auth_error.error_response()));
1089    };
1090    if !return_to_is_on_this_deployment(state, &return_to, &authorize_path) {
1091        return Err(Box::new(auth_error.error_response()));
1092    }
1093
1094    // A cookie that was presented and did not resolve is stale: this browser
1095    // believes it is signed in and is not. `reauth` tells the SPA to say so and
1096    // to authenticate afresh instead of trusting what it already holds, and the
1097    // dead cookie is removed on the way out so the next attempt starts clean.
1098    let stale = presented.is_some();
1099    // W5. The presentation the relying party asked for, on the honour lane and
1100    // nowhere else: `authn_params` is `None` for every client registered
1101    // `ignore`, so `Cosmetic::NONE` is what they get and their `/login` URL is
1102    // byte-identical to the one W3 built. Invariant 4, as a type rather than as
1103    // a branch somebody has to remember to write.
1104    //
1105    // W7 — the tenant default, which W5 deferred for want of a place to
1106    // configure it. Read only when a bundle arrived at all: a client on the
1107    // `ignore` lane gets `Cosmetic::NONE` and no settings read, so its
1108    // `/login` URL is byte-identical to W3's and costs the same.
1109    let tenant_default = match authn_params.as_ref() {
1110        Some(_) => tenant_default_locale(state, tenant_id).await,
1111        None => None,
1112    };
1113    let mut cosmetic = authn_params
1114        .as_ref()
1115        .map_or(axiam_oauth2::login_hop::Cosmetic::NONE, |params| {
1116            axiam_oauth2::login_hop::Cosmetic::from_params(params, tenant_default)
1117        });
1118    // G-B (#817) — the provider to start with, on **every** lane: the hint is
1119    // AXIAM's own parameter, not one of the nine `authn_request_params`
1120    // governs, and it changes nothing that is issued. A client that sends no
1121    // hint and registered no default does no lookup and gets the URL it got
1122    // before. Beside a `request_uri` the pushed copy is the hint (RFC 9126
1123    // §4), and the query string's copy is ignored.
1124    let raw_hint = if q.request_uri.is_some() {
1125        pushed_idp_hint.as_deref()
1126    } else {
1127        q.axiam_idp_hint.as_deref()
1128    };
1129    cosmetic.idp = resolve_login_idp(
1130        state,
1131        tenant_id,
1132        bounded_idp_hint(raw_hint),
1133        client.default_idp,
1134    )
1135    .await;
1136    // #835 (D-N28, F4-W3-12) — the hop says when the request in force demands
1137    // a fresh authentication (`prompt` naming `login`, or `max_age=0`), so the
1138    // sign-in page never starts a provider by itself for it: neither value is
1139    // forwarded upstream, and a provider that still holds a session would
1140    // answer with nobody present. The page cannot read this itself — under
1141    // PAR the values are in the pushed request — so the server, which sees
1142    // both carriers, marks it. The pushed copy beside a `request_uri`, the
1143    // query otherwise (RFC 9126 §4), each through the honour lane's parse.
1144    //
1145    // Honour lane only, mirroring the server's own decision: off it,
1146    // `prompt=login` and `max_age` are dropped (`ignore`) or refused on the
1147    // return leg (`fapi2`), nothing is asserted about the authentication's
1148    // freshness, and the `/login` URL stays the one W3 built (invariant 4).
1149    cosmetic.fresh_authentication = honour
1150        && if q.request_uri.is_some() {
1151            pushed_fresh_authentication
1152        } else {
1153            authn_params
1154                .as_ref()
1155                .is_some_and(AuthnRequestParams::demands_fresh_authentication)
1156        };
1157    let location =
1158        axiam_oauth2::login_hop::build_login_redirect_for(&return_to, stale, None, &cosmetic);
1159
1160    let mut builder = HttpResponse::Found();
1161    builder
1162        .append_header((actix_web::http::header::LOCATION, location))
1163        // The URL carries the whole authorization request; it must not be
1164        // cached, and it must not travel onward as a referrer.
1165        .append_header((actix_web::http::header::CACHE_CONTROL, "no-store"))
1166        .append_header(("Referrer-Policy", "no-referrer"));
1167    if stale {
1168        // T23.1.8: the copy this request could have carried — the tenant one on
1169        // a tenant path, the bare one otherwise — and only that copy. The
1170        // others may hold a later sign-in into another tenant that is still
1171        // good where it is scoped.
1172        builder.cookie(crate::middleware::csrf::clear_presented_op_session_cookie(
1173            crate::middleware::tenant_path::binding_of(http_req).map(|b| b.tenant_id),
1174        ));
1175    }
1176    Err(Box::new(builder.finish()))
1177}
1178
1179/// G-B (#817) — an `axiam_idp_hint` as `authn_params` bounds it: blank, longer
1180/// than `MAX_HINT_LEN` or repaired from non-UTF-8 input is no hint at all.
1181///
1182/// One parse for both carriers and both arms, so the bound cannot differ
1183/// between the first leg and an interaction hop.
1184fn bounded_idp_hint(raw: Option<&str>) -> Option<String> {
1185    AuthnRequestParams::parse(&RawAuthnParams {
1186        axiam_idp_hint: raw,
1187        ..Default::default()
1188    })
1189    .idp_hint
1190}
1191
1192/// G-B (#817) — which provider the sign-in page starts with, if any.
1193///
1194/// The hint, when it names exactly one provider; otherwise the client's
1195/// `default_idp`, when that still names one; otherwise none. "Names" is
1196/// decided against **one** list: the request tenant's effective, enabled
1197/// providers — its own plus the organization's inheritable, unshadowed ones —
1198/// from the resolver `list_providers_public` uses, so the answer can only ever
1199/// be a provider that endpoint already shows this tenant's sign-in page.
1200///
1201/// # Why a lookup is permissible here when `login_hint` forbids one (T-493)
1202///
1203/// `login_hint`'s rule is that nothing looks it up, because a lookup on an
1204/// account identifier is an enumeration oracle. A provider is not an account,
1205/// and the list consulted is public for the tenant: whatever a hint could
1206/// distinguish — exists, enabled, inherited, another tenant's — can already be
1207/// read from that listing, or is invisible because the hint matches only
1208/// within it. So the response does not need to stay equal *by construction*;
1209/// it is equal by the one rule below, which the authorize tests pin byte for
1210/// byte:
1211///
1212/// * a hint that matches nothing (unknown, disabled, not inheritable,
1213///   shadowed, another tenant's, or a URL) is treated as **no hint**, so the
1214///   client default then applies exactly as it would have without the hint;
1215/// * a hint that matches more than one provider (a slug shared by two kinds,
1216///   or a kind name two providers share) is ambiguous and treated the same
1217///   way;
1218/// * a default that no longer matches (its provider was disabled or deleted
1219///   after registration) is ignored, never an error — the registration was
1220///   valid when written, and the user still gets a sign-in page;
1221/// * a datastore failure is ignored too: a sign-in page without a preselected
1222///   provider is always a correct answer.
1223///
1224/// What a hint may be — a UUID, a slug or a branded kind name, in that order —
1225/// is [`match_idp_hint`]'s rule. Only the matched config's own `id` is
1226/// returned — a type, never the hint.
1227///
1228/// No hint and no default: no read at all, so a request that uses neither
1229/// costs what it cost before G-B.
1230async fn resolve_login_idp<C: Connection + Clone>(
1231    state: &AppState<C>,
1232    tenant_id: Uuid,
1233    hint: Option<String>,
1234    default_idp: Option<Uuid>,
1235) -> Option<Uuid> {
1236    if hint.is_none() && default_idp.is_none() {
1237        return None;
1238    }
1239    let organization_id = match state.tenant_repo.get_by_id(tenant_id).await {
1240        Ok(tenant) => tenant.organization_id,
1241        Err(e) => {
1242            tracing::warn!(error = %e, %tenant_id, "could not read the tenant to resolve a sign-in provider; showing the chooser");
1243            return None;
1244        }
1245    };
1246    let effective = match crate::handlers::federation_login::effective_providers(
1247        state,
1248        crate::handlers::federation_login::Workspace {
1249            organization_id,
1250            tenant_id,
1251        },
1252    )
1253    .await
1254    {
1255        Ok(list) => list,
1256        Err(e) => {
1257            tracing::warn!(error = ?e, %tenant_id, "could not list the tenant's providers to resolve a sign-in provider; showing the chooser");
1258            return None;
1259        }
1260    };
1261    if let Some(hint) = hint.as_deref() {
1262        let candidates: Vec<IdpHintCandidate<'_>> = effective
1263            .iter()
1264            .map(|p| IdpHintCandidate {
1265                id: p.config.id,
1266                slug: p.config.provider_slug.as_deref(),
1267                kind: p.config.provider_kind,
1268            })
1269            .collect();
1270        if let Some(id) = match_idp_hint(&candidates, hint) {
1271            return Some(id);
1272        }
1273    }
1274    default_idp.filter(|id| effective.iter().any(|p| p.config.id == *id))
1275}
1276
1277/// G-B (#817) — what [`match_idp_hint`] needs of one effective provider: the
1278/// three things a hint may name it by, all of which the public providers
1279/// listing already shows (`id`, `provider_kind`) or the operator chose to make
1280/// addressable (`provider_slug`).
1281#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
1282struct IdpHintCandidate<'a> {
1283    id: Uuid,
1284    slug: Option<&'a str>,
1285    kind: axiam_core::models::federation::ProviderKind,
1286}
1287
1288/// G-B (#817, D-N24) — the one provider `hint` names among `candidates` (the
1289/// request tenant's effective, enabled providers), or `None`.
1290///
1291/// Three forms, tried in order, and the first form that matches **anything**
1292/// decides — a later form is never consulted to break an earlier form's tie:
1293///
1294/// 1. **An explicit identifier: the UUID, or the `provider_slug`.** A UUID is
1295///    compared as a UUID, so any spelling `Uuid::parse_str` accepts names the
1296///    same provider; a slug is compared exactly (slugs are lower-case
1297///    `[a-z0-9-]` by validation). The two are one tier, unchanged from W3.1:
1298///    a lower-case hyphenated UUID is also a well-formed slug, so a hint that
1299///    is one provider's id *and* another's slug is ambiguous and ignored
1300///    rather than settled by a precedence nobody chose on purpose.
1301/// 2. **A branded kind name** — `google`, `github`, `facebook`, `apple`,
1302///    `microsoft`: the `provider_kind` value the public listing carries for
1303///    each button, compared ASCII case-insensitively. Those names are
1304///    lower-case canonical on the wire (`ProviderKind::as_str`), so `Google`
1305///    has exactly one meaning and refusing it would only punish a
1306///    capitalisation; nothing else is folded (no `-`/`_` swap, no inner
1307///    whitespace — surrounding whitespace was already trimmed, for every
1308///    form, by the parameter parse).
1309///
1310/// **The slug wins over the kind, including case-folded.** A slug is the
1311/// operator's explicit choice of a word for one provider; a kind name is a
1312/// category. If an operator slugged a generic provider `google`, the hint
1313/// `google` names that provider (tier 1), and so must every spelling of it:
1314/// a hint that equals any candidate's slug ignoring ASCII case is never read
1315/// as a kind name. Otherwise `Google` would silently reach the branded Google
1316/// while `google` reaches the operator's provider — two providers for one
1317/// word. Such a hint (`Google` beside a slug `google`) matches nothing and is
1318/// ignored. Only effective providers' slugs count: consulting a disabled
1319/// provider's slug would let the hint's outcome reveal a provider the public
1320/// listing does not show (T-493).
1321///
1322/// **Ambiguity is no answer**, in either tier: a kind two effective providers
1323/// share (two tenant Google configs — nothing stops a tenant holding two) is
1324/// ignored, exactly as a shared slug is. An organization's Google and the
1325/// tenant's own Google never both count: the tenant's shadows the
1326/// organization's in `effective_providers`, so the list holds one of them.
1327///
1328/// **The generic kinds are not accepted** (`generic_oidc`, `generic_oauth2`,
1329/// `generic_saml`). They name a protocol family, not a provider: a client's
1330/// `generic_oidc` would mean whichever OIDC provider a tenant happens to hold
1331/// alone today, and the day the tenant swaps Okta for Auth0 the same hint
1332/// would start a sign-in at a provider the client never named. A branded name
1333/// means the same provider in every tenant. A generic provider is named by
1334/// its slug or its id.
1335fn match_idp_hint(candidates: &[IdpHintCandidate<'_>], hint: &str) -> Option<Uuid> {
1336    fn only(mut ids: impl Iterator<Item = Uuid>) -> Option<Uuid> {
1337        match (ids.next(), ids.next()) {
1338            (Some(one), None) => Some(one),
1339            _ => None,
1340        }
1341    }
1342
1343    let as_id = Uuid::parse_str(hint).ok();
1344    let explicit = |c: &&IdpHintCandidate<'_>| Some(c.id) == as_id || c.slug == Some(hint);
1345    if candidates.iter().any(|c| explicit(&c)) {
1346        return only(candidates.iter().filter(explicit).map(|c| c.id));
1347    }
1348
1349    if candidates
1350        .iter()
1351        .any(|c| c.slug.is_some_and(|slug| slug.eq_ignore_ascii_case(hint)))
1352    {
1353        return None;
1354    }
1355    let kind = axiam_core::models::federation::ProviderKind::ALL
1356        .iter()
1357        .copied()
1358        .find(|k| !k.uses_slug() && k.as_str().eq_ignore_ascii_case(hint))?;
1359    only(candidates.iter().filter(|c| c.kind == kind).map(|c| c.id))
1360}
1361
1362/// W7 — the tenant's fallback UI language, for the last step of W5's
1363/// `ui_locales` chain (plan §4.6's deferral, now picked up).
1364///
1365/// W5 shipped `select_ui_locale(requested, tenant_default)` and unit-tested it
1366/// through all three steps, then passed `None` at both call sites because there
1367/// was nowhere for an operator to set the value. There is now:
1368/// `TenantSettingsOverride::default_locale`, on the settings surface every
1369/// other per-tenant control lives on. This is the one argument those two call
1370/// sites were waiting for.
1371///
1372/// Three ways to get `None`, and all three land on the deployment default
1373/// (`en`), which is what every deployment did before this wave:
1374///
1375/// * the tenant has no preference;
1376/// * the settings could not be read (a login page is not worth failing over a
1377///   language);
1378/// * the stored tag is one this build does not ship. `Locale::from_tag` is an
1379///   **exact** match rather than an RFC 4647 lookup, deliberately: a stored
1380///   `fr-CA` means "somebody wrote something this binary does not ship", and
1381///   answering it with French would be a guess presented as a setting.
1382async fn tenant_default_locale<C: Connection + Clone>(
1383    state: &AppState<C>,
1384    tenant_id: Uuid,
1385) -> Option<axiam_oauth2::locale::Locale> {
1386    let tenant = state.tenant_repo.get_by_id(tenant_id).await.ok()?;
1387    let settings = axiam_core::repository::SettingsRepository::get_effective_settings(
1388        &state.settings_repo,
1389        tenant.organization_id,
1390        tenant_id,
1391    )
1392    .await
1393    .ok()?;
1394    let tag = settings.oidc.default_locale?;
1395    let locale = axiam_oauth2::locale::Locale::from_tag(&tag);
1396    if locale.is_none() {
1397        tracing::warn!(
1398            %tenant_id,
1399            configured = %tag,
1400            "this tenant's default_locale is not a locale this build ships; the sign-in page \
1401             falls back to the deployment default"
1402        );
1403    }
1404    locale
1405}
1406
1407/// Whether the tenant's effective settings have the GDPR-sensitive scopes
1408/// (`address`, `phone`) on. A settings read that fails is "off", logged.
1409async fn sensitive_scopes_enabled<C: Connection + Clone>(
1410    state: &AppState<C>,
1411    tenant_id: Uuid,
1412) -> bool {
1413    // The organization the settings baseline belongs to. Resolved from the
1414    // tenant rather than taken from the caller's principal, the same way
1415    // `axiam_auth::lockout` resolves it: the authorization endpoint's
1416    // principal type carries no `org_id`, and adding one to it would put a
1417    // field on the hot path for a branch almost no request takes.
1418    match state.tenant_repo.get_by_id(tenant_id).await {
1419        Ok(tenant) => match axiam_core::repository::SettingsRepository::get_effective_settings(
1420            &state.settings_repo,
1421            tenant.organization_id,
1422            tenant_id,
1423        )
1424        .await
1425        {
1426            Ok(settings) => settings.oidc.sensitive_scopes_enabled,
1427            Err(e) => {
1428                tracing::error!(
1429                    error = %e,
1430                    %tenant_id,
1431                    "could not read the tenant's effective settings while deciding whether the \
1432                     address/phone scopes are enabled; treating them as disabled"
1433                );
1434                false
1435            }
1436        },
1437        Err(e) => {
1438            tracing::error!(
1439                error = %e,
1440                %tenant_id,
1441                "could not resolve the organization behind this tenant; treating the \
1442                 address/phone scopes as disabled"
1443            );
1444            false
1445        }
1446    }
1447}
1448
1449/// W7 / X7 G8 — resolve what this authorization request's GDPR-sensitive
1450/// scopes have earned, so `AuthorizeService` can decide without a repository.
1451///
1452/// Returns `(state, switch_is_off)`. Two values rather than one because they
1453/// come from two reads and `axiam_oauth2::sensitive::decide` checks them
1454/// against each other; collapsing them here would be collapsing the check.
1455///
1456/// # Costs nothing for a request that asks for nothing
1457///
1458/// The first line answers "did this request name `address` or `phone`", which
1459/// is a scan of a short vector, and returns before either database read. Every
1460/// client registered today asks for neither — they could not, the scopes were
1461/// unregistrable — so this function adds one string comparison per scope to
1462/// every authorization request in every existing deployment and nothing else.
1463/// That is invariant 4 measured rather than asserted.
1464///
1465/// # A read that fails is not a release
1466///
1467/// Both reads fail closed and in different directions, which is deliberate:
1468/// a settings read that fails reports the switch as **off** (refuse), and a
1469/// consent read that fails reports the consent as **missing** (ask again).
1470/// Neither answer can release data, and the second is recoverable by the end
1471/// user rather than terminal.
1472async fn resolve_sensitive_scopes<C: Connection + Clone>(
1473    state: &AppState<C>,
1474    tenant_id: Uuid,
1475    user_id: Uuid,
1476    client_id: &str,
1477    scope: Option<&str>,
1478) -> (axiam_oauth2::sensitive::Requested, bool) {
1479    let scopes: Vec<String> = scope
1480        .unwrap_or("")
1481        .split_whitespace()
1482        .map(String::from)
1483        .collect();
1484    let wanted = axiam_oauth2::sensitive::requested(&scopes);
1485    if wanted.is_empty() {
1486        return (axiam_oauth2::sensitive::Requested::None, false);
1487    }
1488
1489    let enabled = sensitive_scopes_enabled(state, tenant_id).await;
1490    if !enabled {
1491        return (axiam_oauth2::sensitive::Requested::Disabled, true);
1492    }
1493
1494    let consent_type = axiam_oauth2::sensitive::consent_type(client_id);
1495    let version = axiam_oauth2::sensitive::consent_version(&wanted);
1496    let consented = match axiam_core::repository::ConsentRepository::list_by_user(
1497        &state.gdpr.consent_repo,
1498        tenant_id,
1499        user_id,
1500    )
1501    .await
1502    {
1503        Ok(records) => records
1504            .iter()
1505            .any(|c| c.consent_type == consent_type && c.version == version),
1506        Err(e) => {
1507            tracing::error!(
1508                error = %e,
1509                %tenant_id,
1510                "could not read the end user's consent records; treating the release as \
1511                 unconsented, which asks again rather than releasing"
1512            );
1513            false
1514        }
1515    };
1516
1517    (
1518        if consented {
1519            axiam_oauth2::sensitive::Requested::Consented
1520        } else {
1521            axiam_oauth2::sensitive::Requested::ConsentMissing
1522        },
1523        false,
1524    )
1525}
1526
1527/// T21.4 / D4 — resolve whether this authorization needs the external-client
1528/// consent hop.
1529///
1530/// Returns `NotApplicable` for a client an administrator created, which is
1531/// every client in most deployments and is the whole of I1 for this gate: the
1532/// function learns the client's `managed_by` and returns before touching the
1533/// consent repository.
1534///
1535/// # The cost, measured rather than assumed
1536///
1537/// Learning `managed_by` is an indexed read by `client_id` — a read
1538/// `AuthorizeService` repeats for its own validation. It is not avoidable at
1539/// this boundary: the service owns the client lookup and this handler owns
1540/// the consent repository, and the decision needs both. Passing a consent
1541/// repository into the service would put a repository into a crate whose
1542/// whole design is that it decides and does not fetch (see
1543/// `crate::sensitive`'s module docs), and returning early from the service so
1544/// the handler could resolve and re-call would run every validation twice.
1545///
1546/// The read was first shipped uncached, on the argument that a
1547/// browser-interactive endpoint would not feel one more round trip. The
1548/// 2026-09-18 benchmark A/B refuted that: `oauth2_authorize` lost ~19% of its
1549/// throughput to it. So the answer now comes from
1550/// [`crate::client_managed_by_cache`] after the first read — safe because
1551/// `managed_by` can never change for a `(tenant_id, client_id)`; the module
1552/// docs give the argument. Only the one field is cached; the service's own
1553/// read of the client, which validates everything that *can* change, is
1554/// untouched.
1555///
1556/// The **consent** read, which is the more expensive of the two, still
1557/// happens only for a client that is actually external.
1558///
1559/// # Both reads fail closed, in the recoverable direction
1560///
1561/// A client read that fails reports `NotApplicable` — there is no client, so
1562/// the authorization is about to fail anyway inside the service, with the
1563/// error that names the real problem rather than a consent question about a
1564/// client that does not exist. A consent read that fails reports the consent
1565/// as **missing**, which asks again rather than proceeding: the same direction
1566/// `resolve_sensitive_scopes` takes, for the same reason.
1567async fn resolve_external_consent<C: Connection + Clone>(
1568    state: &AppState<C>,
1569    tenant_id: Uuid,
1570    user_id: Uuid,
1571    client_id: &str,
1572    scope: Option<&str>,
1573) -> axiam_oauth2::external_consent::Requested {
1574    use axiam_oauth2::external_consent::Requested;
1575
1576    let managed_by = match state.client_managed_by_cache.get(tenant_id, client_id) {
1577        Some(managed_by) => managed_by,
1578        None => {
1579            let Ok(client) = axiam_core::repository::OAuth2ClientRepository::get_by_client_id(
1580                &state.oauth2_client_repo,
1581                tenant_id,
1582                client_id,
1583            )
1584            .await
1585            else {
1586                // Not cached: a missing client is never remembered.
1587                return Requested::NotApplicable;
1588            };
1589            state
1590                .client_managed_by_cache
1591                .insert(tenant_id, client_id, client.managed_by);
1592            client.managed_by
1593        }
1594    };
1595    if !axiam_oauth2::external_consent::applies_to(managed_by) {
1596        return Requested::NotApplicable;
1597    }
1598
1599    let scopes: Vec<String> = scope
1600        .unwrap_or("")
1601        .split_whitespace()
1602        .map(String::from)
1603        .collect();
1604    let consent_type = axiam_oauth2::external_consent::consent_type(client_id);
1605    let version = axiam_oauth2::external_consent::version(&scopes);
1606
1607    match axiam_core::repository::ConsentRepository::list_by_user(
1608        &state.gdpr.consent_repo,
1609        tenant_id,
1610        user_id,
1611    )
1612    .await
1613    {
1614        Ok(records)
1615            if records
1616                .iter()
1617                .any(|c| c.consent_type == consent_type && c.version == version) =>
1618        {
1619            Requested::Consented
1620        }
1621        Ok(_) => Requested::ConsentMissing,
1622        Err(e) => {
1623            tracing::error!(
1624                error = %e,
1625                %tenant_id,
1626                "could not read the end user's consent records for an externally registered \
1627                 client; treating the client as unconsented, which asks again rather than \
1628                 letting it act as the user"
1629            );
1630            Requested::ConsentMissing
1631        }
1632    }
1633}
1634
1635/// `GET /oauth2/authorize` -- OAuth2 authorization endpoint.
1636///
1637/// A request carrying an access token authorizes as its subject. A request
1638/// carrying none is answered with a 401 JSON body — unless the client it names
1639/// is registered `browser_sso`, in which case it is either recognised by its
1640/// `axiam_op_session` cookie or redirected to the sign-in page and brought back
1641/// (W3, plan §4.0). See [`resolve_authorize_principal`].
1642///
1643/// On success, redirects to `redirect_uri?code=...&state=...`.
1644/// On error, redirects with `?error=...&error_description=...&state=...`.
1645#[utoipa::path(
1646    get,
1647    path = "/oauth2/authorize",
1648    tag = "oauth2",
1649    params(AuthorizeQuery),
1650    responses(
1651        (status = 302, description = "Redirect with authorization code, or to \
1652                                      the sign-in page for a browser_sso client"),
1653        (status = 400, description = "OAuth2 error", body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
1654        (status = 401, description = "No authenticated principal, and the \
1655                                      client did not opt into the login hop"),
1656        (status = 429, description = "Rate limit exceeded \
1657                                      (`AXIAM__RATE_LIMIT__END_SESSION_PER_MIN`, bucket \
1658                                      `oauth2_authorize`, one allowance shared by this \
1659                                      route and its `/t/{tenant_id}` mount)"),
1660    ),
1661    security(("bearer" = []))
1662)]
