3133    attempted_client_id: Option<&str>,
3134    grant_type: &str,
3135    peer_ip: Option<String>,
3136    forwarded_ip_untrusted: Option<String>,
3137) {
3138    // Control 1 — attribute the row to the request's tenant only when that
3139    // tenant demonstrably exists; otherwise fall back to the system partition.
3140    // `attributed_tenant` is nil in both the unknown and the indeterminate
3141    // case, so a caller can never place a row in a real tenant's log by naming
3142    // one.
3143    let (attributed_tenant, tenant_note) = match tenant_repo.get_by_id(tenant_id).await {
3144        Ok(_) => (tenant_id, None),
3145        Err(AxiamError::NotFound { .. }) => {
3146            tracing::warn!(
3147                claimed_tenant_id = %tenant_id,
3148                %grant_type,
3149                "oauth2: client-auth-failure audit routed to the system partition — unknown tenant (SEC-087)"
3150            );
3151            (Uuid::nil(), Some("unknown_tenant"))
3152        }
3153        Err(e) => {
3154            // A DB fault must not turn a 401 into a 500 (T-15-04), and must not
3155            // cost the telemetry either (§22.3 residual 2). Record it as
3156            // unattributed rather than dropping it.
3157            tracing::error!(
3158                error = %e,
3159                claimed_tenant_id = %tenant_id,
3160                "oauth2: tenant lookup failed for client-auth-failure audit; \
3161                 routing to the system partition"
3162            );
3163            (Uuid::nil(), Some("tenant_lookup_failed"))
3164        }
3165    };
3166
3167    // Control 2 — bound the one remaining caller-controlled string, by BYTES,
3168    // truncating on a character boundary so the stored value stays valid UTF-8.
3169    let attempted_client_id: Option<String> =
3170        attempted_client_id.map(truncate_bytes_on_char_boundary);
3171
3172    if let Err(e) = audit_repo
3173        .append(CreateAuditLogEntry {
3174            tenant_id: attributed_tenant,
3175            actor_id: Uuid::nil(),
3176            actor_type: ActorType::System,
3177            action: "oauth2.client_auth_failed".into(),
3178            resource_id: None,
3179            outcome: AuditOutcome::Failure,
3180            // Control 3 — the unforgeable half.
3181            ip_address: peer_ip,
3182            metadata: Some(serde_json::json!({
3183                "client_id": attempted_client_id,
3184                "grant_type": grant_type,
3185                // Named so nobody downstream mistakes it for verified input.
3186                "forwarded_for_untrusted": forwarded_ip_untrusted,
3187                // Present only on a system-partition row: the tenant the caller
3188                // named, and why it could not be attributed. Also caller-supplied.
3189                "claimed_tenant_id_untrusted": tenant_note.map(|_| tenant_id.to_string()),
3190                "unattributed_reason": tenant_note,
3191            })),
3192        })
3193        .await
3194    {
3195        // Never propagated: a token-endpoint 401 must not become a 500 because
3196        // the audit sink is unavailable (T-15-04).
3197        tracing::error!(
3198            error = %e,
3199            tenant_id = %attributed_tenant,
3200            %grant_type,
3201            "oauth2: failed to write oauth2.client_auth_failed audit log"
3202        );
3203    }
3204}
3205
3206/// `POST /oauth2/revoke` -- Token revocation endpoint (RFC 7009).
3207///
3208/// Accepts form-encoded body. Always returns 200 per the spec — invalid
3209/// tokens are silently ignored.
3210#[utoipa::path(
3211    post,
3212    path = "/oauth2/revoke",
3213    tag = "oauth2",
3214    params(TenantQuery),
3215    request_body(
3216        content_type = "application/x-www-form-urlencoded",
3217        content = RevokeRequest,
3218    ),
3219    responses(
3220        (status = 200, description = "Token revoked (or was already invalid)"),
3221        (status = 400, description = "Malformed request, no client_id in the body, the \
3222                                      Authorization header or the assertion, or a body \
3223                                      client_id that disagrees with the header",
3224         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
3225        (status = 401, description = "Client authentication failed",
3226         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
3227    ),
3228)]
3229pub async fn revoke<C: Connection + Clone>(
3230    http_req: HttpRequest,
3231    tenant_query: web::Query<TenantQuery>,
3232    form: web::Form<RevokeRequest>,
3233    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3234) -> HttpResponse {
3235    // W8 / RFC 6749 §5.2 — the challenge is decided from the request,
3236    // before the handler runs, and applied to whatever 401 it produces.
3237    let challenge = client_auth_challenge(&http_req);
3238    with_client_auth_challenge(
3239        revoke_inner(http_req, tenant_query, form, state).await,
3240        challenge,
3241    )
3242}
3243
3244/// See [`token_inner`] for why this split exists.
3245async fn revoke_inner<C: Connection + Clone>(
3246    http_req: HttpRequest,
3247    tenant_query: web::Query<TenantQuery>,
3248    form: web::Form<RevokeRequest>,
3249    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3250) -> HttpResponse {
3251    let tenant_id = tenant_query.into_inner().tenant_id;
3252
3253    // SEC-093: carry the connection's client certificate through, so a client
3254    // registered for `tls_client_auth` can actually revoke. The assertion
3255    // parameters are folded in by `revoke_token` from the form.
3256    let ctx = match token_request_context(&http_req) {
3257        Ok(ctx) => ctx,
3258        Err(response) => return *response,
3259    };
3260
3261    match state
3262        .oauth2
3263        .token_service
3264        .revoke_token(tenant_id, form.into_inner(), &ctx)
3265        .await
3266    {
3267        Ok(()) => HttpResponse::Ok().finish(),
3268        Err(e) => build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
3269    }
3270}
3271
3272/// `POST /oauth2/introspect` -- Token introspection endpoint (RFC 7662).
3273///
3274/// Accepts form-encoded body. Returns an `IntrospectionResponse` with
3275/// `active: true/false` and optional metadata.
3276#[utoipa::path(
3277    post,
3278    path = "/oauth2/introspect",
3279    tag = "oauth2",
3280    params(TenantQuery),
3281    request_body(
3282        content_type = "application/x-www-form-urlencoded",
3283        content = IntrospectRequest,
3284    ),
3285    responses(
3286        (status = 200, description = "Token introspection result",
3287         body = IntrospectionResponse),
3288        (status = 400, description = "Malformed request, no client_id in the body, the \
3289                                      Authorization header or the assertion, or a body \
3290                                      client_id that disagrees with the header",
3291         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
3292        (status = 401, description = "Client authentication failed",
3293         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
3294    ),
3295)]
3296pub async fn introspect<C: Connection + Clone>(
3297    http_req: HttpRequest,
3298    tenant_query: web::Query<TenantQuery>,
3299    form: web::Form<IntrospectRequest>,
3300    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3301) -> HttpResponse {
3302    // W8 / RFC 6749 §5.2 — the challenge is decided from the request,
3303    // before the handler runs, and applied to whatever 401 it produces.
3304    let challenge = client_auth_challenge(&http_req);
3305    with_client_auth_challenge(
3306        introspect_inner(http_req, tenant_query, form, state).await,
3307        challenge,
3308    )
3309}
3310
3311/// See [`token_inner`] for why this split exists.
3312async fn introspect_inner<C: Connection + Clone>(
3313    http_req: HttpRequest,
3314    tenant_query: web::Query<TenantQuery>,
3315    form: web::Form<IntrospectRequest>,
3316    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3317) -> HttpResponse {
3318    let tenant_id = tenant_query.into_inner().tenant_id;
3319
3320    // SEC-093 — as for `revoke`.
3321    let ctx = match token_request_context(&http_req) {
3322        Ok(ctx) => ctx,
3323        Err(response) => return *response,
3324    };
3325
3326    match state
3327        .oauth2
3328        .token_service
3329        .introspect_token(tenant_id, form.into_inner(), &ctx)
3330        .await
3331    {
3332        Ok(resp) => HttpResponse::Ok()
3333            .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
3334            .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"))
3335            .json(resp),
3336        Err(e) => build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
3337    }
3338}
3339
3340// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
3341// OIDC endpoints
3342// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
3343
3344/// `GET /.well-known/openid-configuration` -- OIDC Discovery document.
3345///
3346/// Also served, byte-for-byte, at `/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server`
3347/// (RFC 8414 §3) — `server.rs` routes both paths to this same function (T21.1).
3348/// MCP clients probe the RFC 8414 path first, and several client libraries
3349/// never implement the OIDC path as a fallback. The OpenAPI document lists the
3350/// alias as its own entry (`openapi.rs::add_oauth_authorization_server_alias`)
3351/// so the two stay documented without a second hand-written annotation that
3352/// could drift from this one.
3353///
3354/// Returns the OpenID Provider metadata per OpenID Connect Discovery 1.0.
3355/// The issuer URL is taken from `AuthConfig::oauth2_issuer_url` when set,
3356/// falling back to `AuthConfig::jwt_issuer` otherwise.
3357///
3358/// RFC 8705 §5 `mtls_endpoint_aliases` is included when — and only when —
3359/// `AuthConfig::oauth2_mtls_base_url` names a separate mutual-TLS host.
3360/// Query string for [`discovery`] (W7).
3361#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
3362pub struct DiscoveryQuery {
3363    /// Which tenant the document should describe (X7 G8, plan §6).
3364    ///
3365    /// Optional, and ignored by everything except the two sensitive-scope
3366    /// rows: every other field in the document is a property of the
3367    /// deployment. A caller that omits it — which is every caller written
3368    /// before W7, and the conformance suite — receives exactly the document
3369    /// W6 served.
3370    ///
3371    /// Named the same way `/oauth2/authorize`, `/oauth2/end_session` and
3372    /// `/oauth2/token` name a tenant, rather than by a path prefix or a
3373    /// header, because a `.well-known` path is fixed by specification and a
3374    /// query parameter is the only place left to put it.
3375    pub tenant_id: Option<Uuid>,
3376}
3377
3378#[utoipa::path(
3379    get,
3380    path = "/.well-known/openid-configuration",
3381    params(("tenant_id" = Option<Uuid>, Query,
3382            description = "Tenant whose OIDC capabilities the document should describe. \
3383                           Omit for the deployment-wide document.")),
3384    tag = "oidc",
3385    responses(
3386        (status = 200, description = "OpenID Connect Discovery document",
3387         body = OidcDiscoveryDocument),
3388    ),
3389)]
3390pub async fn discovery<C: Connection + Clone>(
3391    query: web::Query<DiscoveryQuery>,
3392    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3393) -> HttpResponse {
3394    discovery_document(query.tenant_id, None, &state).await
3395}
3396
3397/// Authorization-server metadata for one tenant, at the RFC 8414 §3.1
3398/// path-insertion form of the issuer `{root}/t/{tenant_id}`.
3399//
3400// The rest of the rationale is deliberately NOT a doc comment: utoipa lifts a
3401// doc block verbatim into the OpenAPI `description`, and eleven SDK repos
3402// vendor `sdks/openapi.json` byte-for-byte. Same reasoning as the F3 note on
3403// `jwks` and the one on `MtlsEndpointAliases`.
3404//
3405// T21.6. Mounted only where `AXIAM__AUTH__TENANT_ISSUER_PATHS` is set. The
3406// document names `{root}/t/{tenant_id}` as its `issuer` and every endpoint
3407// under that same prefix, with no `tenant_id` query anywhere — which is what
3408// makes the issuer one RFC 8414 §2 permits, and therefore one an MCP server
3409// can put in the `authorization_servers` of its RFC 9728 metadata.
3410#[utoipa::path(
3411    get,
3412    path = "/.well-known/oauth-authorization-server/t/{tenant_id}",
3413    params(("tenant_id" = Uuid, Path, description = "The tenant this issuer names.")),
3414    tag = "oidc",
3415    responses(
3416        (status = 200, description = "Authorization-server metadata for one tenant",
3417         body = OidcDiscoveryDocument),
3418        (status = 400, description = "The tenant segment is not a UUID",
3419         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
3420    ),
3421)]
3422pub async fn discovery_rfc8414_tenant_path<C: Connection + Clone>(
3423    req: HttpRequest,
3424    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3425) -> HttpResponse {
3426    tenant_path_discovery(&req, &state).await
3427}
3428
3429/// The same tenant discovery document, at the OIDC Discovery path with the
3430/// RFC 8414 §3.1 insertion applied.
3431//
3432// T21.6. See `discovery_rfc8414_tenant_path`; both delegate to
3433// `tenant_path_discovery`, so the two documents cannot differ.
3434#[utoipa::path(
3435    get,
3436    path = "/.well-known/openid-configuration/t/{tenant_id}",
3437    params(("tenant_id" = Uuid, Path, description = "The tenant this issuer names.")),
3438    tag = "oidc",
3439    responses(
3440        (status = 200, description = "OpenID Connect Discovery document for one tenant",
3441         body = OidcDiscoveryDocument),
3442        (status = 400, description = "The tenant segment is not a UUID",
3443         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
3444    ),
3445)]
3446pub async fn discovery_oidc_tenant_path<C: Connection + Clone>(
3447    req: HttpRequest,
3448    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3449) -> HttpResponse {
3450    tenant_path_discovery(&req, &state).await
3451}
3452
3453/// The same tenant discovery document, at the path OpenID Connect Discovery
3454/// 1.0 §4 constructs by appending `/.well-known/openid-configuration` to the
3455/// issuer.
3456//
3457// T21.6. All three forms exist because clients disagree about which to derive:
3458// RFC 8414 §3.1 inserts the well-known segment after the host, OIDC Discovery
3459// §4 appends it to the issuer. A client that picked either must find AXIAM,
3460// and the three are produced by one function so they cannot differ.
3461#[utoipa::path(
3462    get,
3463    path = "/t/{tenant_id}/.well-known/openid-configuration",
3464    params(("tenant_id" = Uuid, Path, description = "The tenant this issuer names.")),
3465    tag = "oidc",
3466    responses(
3467        (status = 200, description = "OpenID Connect Discovery document for one tenant",
3468         body = OidcDiscoveryDocument),
3469        (status = 400, description = "The tenant segment is not a UUID",
3470         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
3471    ),
3472)]
3473pub async fn discovery_oidc_tenant_appended<C: Connection + Clone>(
3474    req: HttpRequest,
3475    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3476) -> HttpResponse {
3477    tenant_path_discovery(&req, &state).await
3478}
3479
3480/// The body all three T21.6 discovery routes share.
3481///
3482/// Reads the tenant from the path — never from a query, which the tenant form
3483/// does not have — and refuses a segment that is not a UUID with
3484/// `invalid_request`, from the path alone and with no repository read. An
3485/// unknown-but-well-formed tenant is served the document, exactly as
3486/// `?tenant_id=<unknown>` is today: discovery is unauthenticated, and a `404`
3487/// here would be a tenant-enumeration oracle.
3488async fn tenant_path_discovery<C: Connection + Clone>(
3489    req: &HttpRequest,
3490    state: &web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3491) -> HttpResponse {
3492    let Some(tenant_id) = req
3493        .match_info()
3494        .get("tenant_id")
3495        .and_then(|raw| Uuid::parse_str(raw).ok())
3496    else {
3497        return HttpResponse::BadRequest().json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
3498            error: "invalid_request".into(),
3499            error_description: "the tenant segment of the path is not a UUID".into(),
3500        });
3501    };
3502    // `None` means this deployment does not serve tenant paths. Unreachable —
3503    // these routes are mounted only where it does — and answered rather than
3504    // unwrapped.
3505    let Some(issuer) = state.auth_config.tenant_issuer(tenant_id) else {
3506        return HttpResponse::NotFound().finish();
3507    };
3508    discovery_document(Some(tenant_id), Some(issuer), state).await
3509}
3510
3511/// Build and serve a discovery document.
3512///
3513/// `tenant_issuer` is `Some` only for the three T21.6 tenant-path routes. It
3514/// changes exactly two things and nothing else:
3515///
3516/// * the document's `issuer`, and with it every endpoint URL, which is derived
3517///   from it;
3518/// * whether the endpoints carry `?tenant_id=` — they do not under a tenant
3519///   issuer, because the tenant is already in the path, and an issuer with a
3520///   query is not an issuer RFC 8414 §2 allows.
3521///
3522/// Everything else — the capability members, the sensitive-scope rows, the
3523/// mTLS aliases, the error posture — is the same code answering the same way,
3524/// which is what the "the three forms agree" test asserts.
3525async fn discovery_document<C: Connection + Clone>(
3526    described_tenant: Option<Uuid>,
3527    tenant_issuer: Option<String>,
3528    state: &web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3529) -> HttpResponse {
3530    let auth_config = &state.auth_config;
3531    let issuer = tenant_issuer
3532        .as_deref()
3533        .unwrap_or_else(|| auth_config.effective_issuer());
3534    // Guard: effective_issuer must be a valid URL for a compliant
3535    // discovery document.  Startup validation should catch this, but
3536    // defend in depth at the endpoint level.
3537    if url::Url::parse(issuer).is_err() {
3538        return HttpResponse::InternalServerError().json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
3539            error: "server_error".into(),
3540            error_description: "OIDC issuer is not configured as a \
3541                valid URL"
3542                .into(),
3543        });
3544    }
3545    // A misconfigured mTLS base URL fails the request rather than dropping the
3546    // aliases. Serving the document without them would tell an mTLS client the
3547    // conventional endpoints are the ones to use — a silent downgrade to
3548    // exactly the topology RFC 8705 §5 exists to steer it away from — and it
3549    // would look, from the client's side, indistinguishable from a deployment
3550    // that has no mTLS host at all.
3551    // W7 / X7 G8 — whether this tenant may use the sensitive scopes at all.
3552    // `false` when no tenant was named, which is every call made before this
3553    // wave: the document such a caller receives is byte-identical to the one
3554    // W6 served. See `build_discovery_document_for` for why omitting is the
3555    // truthful answer rather than the cautious one.
3556    // The tenant this document describes: the one the caller named, else the
3557    // deployment's configured default (`AXIAM__AUTH__OAUTH2_DEFAULT_TENANT_ID`,
3558    // unset on a multi-tenant deployment). `None` reproduces the pre-existing
3559    // document exactly, endpoint for endpoint.
3560    //
3561    // ONE value for both halves of the document, deliberately: the endpoints it
3562    // publishes and the scopes it advertises must describe the same tenant. A
3563    // document offering `address` for tenant X while pointing its token
3564    // endpoint at tenant Y would be internally inconsistent in a way no client
3565    // could detect and every client would trust.
3566    let described_tenant = described_tenant.or_else(|| auth_config.default_tenant_id());
3567
3568    // T21.4 — the two per-tenant capability rows are resolved from **one**
3569    // settings read, for the reason `described_tenant` is one value: a
3570    // document that advertised `address` for tenant X and a registration
3571    // endpoint for tenant Y would be internally inconsistent in a way no
3572    // client could detect. Both default to `false` when no tenant was named,
3573    // which is every caller written before W7 and every conformance run — such
3574    // a caller receives a document byte-identical to the one served before
3575    // this task (I1).
3576    let capabilities = match described_tenant {
3577        None => axiam_oauth2::oidc::TenantCapabilities::default(),
3578        Some(tenant_id) => match state.tenant_repo.get_by_id(tenant_id).await {
3579            Ok(tenant) => axiam_core::repository::SettingsRepository::get_effective_settings(
3580                &state.settings_repo,
3581                tenant.organization_id,
3582                tenant_id,
3583            )
3584            .await
3585            .map(|s| axiam_oauth2::oidc::TenantCapabilities {
3586                sensitive_scopes_enabled: s.oidc.sensitive_scopes_enabled,
3587                dynamic_registration_enabled: s.oidc.dynamic_registration.is_enabled(),
3588                // T21.5 — the third row the `TenantCapabilities` struct was
3589                // built to hold, resolved from the same one settings read for
3590                // the same reason: one document, one tenant.
3591                cimd_enabled: s.oidc.cimd.enabled,
3592            })
3593            // A settings read that fails advertises less rather than more.
3594            // A relying party told a scope exists and then refused it has a
3595            // worse day than one that was never told. The same reasoning
3596            // covers the registration endpoint: an MCP client told it may
3597            // register and then refused `403` is worse off than one that was
3598            // never told.
3599            .unwrap_or_default(),
3600            // An unknown tenant is not an error here: discovery is public and
3601            // unauthenticated, and answering `404` for a tenant id would make
3602            // this endpoint a tenant-enumeration oracle. The deployment-wide
3603            // document is served instead, which is what a caller that named no
3604            // tenant gets.
3605            Err(_) => axiam_oauth2::oidc::TenantCapabilities::default(),
3606        },
3607    };
3608
3609    // RFC 8705 §5 — the alias host is re-based on the tenant path too, so an
3610    // mTLS client following an alias reaches the same tenant it discovered. It
3611    // must be: §5 tells such a client it MUST prefer the alias, so an alias
3612    // pointing at the deployment-wide endpoint would silently move it to
3613    // another tenant.
3614    let tenant_path_suffix = tenant_issuer
3615        .as_ref()
3616        .and(described_tenant)
3617        .map(|tenant| format!("{}{}", axiam_auth::config::TENANT_PATH_PREFIX, tenant));
3618    let mtls_base = match tenant_path_suffix {
3619        None => auth_config.mtls_base_url().map(str::to_owned),
3620        Some(ref suffix) => auth_config
3621            .mtls_base_url()
3622            .map(|base| format!("{base}{suffix}")),
3623    };
3624    let doc = match build_discovery_document_for(
3625        issuer,
3626        mtls_base.as_deref(),
3627        capabilities,
3628        // `None` under a tenant issuer: the endpoints are already tenant-scoped
3629        // by their path, and `?tenant_id=` on top would be a second selector
3630        // saying the same thing — which `middleware::tenant_path` refuses on
3631        // arrival anyway. `capabilities` above is still resolved from the real
3632        // `described_tenant`, so the document says what THAT tenant can do.
3633        if tenant_issuer.is_some() {
3634            None
3635        } else {
3636            described_tenant
3637        },
3638    ) {
3639        Ok(doc) => doc,
3640        Err(e) => {
3641            tracing::error!(
3642                error = %e,
3643                "AXIAM__AUTH__OAUTH2_MTLS_BASE_URL is set but unusable; refusing to serve a \
3644                 discovery document without its RFC 8705 §5 mtls_endpoint_aliases"
3645            );
3646            return HttpResponse::InternalServerError().json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
3647                error: "server_error".into(),
3648                error_description: "mTLS endpoint alias base URL is not configured as a \
3649                    valid URL"
3650                    .into(),
3651            });
3652        }
3653    };
3654    HttpResponse::Ok().json(doc)
3655}
3656
3657/// The session-revocation feed document (T-39, T-143).
3658#[derive(serde::Serialize, utoipa::ToSchema)]
3659pub struct RevocationFeedDocument {
3660    /// The digest algorithm the entries are computed with. Always `SHA-256`.
3661    /// A client that does not recognise the value MUST treat the document as
3662    /// unusable and behave exactly as it does when the feed is unreachable —
3663    /// never as though the list were empty, which is a guard that silently
3664    /// honours no revocations at all.
3665    pub alg: String,
3666    /// When this document was produced, as a Unix timestamp.
3667    pub issued_at: i64,
3668    /// How long an entry is published for, in seconds: one access-token
3669    /// lifetime. After that every token naming the session has expired on its
3670    /// own `exp`, so the entry proves nothing and is dropped.
3671    pub ttl: u64,
3672    /// Base64url-unpadded SHA-256 of each revoked session id, in no
3673    /// security-significant order. Never a session id, a subject or a tenant.
3674    pub revoked: Vec<String>,
3675}
3676
3677/// `GET /oauth2/revocations` -- sessions revoked within the last access-token
3678/// lifetime, as unlinkable hashes (T-39, T-143).
3679///
3680/// Mounted only where `AXIAM__AUTH__REVOCATION_FEED_ENABLED` is set; a
3681/// deployment that has not opted in does not serve this path at all.
3682///
3683/// An SDK route guard that polls this rejects a revoked session within one
3684/// poll interval rather than within one access-token lifetime. It is a
3685/// narrowing of a residual window and **not** a control: a guard that cannot
3686/// fetch the document, or that cannot interpret it, must behave exactly as it
3687/// does today. See `sdks/CONTRACT.md` §10.4.
3688//
3689// F3 note, deliberately not a doc comment: the reasoning for the entry format,
3690// the bound and the five properties lives in `axiam_core::revocation_feed`,
3691// where whoever changes this will read it. utoipa lifts a doc block verbatim
3692// into the OpenAPI `description` and eleven SDK repositories vendor that file
3693// byte-for-byte, so a design argument here becomes a re-vendor round.
3694#[utoipa::path(
3695    get,
3696    path = "/oauth2/revocations",
3697    tag = "oidc",
3698    responses(
3699        (status = 200, description = "Revocation feed", body = RevocationFeedDocument),
3700        (status = 304, description = "Not Modified -- ETag matches If-None-Match"),
3701    ),
3702)]
3703pub async fn revocations<C: Connection + Clone>(
3704    req: HttpRequest,
3705    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3706) -> HttpResponse {
3707    let ttl = state.auth_config.access_token_lifetime_secs;
3708    let repo = axiam_db::SurrealRevokedSessionRepository::new(state.db.clone());
3709    let revoked = match repo.list_live(chrono::Utc::now()).await {
3710        Ok(hashes) => hashes,
3711        Err(e) => {
3712            // A guard treats an unreachable feed exactly as it treats one it
3713            // never fetched, so failing loudly here costs nothing a client can
3714            // act on. Answering `200` with an empty list would be worse than
3715            // failing: it asserts that nothing has been revoked.
3716            tracing::warn!(error = %e, "revocation feed could not be read");
3717            return HttpResponse::ServiceUnavailable().finish();
3718        }
3719    };
3720
3721    let document = RevocationFeedDocument {
3722        alg: axiam_core::revocation_feed::REVOCATION_HASH_ALG.to_owned(),
3723        issued_at: chrono::Utc::now().timestamp(),
3724        ttl,
3725        revoked,
3726    };
3727
3728    // The ETag covers the entry list and nothing else. `issued_at` changes on
3729    // every call by construction, so including it would make every poll a
3730    // full transfer — which is the one thing a feed meant to be polled must
3731    // not do.
3732    let etag = {
3733        use sha2::Digest as _;
3734        let mut hasher = sha2::Sha256::new();
3735        for entry in &document.revoked {
3736            hasher.update(entry.as_bytes());
3737            hasher.update(b"\n");
3738        }
3739        format!(
3740            "\"{}\"",
3741            base64::Engine::encode(
3742                &base64::engine::general_purpose::URL_SAFE_NO_PAD,
3743                hasher.finalize()
3744            )
3745        )
3746    };
3747
3748    // Half a poll interval's worth of caching at the shortest interval the
3749    // contract permits: long enough that a misbehaving poller cannot turn the
3750    // feed into a load source, short enough that it cannot widen the window
3751    // the feed exists to narrow.
3752    let cache_control = "public, max-age=15";
3753
3754    if req
3755        .headers()
3756        .get(actix_web::http::header::IF_NONE_MATCH)
3757        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
3758        .is_some_and(|v| v == etag)
3759    {
3760        return HttpResponse::NotModified()
3761            .insert_header(("Cache-Control", cache_control))
3762            .insert_header(("ETag", etag))
3763            .finish();
3764    }
3765
3766    HttpResponse::Ok()
3767        .content_type("application/json")
3768        .insert_header(("Cache-Control", cache_control))
3769        .insert_header(("ETag", etag))
3770        .json(document)
3771}
3772
3773/// `GET /oauth2/jwks` -- JSON Web Key Set.
3774///
3775/// Returns the public signing keys used by the authorization server
3776/// so that relying parties can verify JWTs without sharing a secret.
3777///
3778/// B3: served from an in-process cache (`state.oauth2_jwks_cache`) keyed by
3779/// a hash of the source PEM, with a `Cache-Control: public, max-age=<n>`
3780/// header (configurable, default 300s) and a strong `ETag`. Clients that
3781/// send a matching `If-None-Match` get `304 Not Modified` with no body;
3782/// clients that ignore caching headers entirely still get the identical
3783/// `200 OK` + JWKS body they always did -- this is a pure additive change.
3784/// See `axiam_oauth2::jwks_cache` module docs for the cache design and the
3785/// documented limitations (no key-rotation mechanism exists yet; the
3786/// endpoint serves one global, not per-tenant, key set).
3787//
3788// F3 note, deliberately NOT a doc comment: the field is `state.oauth2.
3789// oauth2_jwks_cache` since the sub-state split. The line above still says
3790// `state.oauth2_jwks_cache` because utoipa lifts this doc block verbatim into
3791// the OpenAPI `description`, which eleven SDK repos vendor byte-for-byte. An
3792// internal field rename is not a reason to churn a published API artifact and
3793// re-vendor it eleven times; that the description names an internal field at
3794// all is a separate (real) smell, and fixing it deserves its own change with a
3795// spec regeneration and a re-vendor round.
3796#[utoipa::path(
3797    get,
3798    path = "/oauth2/jwks",
3799    tag = "oidc",
3800    responses(
3801        (status = 200, description = "JWKS document", body = JwksDocument),
3802        (status = 304, description = "Not Modified -- ETag matches If-None-Match"),
3803        (status = 500, description = "Key parsing error"),
3804    ),
3805)]
3806pub async fn jwks<C: Connection + Clone>(
3807    req: HttpRequest,
3808    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3809) -> HttpResponse {
3810    let if_none_match = req
3811        .headers()
3812        .get(actix_web::http::header::IF_NONE_MATCH)
3813        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok());
3814
3815    let cache_control = state.oauth2.oauth2_jwks_cache_config.cache_control_header();
3816
3817    match state
3818        .oauth2
3819        .oauth2_jwks_cache
3820        .get(&state.auth_config.jwt_public_key_pem, if_none_match)
3821    {
3822        Ok(JwksCacheResponse::Fresh { body, etag }) => HttpResponse::Ok()
3823            .content_type("application/json")
3824            .insert_header(("Cache-Control", cache_control))
3825            .insert_header(("ETag", etag))
3826            .body(body),
3827        Ok(JwksCacheResponse::NotModified { etag }) => HttpResponse::NotModified()
3828            .insert_header(("Cache-Control", cache_control))
3829            .insert_header(("ETag", etag))
3830            .finish(),
3831        Err(e) => HttpResponse::InternalServerError().json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
3832            error: "server_error".into(),
3833            error_description: e,
3834        }),
3835    }
3836}
3837
3838/// `GET /oauth2/userinfo` -- OIDC UserInfo endpoint.
3839///
3840/// Returns claims about the authenticated user. Requires a valid
3841/// Bearer access token whose account may still sign in (#520). Email and
3842/// username are included based on the scopes present in the access token.
3843#[utoipa::path(
3844    get,
3845    path = "/oauth2/userinfo",
3846    tag = "oidc",
3847    responses(
3848        (status = 200, description = "UserInfo response",
3849         body = UserInfoResponse),
3850        (status = 401, description = "Invalid, expired, revoked or missing access token, \
3851                                      or one whose account may no longer sign in"),
3852    ),
3853    security(("bearer" = []))
3854)]
3855pub async fn userinfo<C: Connection + Clone>(
3856    req: HttpRequest,
3857    user: AuthenticatedUser,
3858    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
3859) -> HttpResponse {
3860    userinfo_claims_for(&user, &state, Some(&req)).await
3861}
3862
3863/// The UserInfo response itself, for a principal already authenticated.
3864///
3865/// Shared verbatim by [`userinfo`] (GET) and [`userinfo_post`] (POST), which is
3866/// the whole reason W6 could add a method without touching what the endpoint
3867/// answers. Nothing here reads the request: the response is a function of the
3868/// token's subject and its scopes, so the two methods cannot diverge except in
3869/// how the token was carried.
3870/// W7 threads the `HttpRequest` in so the X7 G8 audit row can carry a peer
3871/// address. It is used for **nothing else**: the response stays a function of
3872/// the token's subject and its scopes, which is what keeps `GET` and `POST`
3873/// byte-identical (evidence row 91). `None` writes the audit row without an
3874/// address rather than not writing it.
3875async fn userinfo_claims_for<C: Connection + Clone>(
3876    user: &AuthenticatedUser,
3877    state: &AppState<C>,
3878    http_req: Option<&HttpRequest>,
3879) -> HttpResponse {
3880    let scopes: Vec<String> = user
3881        .claims
3882        .0
3883        .scope
3884        .as_deref()
3885        .unwrap_or("")
3886        .split_whitespace()
3887        .map(String::from)
3888        .collect();
3889
3890    let has_scope = |s: &str| scopes.iter().any(|sc| sc == s);
3891
3892    // OIDC Core §5.5 — claims this grant asked for by name, resolved at the
3893    // authorization endpoint and carried in the token. Already filtered to
3894    // what AXIAM will release on a request alone
3895    // (`axiam_oauth2::claims_request::RELEASABLE`), so nothing here has to
3896    // re-decide whether a claim is consent-gated: `phone_number`,
3897    // `phone_number_verified` and `address` can never appear in this list, and
3898    // `release_sensitive_claims` below is untouched by it.
3899    let requested: &[String] = user
3900        .claims
3901        .0
3902        .axiam_requested_claims
3903        .as_deref()
3904        .unwrap_or(&[]);
3905    let asked_for = |c: &str| requested.iter().any(|r| r == c);
3906    // A claim is released when the scope that bundles it was granted, **or**
3907    // when it was asked for by name. §5.5 is an alternative to the scope, not
3908    // a filter on it — a client may use either.
3909    let release = |claim: &str, scope: &str| has_scope(scope) || asked_for(claim);
3910
3911    // The account is read on every call (#520, P23W1-12), not only when a
3912    // scope needs a claim from it. UserInfo used to answer a suspended user's
3913    // access token until `exp`; it now applies the rule every grant and
3914    // `/oauth2/authorize` apply (`account_may_act`) and answers a locked,
3915    // inactive, anonymized, deleted or removed account with the same `401` an
3916    // expired or revoked token gets — one shape, so the answer says nothing
3917    // about which it was. One indexed read per call: UserInfo is a relying
3918    // party's occasional question, not the authorization hot path.
3919    //
3920    // UserInfo describes the SUBJECT of the token, and that account lives in
3921    // the tenant the subject inhabits — never one it happens to be acting on.
3922    // The two differ only for an organization-level principal whose request
3923    // carried `X-Axiam-Tenant`; reading the acting tenant there found no
3924    // account and answered with `sub` alone.
3925    let account = match state
3926        .user_repo
3927        .get_by_id(user.principal_tenant_id, user.user_id)
3928        .await
3929    {
3930        Ok(u) => u,
3931        Err(AxiamError::NotFound { .. }) => return userinfo_account_may_not_act(),
3932        Err(e) => {
3933            tracing::error!(
3934                user_id = %user.user_id,
3935                tenant_id = %user.principal_tenant_id,
3936                error = %e,
3937                "userinfo: failed to fetch user for scoped claims"
3938            );
3939            return HttpResponse::InternalServerError().json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
3940                error: "server_error".into(),
3941                error_description: "failed to retrieve user claims".into(),
3942            });
3943        }
3944    };
3945    if let Err(reason) = axiam_auth::service::account_may_act(&account) {
3946        tracing::info!(
3947            user_id = %user.user_id,
3948            tenant_id = %user.principal_tenant_id,
3949            reason = %reason,
3950            "userinfo: refusing a token whose account may no longer sign in"
3951        );
3952        return userinfo_account_may_not_act();
3953    }
3954
3955    // The row is needed by every releasable claim, so its claims are taken
3956    // when any of them might be released rather than when a particular scope
3957    // is present.
3958    let (email, profile) = if has_scope("email") || has_scope("profile") || !requested.is_empty() {
3959        let u = account;
3960        (
3961            if release("email", "email") || asked_for("email_verified") {
3962                // Both members or neither: `email_verified` describes
3963                // `email`, so they are produced by one branch rather than
3964                // by two that could drift apart.
3965                Some((u.email, u.email_verified_at.is_some()))
3966            } else {
3967                None
3968            },
3969            // Read whenever *any* profile claim might be released. Which
3970            // ones actually appear is decided per claim below, because
3971            // §5.5 lets a client ask for `nickname` without asking for
3972            // `name`.
3973            Some((
3974                u.username,
3975                axiam_core::models::user::ProfileClaims::from_metadata(&u.metadata),
3976                u.updated_at,
3977            )),
3978        )
3979    } else {
3980        (None, None)
3981    };
3982
3983    // W7 / X7 G8 — the sensitive claims, decided here and nowhere else.
3984    let released = release_sensitive_claims(user, state, &scopes, http_req).await;
3985
3986    HttpResponse::Ok().json(UserInfoResponse {
3987        sub: user.user_id.to_string(),
3988        email: email
3989            .as_ref()
3990            .filter(|_| release("email", "email"))
3991            .map(|(address, _)| address.clone()),
3992        email_verified: email
3993            .as_ref()
3994            .filter(|_| release("email_verified", "email"))
3995            .map(|&(_, verified)| verified),
3996        preferred_username: profile
3997            .as_ref()
3998            .filter(|_| release("preferred_username", "profile"))
3999            .map(|(username, _, _)| username.clone()),
4000        // Absent, not null, when SCIM never provisioned them — OIDC Core
4001        // §5.3.2: a claim the OP cannot assert is omitted.
4002        name: profile
4003            .as_ref()
4004            .filter(|_| release("name", "profile"))
4005            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.name.clone()),
4006        given_name: profile
4007            .as_ref()
4008            .filter(|_| release("given_name", "profile"))
4009            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.given_name.clone()),
4010        family_name: profile
4011            .as_ref()
4012            .filter(|_| release("family_name", "profile"))
4013            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.family_name.clone()),
4014        middle_name: profile
4015            .as_ref()
4016            .filter(|_| release("middle_name", "profile"))
4017            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.middle_name.clone()),
4018        nickname: profile
4019            .as_ref()
4020            .filter(|_| release("nickname", "profile"))
4021            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.nickname.clone()),
4022        profile: profile
4023            .as_ref()
4024            .filter(|_| release("profile", "profile"))
4025            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.profile.clone()),
4026        picture: profile
4027            .as_ref()
4028            .filter(|_| release("picture", "profile"))
4029            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.picture.clone()),
4030        website: profile
4031            .as_ref()
4032            .filter(|_| release("website", "profile"))
4033            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.website.clone()),
4034        gender: profile
4035            .as_ref()
4036            .filter(|_| release("gender", "profile"))
4037            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.gender.clone()),
4038        birthdate: profile
4039            .as_ref()
4040            .filter(|_| release("birthdate", "profile"))
4041            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.birthdate.clone()),
4042        zoneinfo: profile
4043            .as_ref()
4044            .filter(|_| release("zoneinfo", "profile"))
4045            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.zoneinfo.clone()),
4046        locale: profile
4047            .as_ref()
4048            .filter(|_| release("locale", "profile"))
4049            .and_then(|(_, c, _)| c.locale.clone()),
4050        // OIDC Core §5.1 wants a NumericDate, not an RFC 3339 string.
4051        //
4052        // Scope-only: `updated_at` describes when the profile last changed, so
4053        // releasing it to a client that was granted no profile claim would
4054        // answer a question it did not ask and could not interpret.
4055        updated_at: profile
4056            .as_ref()
4057            .filter(|_| has_scope("profile"))
4058            .map(|&(_, _, at)| at.timestamp()),
4059        phone_number: released.phone_number,
4060        phone_number_verified: released.phone_number_verified,
4061        address: released.address,
4062        tenant_id: user.tenant_id.to_string(),
4063        org_id: user.org_id.to_string(),
4064    })
4065}
4066
4067/// UserInfo's answer for a token whose account may no longer act (#520,
4068/// P23W1-12): the `401` the authentication extractor gives an expired or
4069/// revoked token, built from the same error, so a relying party cannot tell a
4070/// suspended account from a dead token.
4071fn userinfo_account_may_not_act() -> HttpResponse {
4072    use actix_web::ResponseError as _;
4073    crate::error::AxiamApiError::from(AxiamError::AuthenticationFailed {
4074        reason: "session revoked or expired".into(),
4075    })
4076    .error_response()
4077}
4078
4079/// What the sensitive-scope gates allowed this UserInfo call to say.
4080///
4081/// A struct rather than a tuple so the two telephone members cannot be
4082/// separated: `phone_number_verified` is a statement *about* `phone_number`
4083/// and emitting it alone would assert something about a value the relying
4084/// party was not given.
4085#[derive(Default)]
4086struct ReleasedSensitiveClaims {
4087    phone_number: Option<String>,
4088    phone_number_verified: Option<bool>,
4089    address: Option<axiam_core::models::user::Address>,
4090}
4091
4092impl ReleasedSensitiveClaims {
4093    /// The claim **names** released, for the audit row. Never the values.
4094    fn names(&self) -> Vec<&'static str> {
4095        let mut names = Vec::new();
4096        if self.phone_number.is_some() {
4097            names.push("phone_number");
4098        }
4099        if self.phone_number_verified.is_some() {
4100            names.push("phone_number_verified");
4101        }
4102        if self.address.is_some() {
4103            names.push("address");
4104        }
4105        names
4106    }
4107}
4108
4109/// Decide, and record, what `address` and `phone` release for this call
4110/// (X7 G8, plan §4.8).
4111///
4112/// # Every gate is re-asked here, at the moment of release
4113///
4114/// The authorization endpoint asked all of them too, and none of its answers
4115/// is reused. That is the whole design: an access token lives fifteen minutes
4116/// and a refresh of it lives thirty days, so a decision taken when the token
4117/// was minted is a decision that outlives the facts it rested on. Asking again
4118/// is what makes withdrawal immediate (T8.4) rather than effective on the next
4119/// token.
4120///
4121/// In order, cheapest and most decisive first:
4122///
4123/// 1. **The token's scopes.** No `phone`, no telephone number. A scope the
4124///    relying party never asked for cannot be released by any later gate
4125///    saying yes.
4126/// 2. **The relying party.** Taken from the token's `client_id`
4127///    (RFC 9068 §2.2). A token that names none — every token issued before W7,
4128///    and every token minted by a login, a device flow or an exchange — cannot
4129///    have a consent record looked up for it, and releases nothing. Fail
4130///    closed, and the direction matters: the alternative is "any consent this
4131///    subject ever gave", which would hand a postal address to a client the
4132///    subject consented to a *different* client receiving.
4133/// 3. **The profile (M10).** A `fapi2` client is refused here regardless of
4134///    scope, switch or record — the third of the three places the FAPI gate
4135///    runs, and the only one where no authorization request is in hand.
4136/// 4. **The tenant switch.** The operator's decision outranks the subject's.
4137/// 5. **The consent record**, matched on client *and* on the exact scope set.
4138///
4139/// # A read that fails releases nothing
4140///
4141/// Every fallible step below resolves to "release nothing" and logs. A
4142/// UserInfo response missing an optional claim is what OIDC Core §5.3.2 calls
4143/// an omitted claim and what the conformance suite treats as a WARNING; a
4144/// UserInfo response carrying a postal address the subject withdrew is a
4145/// personal-data breach. Those are not comparable failures and the code does
4146/// not treat them as if they were.
4147async fn release_sensitive_claims<C: Connection + Clone>(
4148    user: &AuthenticatedUser,
4149    state: &AppState<C>,
4150    scopes: &[String],
4151    http_req: Option<&HttpRequest>,
4152) -> ReleasedSensitiveClaims {
4153    let none = ReleasedSensitiveClaims::default();
4154
4155    // 1. Scope.
4156    let wanted = axiam_oauth2::sensitive::requested(scopes);
4157    if wanted.is_empty() {
4158        return none;
4159    }
4160
4161    // 2. The relying party.
4162    let Some(client_id) = user.claims.0.client_id.as_deref() else {
4163        tracing::debug!(
4164            user_id = %user.user_id,
4165            "an access token carrying a sensitive scope names no client_id, so no consent \
4166             record can be found for it; releasing nothing"
4167        );
4168        return none;
4169    };
4170
4171    // 3. The profile — M10.
4172    match state
4173        .oauth2_client_repo
4174        .get_by_client_id(user.principal_tenant_id, client_id)
4175        .await
4176    {
4177        Ok(client) if client.profile.is_fapi2() => {
4178            tracing::warn!(
4179                %client_id,
4180                "a fapi2-issued access token carries a GDPR-sensitive scope; the fapi2 lane \
4181                 collects no consent record and nothing is released"
4182            );
4183            return none;
4184        }
4185        Ok(_) => {}
4186        Err(e) => {
4187            tracing::error!(
4188                error = %e,
4189                %client_id,
4190                "could not read the client behind this access token; releasing no sensitive \
4191                 claims rather than releasing them unprofiled"
4192            );
4193            return none;
4194        }
4195    }
4196
4197    // 4. The tenant switch.
4198    let enabled = match state.tenant_repo.get_by_id(user.principal_tenant_id).await {
4199        Ok(tenant) => axiam_core::repository::SettingsRepository::get_effective_settings(
4200            &state.settings_repo,
4201            tenant.organization_id,
4202            user.principal_tenant_id,
4203        )
4204        .await
4205        .map(|s| s.oidc.sensitive_scopes_enabled)
4206        .unwrap_or(false),
4207        Err(_) => false,
4208    };
4209    if !enabled {
4210        return none;
4211    }
4212
4213    // 5. The consent record, on this client and this exact scope set.
4214    let consent_type = axiam_oauth2::sensitive::consent_type(client_id);
4215    let version = axiam_oauth2::sensitive::consent_version(&wanted);
4216    let consented = axiam_core::repository::ConsentRepository::list_by_user(
4217        &state.gdpr.consent_repo,
4218        user.principal_tenant_id,
4219        user.user_id,
4220    )
4221    .await
4222    .map(|records| {
4223        records
4224            .iter()
4225            .any(|c| c.consent_type == consent_type && c.version == version)
4226    })
4227    .unwrap_or(false);
4228    if !consented {
4229        return none;
4230    }
4231
4232    // Only now is the row read. Reaching for the data before the gates had
4233    // finished would mean a telephone number in memory on a path that was
4234    // never going to release it.
4235    let Ok(subject) = state
4236        .user_repo
4237        .get_by_id(user.principal_tenant_id, user.user_id)
4238        .await
4239    else {
4240        tracing::error!(
4241            user_id = %user.user_id,
4242            "could not read the subject for its consented claims; releasing nothing"
4243        );
4244        return none;
4245    };
4246
4247    let mut released = ReleasedSensitiveClaims::default();
4248    if wanted.contains(&"phone") {
4249        // The verified flag rides with the number and never travels alone.
4250        if let Some(number) = subject.phone_number {
4251            released.phone_number_verified = Some(subject.phone_number_verified_at.is_some());
4252            released.phone_number = Some(number);
4253        }
4254    }
4255    if wanted.contains(&"address") {
4256        released.address = subject.address;
4257    }
4258
4259    let names = released.names();
4260    if !names.is_empty() {
4261        audit_sensitive_claims_released(state, http_req, user, client_id, &names).await;
4262    }
4263    released
4264}
4265
4266/// Record that sensitive claims were released — **names, never values**
4267/// (X7 G8, T8.6).
4268///
4269/// The audit log is queryable by operators and exported to subjects under
4270/// Art. 15, so a row carrying the telephone number would be a second copy of
4271/// the personal data in a store whose whole point is that it is append-only
4272/// and cannot be erased. What the row has to answer is *who was given what
4273/// kind of thing, when*, and claim names answer that exactly.
4274///
4275/// A failure to write is logged and swallowed. Losing an audit row is bad;
4276/// failing a UserInfo call the four gates already allowed is worse, and would
4277/// turn the audit store's availability into the relying party's.
4278async fn audit_sensitive_claims_released<C: Connection + Clone>(
4279    state: &AppState<C>,
4280    http_req: Option<&HttpRequest>,
4281    user: &AuthenticatedUser,
4282    client_id: &str,
4283    claim_names: &[&str],
4284) {
4285    if let Err(e) = state
4286        .audit_repo
4287        .append(CreateAuditLogEntry {
4288            tenant_id: user.principal_tenant_id,
4289            actor_id: user.user_id,
4290            actor_type: ActorType::User,
4291            action: "userinfo.sensitive_claims_released".into(),
4292            resource_id: None,
4293            outcome: AuditOutcome::Success,
4294            ip_address: http_req.and_then(peer_ip),
4295            metadata: Some(serde_json::json!({
4296                "client_id": truncate_bytes_on_char_boundary(client_id),
4297                "claims": claim_names,
4298            })),
4299        })
4300        .await
4301    {
4302        tracing::error!(
4303            error = %e,
4304            user_id = %user.user_id,
4305            %client_id,
4306            "could not record a sensitive-claims release; the UserInfo response is unaffected"
4307        );
4308    }
4309}
4310
4311/// The form body `POST /oauth2/userinfo` accepts (RFC 6750 §2.2).
4312///
4313/// Unknown fields are ignored, as serde does by default: RFC 6750 §2.2 defines
4314/// one parameter and says nothing about others, and a UserInfo request that
4315/// also carried, say, a `client_id` is not malformed.
4316#[derive(Deserialize, utoipa::ToSchema)]
4317pub struct UserInfoPostForm {
4318    /// The access token, per RFC 6750 §2.2.
4319    access_token: Option<String>,
4320}
4321
4322/// Hand-written so that the token cannot be logged by anyone who reaches for
4323/// `?form` — hazard 1 of plan §4.9's G10, and the same line the repository drew
4324/// in `e33977d` for a `Set-Cookie` header carrying a credential.
4325///
4326/// A derived `Debug` would print the bearer token verbatim into whichever
4327/// `tracing` field, panic message or `dbg!` picked it up. Deriving nothing at
4328/// all would make that a compile error, which is stronger — but it is also
4329/// undone by anyone who adds `#[derive(Debug)]` to make a borrow checker
4330/// message readable. This states the intent where it will be read.
4331impl std::fmt::Debug for UserInfoPostForm {
4332    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
4333        f.debug_struct("UserInfoPostForm")
4334            .field(
4335                "access_token",
4336                &self.access_token.as_ref().map(|_| "<redacted>"),
4337            )
4338            .finish()
4339    }
4340}
4341
4342/// `POST /oauth2/userinfo` — the same endpoint, the other method (W6, plan
4343/// §4.9 G10; OIDC Core §5.3, which says an OP MUST support both).
4344///
4345/// # Why a second handler rather than a second extractor
4346///
4347/// The access token may arrive in the `Authorization` header (RFC 6750 §2.1) or
4348/// — on POST only — in an `access_token` form field (§2.2). The second carrier
4349/// lives in the body, which [`AuthenticatedUser`]'s `FromRequest` impl does not
4350/// read and must not start reading: that impl is on the path `GET` takes, and
4351/// on the path every other authenticated route in the tree takes. Resolving the
4352/// token here instead leaves all of them untouched, which is what makes "GET
4353/// behaviour is unchanged" a property of the routing table rather than a claim
4354/// about a shared code path. Both arms then hand the same
4355/// [`AuthenticatedUser`] to the same [`userinfo_claims`].
4356///
4357/// # The refusals, and where each is decided
4358///
4359/// * **Two carriers, one request.** RFC 6750 §2: *"Clients MUST NOT use more
4360///   than one method to transmit the token in each request."* A form field
4361///   presented together with an `Authorization` header is refused here with
4362///   `400 invalid_request`, and the refusal names neither token — it does not
4363///   read them at all, so there is nothing to leak into the body or the log.
4364///   The `axiam_access` cookie is refused alongside the form field for the same
4365///   reason even though it is not one of RFC 6750's methods: it is a second
4366///   credential naming a possibly different subject, and the alternative to
4367///   refusing is choosing silently between two identities.
4368/// * **The cookie alone still authenticates, exactly as on GET.** That is
4369///   today's behaviour and invariant 4 keeps it. It is a CSRF surface in
4370///   principle — `/oauth2` is not wrapped in `CsrfMiddleware`, only `/api/v1`
4371///   is — and it fails closed in practice because `axiam_access` is
4372///   `SameSite=Strict` (`middleware::csrf::access_cookie`), so a cross-site
4373///   form POST carries no cookie and lands here unauthenticated. That is a
4374///   property of the cookie, not of this handler, so it is pinned by a test
4375///   rather than asserted by this comment
4376///   (`oauth2_userinfo_post_test.rs::the_access_cookie_is_strict_so_a_cross_site_post_cannot_be_authenticated_by_it`).
4377/// * **`access_token` in the query string is never read** — on either method.
4378///   RFC 6750 §2.3 deprecates that form because it puts a credential in a URL,
4379///   a `Referer` and every access log in the path. Refusing it would mean
4380///   reading it first, and the parameter's problem is that it exists at all,
4381///   not that a server honours it. So there is no code here that looks at the
4382///   query string, and a `GET /oauth2/userinfo?access_token=…` is simply an
4383///   unauthenticated request with an odd URL.
4384///
4385/// # An empty field is not a carrier
4386///
4387/// `access_token=` transmits no token, so it is treated as absent rather than
4388/// as a second method or as an invalid credential. A request carrying an empty
4389/// field and a real `Authorization` header is answered, not refused.
4390#[utoipa::path(
4391    post,
4392    path = "/oauth2/userinfo",
4393    tag = "oidc",
4394    request_body(
4395        content = UserInfoPostForm,
4396        content_type = "application/x-www-form-urlencoded",
4397        description = "RFC 6750 §2.2 form-encoded access token. Omit the body \
4398                       entirely to present the token in the Authorization header \
4399                       instead; presenting it both ways is refused."
4400    ),
4401    responses(
4402        (status = 200, description = "UserInfo response — byte-identical to what \
4403                                      GET answers for the same token",
4404         body = UserInfoResponse),
4405        (status = 400, description = "The access token was presented by more than \
4406                                      one method (RFC 6750 §2)"),
4407        (status = 401, description = "Invalid, expired, revoked or missing access token, \
4408                                      or one whose account may no longer sign in"),
4409    ),
4410    security(("bearer" = []))
4411)]
4412pub async fn userinfo_post<C: Connection + Clone>(
4413    req: HttpRequest,
4414    form: Option<web::Form<UserInfoPostForm>>,
4415    maybe_user: MaybeAuthenticatedUser,
4416    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
4417) -> HttpResponse {
4418    use actix_web::ResponseError as _;
4419
4420    let body_token = form
4421        .as_ref()
4422        .and_then(|f| f.access_token.as_deref())
4423        .map(str::trim)
4424        .filter(|t| !t.is_empty());
4425
4426    let Some(body_token) = body_token else {
4427        // No §2.2 carrier: this is a header- or cookie-authenticated request,
4428        // and `MaybeAuthenticatedUser` holds precisely what actix would have
4429        // handed a `AuthenticatedUser` handler — the same principal, or the
4430        // same 401.
4431        return match maybe_user.into_result() {
4432            Ok(user) => userinfo_claims_for(&user, &state, Some(&req)).await,
4433            Err(e) => e.error_response(),
4434        };
4435    };
4436
4437    let header_present = req
4438        .headers()
4439        .contains_key(actix_web::http::header::AUTHORIZATION);
4440    let cookie_present = req.cookie("axiam_access").is_some();
4441    if header_present || cookie_present {
4442        return HttpResponse::BadRequest().json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
4443            error: "invalid_request".into(),
4444            error_description: "the access token was presented by more than one method; \
4445                 RFC 6750 section 2 permits exactly one per request"
4446                .into(),
4447        });
4448    }
4449
4450    match crate::extractors::auth::authenticate_presented_token(&req, body_token).await {
4451        Ok(user) => userinfo_claims_for(&user, &state, Some(&req)).await,
4452        Err(e) => e.error_response(),
4453    }
4454}
4455
4456// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
4457// Helpers
4458// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
4459
4460/// Build a redirect response with error parameters per RFC 6749
4461/// section 4.1.2.1.
4462/// Append the RFC 9207 `iss` authorization-response parameter (X5.1).
4463///
4464/// # Why this matters, and why it is unconditional
4465///
4466/// RFC 9207 exists because of the **mix-up attack**: a client that talks to
4467/// more than one authorization server, and receives an authorization response
4468/// on a shared redirect URI, cannot otherwise tell which server sent it. An
4469/// attacker who controls one of those servers can therefore have a code minted
4470/// by an honest server delivered to the attacker's own token endpoint — or the
4471/// reverse. `iss` names the sender, so the client can check that the response
4472/// came from the server it started the flow with.
4473///
4474/// Emitted for **every** client, not just FAPI ones. RFC 9207 §2.4 says an AS
4475/// that supports the parameter SHOULD include it in every authorization
4476/// response; a client that does not understand it ignores an unknown query
4477/// parameter, which is the behaviour RFC 6749 §4.1.2 already requires of it.
4478/// Making it conditional would mean a client only gets mix-up protection when
4479/// somebody remembered to switch it on — and mix-up is precisely the attack a
4480/// client does not know it is under.
4481///
4482/// It goes on the **error** redirect too. RFC 9207 §2.4 is explicit about
4483/// this, and it is not a formality: one variant of the mix-up attack works by
4484/// injecting an error response, so a client that validates `iss` on success
4485/// and skips it on failure has left the door it just closed ajar.
4486/// The config this request stamps an `iss` with (T21.6).
4487///
4488/// The deployment's own, borrowed and free, for every request that did not
4489/// arrive on a `/t/{tenant_id}` path — which is every request on a deployment
4490/// with `AXIAM__AUTH__TENANT_ISSUER_PATHS` unset. A clone carrying the tenant
4491/// issuer for one that did, so that the RFC 9207 `iss` parameter, the access
4492/// token, the ID token and the logout token all say what the discovery document
4493/// the client read says.
4494fn issuer_config<'a, C: Connection + Clone>(
4495    req: &HttpRequest,
4496    state: &'a AppState<C>,
4497) -> std::borrow::Cow<'a, AuthConfig> {
4498    crate::middleware::tenant_path::minting_config(req, &state.auth_config)
4499}
4500
4501/// [`issuer_config`] for a token-endpoint grant, which carries the issuer on
4502/// its [`TokenRequestContext`] rather than reading the request again (T21.6).
4503fn uma_issuer_config<'a, C: Connection + Clone>(
4504    ctx: &TokenRequestContext,
4505    state: &'a AppState<C>,
4506) -> std::borrow::Cow<'a, AuthConfig> {
4507    match ctx.issuer.as_deref() {
4508        None => std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(&state.auth_config),
4509        Some(issuer) => std::borrow::Cow::Owned(AuthConfig {
4510            request_issuer: Some(issuer.to_owned()),
4511            ..state.auth_config.clone()
4512        }),
4513    }
4514}
4515
4516fn append_issuer(url: &mut url::Url, auth_config: &AuthConfig) {
4517    let issuer = auth_config.effective_issuer();
4518    if issuer.is_empty() {
4519        // Nothing sensible to assert. Emitting an empty `iss` would be worse
4520        // than omitting it: a client comparing it against its configured
4521        // issuer would fail every flow, and one comparing loosely might pass
4522        // anything.
4523        tracing::warn!(
4524            "no issuer is configured, so the RFC 9207 `iss` authorization-response              parameter cannot be emitted; clients cannot detect a mix-up attack"
4525        );
4526        return;
4527    }
4528    url.query_pairs_mut().append_pair("iss", issuer);
4529}
4530
4531fn build_error_redirect(
4532    redirect_uri: &str,
4533    error: &OAuth2Error,
4534    state: Option<&str>,
4535    auth_config: &AuthConfig,
4536) -> HttpResponse {
4537    let mut url = match url::Url::parse(redirect_uri) {
4538        Ok(u) => u,
4539        Err(_) => {
4540            // Unparseable redirect_uri — return direct error
4541            return HttpResponse::BadRequest().json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
4542                error: error.error_code().to_string(),
4543                error_description: error.error_description(),
4544            });
4545        }
4546    };
4547    url.query_pairs_mut()
4548        .append_pair("error", error.error_code())
4549        .append_pair("error_description", &error.error_description());
4550    if let Some(state) = state {
4551        url.query_pairs_mut().append_pair("state", state);
4552    }
4553    // RFC 9207 §2.4 — see `append_issuer`. Error responses carry `iss` too,
4554    // because one form of the mix-up attack is an injected error response.
4555    append_issuer(&mut url, auth_config);
4556    HttpResponse::Found()
4557        .append_header(("Location", url.to_string()))
4558        .finish()
4559}
4560
4561/// `POST /oauth2/device_authorization` — RFC 8628 §3.1–3.2 (B2).
4562///
4563/// Issues a `device_code` (the secret the device polls with), a short
4564/// `user_code` (the string a human reads off a screen and types elsewhere),
4565/// and the `verification_uri` to send them to.
4566///
4567/// **The client authenticates by its registered method (#635).** RFC 8628
4568/// exists for input-constrained clients — a television, a CLI, an IoT sensor —
4569/// which are usually public: a client registered with
4570/// `token_endpoint_auth_method: none` presents nothing, as before, and is
4571/// refused if it presents a credential. A confidential client presents its
4572/// credential here as at the token endpoint (RFC 8628 §3.1 → RFC 6749
4573/// §3.2.1): its secret, a client assertion, or its certificate. Either way
4574/// `client_id` must name a client of a live tenant that holds this grant;
4575/// without that any string could mint pending grants and exhaust the
4576/// user-code space, which is a denial of service against every legitimate
4577/// device at once. A failed client authentication is `401 invalid_client`
4578/// with the RFC 6749 §5.2 challenge, and is audited as at the token endpoint.
4579///
4580/// The endpoint carries its own rate-limit bucket (see `server.rs`) rather
4581/// than sharing the generic token bucket: it is reachable without a
4582/// credential and it allocates state, which is a different abuse profile from
4583/// the token endpoint's.
4584#[utoipa::path(
4585    post,
4586    path = "/oauth2/device_authorization",
4587    tag = "oauth2",
4588    params(TenantQuery),
4589    request_body(
4590        content_type = "application/x-www-form-urlencoded",
4591        content = DeviceAuthorizationRequest,
4592    ),
4593    responses(
4594        (status = 200, description = "Device and user codes",
4595         body = DeviceAuthorizationResponse),
4596        (status = 400, description = "OAuth2 error", body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
4597        (status = 401, description = "Client authentication failed",
4598         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
4599    ),
4600)]
4601pub async fn device_authorization<C: Connection + Clone>(
4602    http_req: HttpRequest,
4603    tenant_query: web::Query<TenantQuery>,
4604    form: web::Form<DeviceAuthorizationRequest>,
4605    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
4606) -> HttpResponse {
4607    // W8 / RFC 6749 §5.2 — the challenge is decided from the request, before
4608    // the handler runs, and applied to whatever 401 it produces.
4609    let challenge = client_auth_challenge(&http_req);
4610    with_client_auth_challenge(
4611        device_authorization_inner(http_req, tenant_query, form, state).await,
4612        challenge,
4613    )
4614}
4615
4616/// See [`token_inner`] for why this split exists.
4617async fn device_authorization_inner<C: Connection + Clone>(
4618    http_req: HttpRequest,
4619    tenant_query: web::Query<TenantQuery>,
4620    form: web::Form<DeviceAuthorizationRequest>,
4621    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
4622) -> HttpResponse {
4623    let tenant_id = tenant_query.into_inner().tenant_id;
4624    let req = form.into_inner();
4625
4626    // No CIMD materialisation here, unlike PAR and the token endpoint: a
4627    // `managed_by: cimd` client can never hold this grant (`cimd.rs` admits
4628    // the two code grants only), so a URL-shaped `client_id` would buy an
4629    // outbound fetch for a refusal.
4630    //
4631    // #635 — the token endpoint's client-authentication path: the same
4632    // request context (the Basic header, the verified client certificate, the
4633    // assertion), the registration deciding the method.
4634    let ctx = match token_request_context(&http_req) {
4635        Ok(ctx) => ctx.with_assertion(
4636            req.client_assertion.as_deref(),
4637            req.client_assertion_type.as_deref(),
4638        ),
4639        Err(response) => return *response,
4640    };
4641    let client_id = match axiam_oauth2::token::resolve_client_id(req.client_id.as_deref(), &ctx) {
4642        Ok(id) => id.to_owned(),
4643        Err(e) => return build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
4644    };
4645    let client = match state
4646        .oauth2
4647        .token_service
4648        .authenticate_device_authorization(
4649            tenant_id,
4650            &client_id,
4651            req.client_secret.as_deref(),
4652            &ctx,
4653        )
4654        .await
4655    {
4656        Ok(client) => client,
4657        Err(e) => {
4658            if matches!(e, OAuth2Error::InvalidClient(_)) {
4659                // The token endpoint's detection, for the same event at a
4660                // second endpoint: detached, so the response time says nothing
4661                // about the tenant (§22.3 residual 1), and attributed by the
4662                // same SEC-087 rules.
4663                let tenant_repo = state.tenant_repo.clone();
4664                let audit_repo = state.audit_repo.clone();
4665                let peer = peer_ip(&http_req);
4666                let forwarded = client_ip(&http_req);
4667                actix_web::rt::spawn(async move {
4668                    append_client_auth_failure_audit(
4669                        &tenant_repo,
4670                        &audit_repo,
4671                        tenant_id,
4672                        Some(client_id.as_str()),
4673                        DEVICE_CODE_GRANT_TYPE,
4674                        peer,
4675                        forwarded,
4676                    )
4677                    .await;
4678                });
4679            }
4680            return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
4681        }
4682    };
4683
4684    match state
4685        .oauth2
4686        .device_authorization_service
4687        .authorize(tenant_id, client, req)
4688        .await
4689    {
4690        Ok(resp) => HttpResponse::Ok()
4691            .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
4692            .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"))
4693            .json(resp),
4694        Err(e) => build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
4695    }
4696}
4697
4698/// The `urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:token-exchange` arm of the token
4699/// endpoint (B3).
4700///
4701/// The form is re-read rather than typed at the route because RFC 8693's
4702/// parameters are disjoint from RFC 6749's and actix deserializes one body
4703/// once — so `TokenRequest` carries the shared fields and the
4704/// exchange-specific ones are pulled from the raw form here.
4705/// X2 — `grant_type=urn:ietf:params:oauth:grant-type:uma-ticket`.
4706///
4707/// Redeems a permission ticket for an RPT. Four things are worth naming.
4708///
4709/// **The client authenticates, and the ticket is bound to it.** A ticket is
4710/// minted by a resource server through the Protection API and redeemed by that
4711/// same resource server; `consume` matches on `client_id`, so a ticket leaked
4712/// to another client is not a usable credential — and a wrong-client attempt
4713/// changes nothing rather than burning the rightful holder's ticket.
4714///
4715/// **`claim_token` is required, though UMA 2.0 calls it optional.** The spec's
4716/// other two ways to name a requesting party — an RPT presented for
4717/// incremental authorization, and interactive claims-gathering — are both
4718/// deferred to v2, so this is the only channel that exists. Requiring it and
4719/// saying so beats resolving to some default subject, which would mint an RPT
4720/// for a party nobody named.
4721///
4722/// **The subject token is validated, not trusted.** It is a signed AXIAM access
4723/// token; its remaining life bounds the RPT's, which is what stops an RPT from
4724/// outliving the authorization it rests on.
4725///
4726/// **Refusals are uniform.** Every ticket rejection — unknown, consumed,
4727/// expired, wrong client — answers `invalid_grant` with one message, because a
4728/// caller that could tell them apart could probe for live ticket handles.
4729async fn handle_uma_ticket<C: Connection + Clone>(
4730    tenant_id: Uuid,
4731    form: TokenRequest,
4732    state: &web::Data<AppState<C>>,
4733    authz: &crate::authz::AuthzData,
4734    ctx: &TokenRequestContext,
4735) -> HttpResponse {
4736    // SEC-093: only `client_id` is required up front. Whether the credential
4737    // that must accompany it is a secret, a certificate or a signed assertion
4738    // is a property of the REGISTRATION, so it cannot be decided before the
4739    // lookup — `authenticate_client` decides it, and answers the same
4740    // `invalid_client` for "no credential" as for "wrong credential".
4741    let Some(client_id) = form.client_id.as_deref() else {
4742        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidClient(
4743            "the uma-ticket grant requires client authentication".into(),
4744        ));
4745    };
4746
4747    let client = match state
4748        .oauth2
4749        .token_service
4750        .authenticate_client(tenant_id, client_id, form.client_secret.as_deref(), ctx)
4751        .await
4752    {
4753        Ok(client) => client,
4754        Err(e) => return build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
4755    };
4756
4757    // T21.2 — a public client may not obtain an RPT. The uma-ticket grant
4758    // mints a token naming a requesting party on the strength of the calling
4759    // client's identity, and a public client's identity is a `client_id`
4760    // anybody who has seen one of its authorization requests knows. The admin
4761    // API cannot refuse this combination at registration — `uma-ticket` is not
4762    // a registrable grant type — so the refusal has to be here.
4763    if client.token_endpoint_auth_method.is_public() {
4764        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::UnauthorizedClient(
4765            "a public client (token_endpoint_auth_method: none) cannot use the uma-ticket \
4766             grant: it presents no credential, and an RPT must be attributable"
4767                .into(),
4768        ));
4769    }
4770
4771    // Counted after authentication, keyed by the authenticated client — the
4772    // same reasoning as the token-exchange bucket: an unauthenticated caller
4773    // must not be able to consume a real client's allowance, and per-IP would
4774    // collapse a whole mesh behind one NAT into a single bucket.
4775    if !state.shared_rate_limit.check_at(
4776        &format!("oauth2_uma_ticket:{}:{}", tenant_id, client.client_id),
4777        chrono::Utc::now(),
4778        state.rate_limit_cfg.uma_ticket_per_min,
4779    ) {
4780        return HttpResponse::TooManyRequests()
4781            .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
4782            .json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
4783                error: "slow_down".into(),
4784                error_description: "uma-ticket rate limit exceeded for this client".into(),
4785            });
4786    }
4787
4788    // SEC-096: the same two gates the token-exchange grant now runs, for the
4789    // same reason — an RPT is an access token, and a `fapi2` client must not
4790    // be able to obtain an unconstrained one through the grant nobody wired
4791    // the profile gate into.
4792    if let Err(e) = axiam_oauth2::fapi::enforce_token_request(&client, ctx.evidence()) {
4793        return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
4794    }
4795    let cnf = match state
4796        .oauth2
4797        .token_service
4798        .certificate_binding_for(&client, ctx)
4799    {
4800        Ok(cnf) => cnf,
4801        Err(e) => return build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
4802    };
4803
4804    let Some(ticket) = form.ticket.as_deref().filter(|t| !t.is_empty()) else {
4805        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest(
4806            "ticket is required for the uma-ticket grant".into(),
4807        ));
4808    };
4809
4810    let Some(claim_token) = form.claim_token.as_deref().filter(|t| !t.is_empty()) else {
4811        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest(
4812            "claim_token is required for the uma-ticket grant: it names the \
4813             requesting party, and v1 implements neither incremental \
4814             authorization nor claims-gathering"
4815                .into(),
4816        ));
4817    };
4818
4819    // An unrecognised format is refused rather than assumed. Guessing would
4820    // mean validating a foreign token with the wrong verifier.
4821    if let Some(format) = form.claim_token_format.as_deref().filter(|f| !f.is_empty())
4822        && format != UMA_CLAIM_TOKEN_FORMAT
4823    {
4824        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest(format!(
4825            "unsupported claim_token_format '{format}'; v1 accepts only \
4826             '{UMA_CLAIM_TOKEN_FORMAT}'"
4827        )));
4828    }
4829
4830    let claims = match axiam_auth::token::validate_access_token(claim_token, &state.auth_config) {
4831        Ok(validated) => validated.0,
4832        Err(_) => {
4833            return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidGrant(
