4834                "claim_token is not a valid access token".into(),
4835            ));
4836        }
4837    };
4838
4839    let Ok(subject_id) = claims.sub.parse::<Uuid>() else {
4840        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidGrant(
4841            "claim_token does not name a user as its subject".into(),
4842        ));
4843    };
4844
4845    // The requesting party must belong to the tenant the ticket was minted in.
4846    // Without this a token from tenant A could redeem a ticket in tenant B and
4847    // be evaluated against B's grants under A's subject id.
4848    if claims.tenant_id != tenant_id.to_string() {
4849        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidGrant(
4850            "claim_token belongs to a different tenant".into(),
4851        ));
4852    }
4853
4854    let subject_remaining_secs = claims.exp - chrono::Utc::now().timestamp();
4855
4856    let granted = match state
4857        .uma_service(authz.get_ref().clone())
4858        .exchange_ticket(
4859            tenant_id,
4860            &client.client_id,
4861            ticket,
4862            subject_id,
4863            subject_remaining_secs,
4864        )
4865        .await
4866    {
4867        Ok(granted) => granted,
4868        Err(e) => {
4869            tracing::info!(
4870                target: "axiam::audit",
4871                event = "uma.rpt_refused",
4872                tenant_id = %tenant_id,
4873                client_id = %client.client_id,
4874                subject = %subject_id,
4875                error = e.wire_error(),
4876                "uma-ticket grant refused"
4877            );
4878            return uma_refusal_response(e);
4879        }
4880    };
4881
4882    let org_id = match claims.org_id.parse::<Uuid>() {
4883        Ok(id) => id,
4884        Err(_) => {
4885            return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::ServerError(
4886                "claim_token carries an unparseable org_id".into(),
4887            ));
4888        }
4889    };
4890
4891    let rpt = match axiam_auth::token::issue_rpt(
4892        subject_id,
4893        tenant_id,
4894        org_id,
4895        granted.permissions.clone(),
4896        granted.lifetime_secs,
4897        // T21.6 — the RPT is a token this request minted, so it names the
4898        // issuer the request arrived under like every other one.
4899        &uma_issuer_config(ctx, state),
4900        cnf.clone(),
4901    ) {
4902        Ok(token) => token,
4903        Err(e) => return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::ServerError(e.to_string())),
4904    };
4905
4906    // Audited before the token is returned: the ticket is already consumed at
4907    // this point, so a crash between issuing and logging would leave a spent
4908    // ticket with no record of what it bought.
4909    tracing::info!(
4910        target: "axiam::audit",
4911        event = "uma.rpt_issued",
4912        tenant_id = %tenant_id,
4913        client_id = %client.client_id,
4914        subject = %subject_id,
4915        pairs = granted.permissions.len(),
4916        lifetime_secs = granted.lifetime_secs,
4917        "RPT issued"
4918    );
4919
4920    HttpResponse::Ok()
4921        .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
4922        .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"))
4923        .json(TokenResponse {
4924            access_token: rpt,
4925            // SEC-096 / RFC 9449 §5: `DPoP` when the RPT is DPoP-bound,
4926            // `Bearer` for everything else — which is every client that did
4927            // not register DPoP binding.
4928            token_type: axiam_oauth2::dpop::token_type_for(cnf.as_ref()),
4929            expires_in: granted.lifetime_secs as u64,
4930            refresh_token: None,
4931            scope: None,
4932            id_token: None,
4933        })
4934}
4935
4936/// Render a [`UmaError`] as the token endpoint's error response.
4937///
4938/// # Why `access_denied` does not go through `build_oauth2_error_response`
4939///
4940/// That helper maps everything except `invalid_client` and `server_error` to
4941/// **400**, which is right for RFC 6749 and right for RFC 8628 — the device
4942/// grant's `access_denied` (the user said no) is a 400 and must stay one.
4943///
4944/// UMA 2.0 §3.3.6 specifies **403** for a request the authorization server
4945/// refuses on the merits, and a UMA-conforming resource server distinguishes
4946/// "you may not have this" from "your request was malformed" by exactly that
4947/// status. Routing the UMA refusal through the shared helper would answer 400
4948/// and quietly fail conformance, so this one status is built here. Every other
4949/// UMA error keeps the shared mapping.
4950fn uma_refusal_response(e: UmaError) -> HttpResponse {
4951    if matches!(e, UmaError::AccessDenied) {
4952        return HttpResponse::Forbidden()
4953            .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
4954            .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"))
4955            .json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
4956                // v1 sends no `need_info` alongside it: that field exists to
4957                // steer a client into claims-gathering, which is deferred, so
4958                // sending it would point at a flow that does not run.
4959                error: "access_denied".into(),
4960                error_description:
4961                    "the requesting party is not authorized for every requested permission".into(),
4962            });
4963    }
4964    build_oauth2_error_response(&uma_error_to_oauth2(e))
4965}
4966
4967/// Map a [`UmaError`] onto the OAuth2 error shape the token endpoint answers.
4968fn uma_error_to_oauth2(e: UmaError) -> OAuth2Error {
4969    match e {
4970        UmaError::InvalidRequest(m) => OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest(m),
4971        UmaError::InvalidGrant(m) => OAuth2Error::InvalidGrant(m),
4972        UmaError::AccessDenied => OAuth2Error::AccessDenied(
4973            "the requesting party is not authorized for every requested permission".into(),
4974        ),
4975        UmaError::ExpiredSubjectToken => {
4976            OAuth2Error::InvalidGrant("the presented claim_token has expired".into())
4977        }
4978        UmaError::Server(m) => OAuth2Error::ServerError(m),
4979    }
4980}
4981
4982async fn handle_token_exchange<C: Connection + Clone>(
4983    tenant_id: Uuid,
4984    form: TokenRequest,
4985    state: &web::Data<AppState<C>>,
4986    ctx: &TokenRequestContext,
4987) -> HttpResponse {
4988    // SEC-093 — see `handle_uma_ticket` for why only `client_id` is required
4989    // before the lookup.
4990    let Some(client_id) = form.client_id.as_deref() else {
4991        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidClient(
4992            "the token-exchange grant requires client authentication".into(),
4993        ));
4994    };
4995
4996    let client = match state
4997        .oauth2
4998        .token_service
4999        .authenticate_client(tenant_id, client_id, form.client_secret.as_deref(), ctx)
5000        .await
5001    {
5002        Ok(client) => client,
5003        Err(e) => return build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
5004    };
5005
5006    // T21.2 — and a public client may not exchange. The admin API refuses the
5007    // grant at registration; this is the request-time half, so a row that
5008    // acquired the combination another way is refused here too rather than
5009    // minting a token for an audience on the strength of a `client_id`.
5010    if client.token_endpoint_auth_method.is_public() {
5011        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::UnauthorizedClient(
5012            "a public client (token_endpoint_auth_method: none) cannot use the token-exchange \
5013             grant: it presents no credential, and an exchange must be attributable"
5014                .into(),
5015        ));
5016    }
5017
5018    // B3: the exchange's own bucket, applied here rather than as route
5019    // middleware because `/oauth2/token` serves every grant and actix
5020    // deserializes the body once — the grant is not knowable until the form
5021    // has been read, which is after the middleware chain has run.
5022    //
5023    // Keyed by the authenticated client, deliberately: an exchange always
5024    // carries client credentials, so unlike the device endpoints there is a
5025    // real identity to key on, and per-IP would collapse a whole mesh behind
5026    // one NAT into a single bucket. Counted AFTER authentication so an
5027    // unauthenticated caller cannot consume a real client's allowance.
5028    //
5029    // `check_at` rather than `check`: it derives the window itself so it can
5030    // also see the OFFSET into it, which is the sliding-window bound the J1
5031    // fix added after run 5 measured boundary over-admission. Re-deriving a
5032    // truncated window here would quietly opt this endpoint out of that fix.
5033    if !state.shared_rate_limit.check_at(
5034        &format!("oauth2_token_exchange:{}:{}", tenant_id, client.client_id),
5035        chrono::Utc::now(),
5036        state.rate_limit_cfg.token_exchange_per_min,
5037    ) {
5038        return HttpResponse::TooManyRequests()
5039            .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
5040            .json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
5041                error: "slow_down".into(),
5042                error_description: "token-exchange rate limit exceeded for this client".into(),
5043            });
5044    }
5045
5046    // SEC-096, gate 1: the profile the REGISTRATION declares, re-checked at
5047    // the moment it matters — the same call the authorization_code,
5048    // client_credentials and refresh_token grants make. Without it a `fapi2`
5049    // client could obtain an unconstrained token through this grant while
5050    // being refused one through the other three, which is a conformance
5051    // failure and an authentication downgrade in the same request.
5052    if let Err(e) = axiam_oauth2::fapi::enforce_token_request(&client, ctx.evidence()) {
5053        return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
5054    }
5055
5056    // SEC-096, gate 2: the confirmation claim the EXCHANGING client earned on
5057    // this request. `None` for every client that registered no binding, so
5058    // this is byte-identical to the pre-SEC-096 response for them; for a
5059    // client that registered binding and presented nothing it refuses rather
5060    // than laundering a bound token into an unbound one.
5061    let cnf = match state
5062        .oauth2
5063        .token_service
5064        .certificate_binding_for(&client, ctx)
5065    {
5066        Ok(cnf) => cnf,
5067        Err(e) => return build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
5068    };
5069
5070    let Some(exchange_req) = form.exchange_request() else {
5071        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest(
5072            "subject_token and subject_token_type are required for the \
5073             token-exchange grant"
5074                .into(),
5075        ));
5076    };
5077
5078    // #613 (T-289): the exchange narrows to `client.scopes`, and for an
5079    // externally registered client those are bounded by the tenant's current
5080    // `dcr_allowed_scopes`. No read for an administrator's client.
5081    let mut client = client;
5082    if let Err(e) = state
5083        .oauth2
5084        .token_service
5085        .apply_scope_ceiling(&mut client)
5086        .await
5087    {
5088        return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
5089    }
5090    let client = client;
5091
5092    match state
5093        .oauth2
5094        .token_exchange_service
5095        .exchange_at(tenant_id, &client, exchange_req, cnf, ctx.issuer.as_deref())
5096        .await
5097    {
5098        Ok(outcome) => {
5099            // Audited BEFORE the token is returned, not after. For an
5100            // impersonation this record is the only surviving evidence that
5101            // the acting party was not the subject — the token deliberately
5102            // carries no `act` claim — so it must not be lost to a crash
5103            // between issuing and logging.
5104            // X4: an external exchange's provenance is recorded in the same
5105            // entry, not a second one. A reader correlating two lines by
5106            // timestamp is a reader who can be made to correlate the wrong
5107            // two, and for a JIT provision this is the only record that a
5108            // partner's IdP created an AXIAM user.
5109            let ext = outcome.external.as_ref();
5110            tracing::info!(
5111                target: "axiam::audit",
5112                event = "oauth2.token_exchange",
5113                tenant_id = %tenant_id,
5114                client_id = %client.client_id,
5115                kind = outcome.kind.as_str(),
5116                subject = %outcome.subject,
5117                actor = outcome.actor.as_deref().unwrap_or("-"),
5118                granted_scopes = %outcome.granted_scopes.join(" "),
5119                audience = %outcome.audience,
5120                external_issuer = ext.map(|e| e.issuer.as_str()).unwrap_or("-"),
5121                external_subject = ext.map(|e| e.external_subject.as_str()).unwrap_or("-"),
5122                federation_provider = ext.map(|e| e.provider_name.as_str()).unwrap_or("-"),
5123                federation_config_id = ext
5124                    .map(|e| e.provider_id.to_string())
5125                    .unwrap_or_else(|| "-".into()),
5126                jit_provisioned = ext.is_some_and(|e| e.newly_provisioned),
5127                "token exchange"
5128            );
5129            HttpResponse::Ok()
5130                .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
5131                .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"))
5132                .json(outcome.response)
5133        }
5134        Err(e) => {
5135            tracing::info!(
5136                target: "axiam::audit",
5137                event = "oauth2.token_exchange",
5138                tenant_id = %tenant_id,
5139                client_id = %client.client_id,
5140                outcome = "refused",
5141                error = e.error_code(),
5142                "token exchange refused"
5143            );
5144            build_oauth2_error_response(&e)
5145        }
5146    }
5147}
5148
5149/// G-A (W2.3) — `grant_type=urn:axiam:params:oauth:grant-type:provider-id-token`:
5150/// a native app signs in with an ID token a federation provider issued to it
5151/// (plan §3.1).
5152///
5153/// The order is the design (see `axiam_oauth2::provider_id_token`):
5154///
5155/// 1. the client authenticates (a public client with `client_id` alone), its
5156///    profile's rules run, it must hold the grant, and its token binding is
5157///    decided — then the `oauth2_provider_id_token` bucket is charged;
5158/// 2. the request's parameters, scopes and target are checked — before the
5159///    provider token is touched, so a malformed request does not spend it;
5160/// 3. `axiam-federation` verifies the token and resolves the user (§3.1
5161///    steps 2–7), every refusal one generic `invalid_grant`; then the
5162///    `login.post_auth` reactor gate runs for that user (CONTRACT §22,
5163///    F4-W2-01), a refusal the same `invalid_grant`;
5164/// 4. consent is resolved for that user and decided (`consent_required` for
5165///    anything needing a ceremony, D-N6);
5166/// 5. the token set is issued, and `federation.native_login` recorded.
5167///
5168/// Every attempt that passes step 1 is audited, accepted or refused, with a
5169/// reason code — never the token, never the nonce.
5170async fn handle_provider_id_token<C: Connection + Clone>(
5171    req: &HttpRequest,
5172    tenant_id: Uuid,
5173    form: TokenRequest,
5174    state: &web::Data<AppState<C>>,
5175    ctx: &TokenRequestContext,
5176) -> HttpResponse {
5177    // --- 1. the client ---------------------------------------------------
5178    let client = match state
5179        .oauth2
5180        .token_service
5181        .authenticate_provider_id_token_client(tenant_id, &form, ctx)
5182        .await
5183    {
5184        Ok(client) => client,
5185        Err(e) => {
5186            if matches!(e, OAuth2Error::InvalidClient(_)) {
5187                // Detached, as at every other grant (§22.3 residual 1).
5188                let tenant_repo = state.tenant_repo.clone();
5189                let audit_repo = state.audit_repo.clone();
5190                let attempted = form.client_id.clone();
5191                let peer = peer_ip(req);
5192                let forwarded = client_ip(req);
5193                actix_web::rt::spawn(async move {
5194                    append_client_auth_failure_audit(
5195                        &tenant_repo,
5196                        &audit_repo,
5197                        tenant_id,
5198                        attempted.as_deref(),
5199                        PROVIDER_ID_TOKEN_GRANT_TYPE,
5200                        peer,
5201                        forwarded,
5202                    )
5203                    .await;
5204                });
5205            }
5206            return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
5207        }
5208    };
5209
5210    // The grant's own bucket, `login_per_min` per tenant and address — the
5211    // preset of the password login it stands in for. Applied here rather than
5212    // as route middleware because `/oauth2/token` serves every grant and the
5213    // grant is known only once the body is read. Keyed on the caller's
5214    // address, not on the client: a mobile app's one public `client_id` is
5215    // shared by every person signing in with it, and a per-client bucket
5216    // would let one caller lock all of them out. And on the tenant (F4-W2-07):
5217    // carrier-grade NAT puts many users of many tenants behind one address,
5218    // and one tenant's traffic must not spend another's allowance. Charged
5219    // after step 1, so an unauthenticated caller cannot spend it for a
5220    // confidential client.
5221    //
5222    // `client_ip` already falls back to the transport peer; a request with
5223    // neither (no forwarded address the rule trusts and no peer) is refused
5224    // rather than pooled into one shared `unknown` bucket, where every such
5225    // caller would spend everyone else's allowance.
5226    let Some(address) = client_ip(req) else {
5227        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest(
5228            "the caller's network address could not be determined".into(),
5229        ));
5230    };
5231    if !state.shared_rate_limit.check_at(
5232        &format!("oauth2_provider_id_token:{tenant_id}:{address}"),
5233        chrono::Utc::now(),
5234        state.rate_limit_cfg.login_per_min,
5235    ) {
5236        return HttpResponse::TooManyRequests()
5237            .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
5238            .append_header(("Retry-After", "60"))
5239            .json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
5240                error: "rate_limit_exceeded".into(),
5241                error_description: "too many native sign-in attempts from this address".into(),
5242            });
5243    }
5244
5245    let client_id = client.client().client_id.clone();
5246    let requested_provider = form.provider.clone();
5247    let audit = NativeLoginAudit {
5248        tenant_id,
5249        client_id: &client_id,
5250        requested_provider: requested_provider.as_deref(),
5251        ip_address: peer_ip(req),
5252    };
5253
5254    // --- 2. the request --------------------------------------------------
5255    let grant = match state
5256        .oauth2
5257        .token_service
5258        .prepare_provider_id_token_grant(client, &form)
5259        .await
5260    {
5261        Ok(grant) => grant,
5262        Err(e) => {
5263            audit.refused(state, None, e.error_code()).await;
5264            return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
5265        }
5266    };
5267    let native = match axiam_oauth2::provider_id_token::native_request(&form) {
5268        Ok(native) => native,
5269        Err(e) => {
5270            // F4-R12-06: a missing `provider`, `id_token` or `nonce`, or an
5271            // over-long nonce, is past step 1 like every other refusal here,
5272            // so it is audited like them — by reason code (`invalid_request`),
5273            // never with the token or the nonce. After the client check and
5274            // the bucket, so an unauthenticated caller cannot write rows.
5275            audit.refused(state, None, e.error_code()).await;
5276            return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
5277        }
5278    };
5279    // F4-W2-05 (D-N14): the tenant's sensitive-scope switch does not depend on
5280    // the user, so it is decided here with the other request checks, before
5281    // the provider token is verified and spent.
5282    let switched_off = !axiam_oauth2::sensitive::requested(grant.scopes()).is_empty()
5283        && !sensitive_scopes_enabled(state, tenant_id).await;
5284    if let Err(e) =
5285        axiam_oauth2::provider_id_token::refuse_switched_off_sensitive_scopes(switched_off)
5286    {
5287        audit.refused(state, None, e.error_code()).await;
5288        return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
5289    }
5290
5291    // --- 3. §3.1 steps 2–7 -----------------------------------------------
5292    // No federation service means no encryption key, so no provider's
5293    // configuration can be read and none can be trusted: the same generic
5294    // refusal as an unknown provider.
5295    let Some(federation) = state.federation.oidc_federation_service.as_ref() else {
5296        audit.refused(state, None, "federation_unavailable").await;
5297        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidGrant(
5298            PUBLIC_REFUSAL_DESCRIPTION.into(),
5299        ));
5300    };
5301    let subject = match federation
5302        .verify_native_id_token(
5303            &state.tenant_repo,
5304            &state.federation.provider_id_token_replay_repo,
5305            tenant_id,
5306            &native,
5307            chrono::Utc::now().timestamp(),
5308        )
5309        .await
5310    {
5311        Ok(subject) => subject,
5312        Err(e) => {
5313            audit.refused(state, None, e.reason_code()).await;
5314            return build_oauth2_error_response(&axiam_oauth2::provider_id_token::refusal_for(&e));
5315        }
5316    };
5317
5318    // --- 3b. the `login.post_auth` gate (F4-W2-01, #828) -----------------
5319    // CONTRACT §22: the event covers every interactive sign-in, after the
5320    // credentials verify and before any session or token exists. This is one,
5321    // so it fires here — through the adapter the SAML, OIDC and usernameless
5322    // passkey paths share (SEC-095), which refuses a `require_mfa` because a
5323    // one-round-trip grant has no step-up to route it into. The provider
5324    // token is already spent at this point; that is the D-N14 rule for every
5325    // user-dependent refusal, and the app obtains a fresh one.
5326    if let Err(reason_code) =
5327        native_login_post_auth(state, req, tenant_id, grant.organization_id(), &subject).await
5328    {
5329        audit.refused(state, Some(&subject), reason_code).await;
5330        let refusal = if reason_code == NATIVE_POST_AUTH_DENIED {
5331            OAuth2Error::InvalidGrant(PUBLIC_REFUSAL_DESCRIPTION.into())
5332        } else {
5333            OAuth2Error::ServerError(
5334                axiam_oauth2::provider_id_token::SERVER_REFUSAL_DESCRIPTION.into(),
5335            )
5336        };
5337        return build_oauth2_error_response(&refusal);
5338    }
5339
5340    // --- 4. consent, for the user the token signed in --------------------
5341    let scope = grant.scopes().join(" ");
5342    let (sensitive, sensitive_switch_off) =
5343        resolve_sensitive_scopes(state, tenant_id, subject.user_id, &client_id, Some(&scope)).await;
5344    let external =
5345        resolve_external_consent(state, tenant_id, subject.user_id, &client_id, Some(&scope)).await;
5346    let consent = axiam_oauth2::provider_id_token::ConsentState {
5347        sensitive,
5348        sensitive_switch_off,
5349        external,
5350    };
5351
5352    // --- 5. the token set --------------------------------------------------
5353    match state
5354        .oauth2
5355        .token_service
5356        .issue_provider_id_token_grant(
5357            tenant_id,
5358            &grant,
5359            &subject,
5360            native.nonce,
5361            consent,
5362            axiam_oauth2::provider_id_token::SignInOrigin {
5363                ip_address: client_ip(req),
5364                user_agent: crate::extractors::client_info::user_agent(req),
5365            },
5366            ctx,
5367        )
5368        .await
5369    {
5370        Ok(issued) => {
5371            // D-N39 (#839): the client has just joined the session the grant
5372            // created — recorded as `/oauth2/authorize` records a code's
5373            // client (B5), because back-channel logout iterates exactly these
5374            // rows. A failure is logged and swallowed, as there: losing a
5375            // logout notification is bad, failing the sign-in is worse.
5376            if let Err(e) = state
5377                .oauth2
5378                .session_client_repo
5379                .record(axiam_core::models::oauth2_client::CreateSessionClient {
5380                    tenant_id,
5381                    session_id: issued.session_id,
5382                    client_id: client_id.clone(),
5383                    user_id: subject.user_id,
5384                })
5385                .await
5386            {
5387                tracing::warn!(
5388                    error = %e,
5389                    "could not record the native client's session participation; \
5390                     back-channel logout will not reach it"
5391                );
5392            }
5393            // Audited before the tokens are returned: for a first sign-in
5394            // this record is the evidence that a provider's token created an
5395            // AXIAM user.
5396            audit.accepted(state, &subject, grant.scopes()).await;
5397            HttpResponse::Ok()
5398                .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
5399                .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"))
5400                .json(issued.response)
5401        }
5402        Err(e) => {
5403            audit.refused(state, Some(&subject), e.error_code()).await;
5404            build_oauth2_error_response(&e)
5405        }
5406    }
5407}
5408
5409/// The audit reason code of a native sign-in a `login.post_auth` reactor
5410/// refused — a veto, a `fail_closed` transport failure, or a `require_mfa` the
5411/// grant cannot honour (F4-W2-01).
5412const NATIVE_POST_AUTH_DENIED: &str = "reactor_denied";
5413
5414/// F4-W2-01 (#828): fire `login.post_auth` for a verified native sign-in.
5415///
5416/// The user is re-read from the tenant the subject was resolved in, so the
5417/// reactor sees the same payload the password, SAML, OIDC and usernameless
5418/// passkey paths send (`AuthService::intercept_login_post_auth` builds it
5419/// once for all of them).
5420///
5421/// Returns the audit reason code of a refusal: [`NATIVE_POST_AUTH_DENIED`]
5422/// when the gate refused — answered as the generic `invalid_grant`, which is
5423/// how the token endpoint already answers a `token.pre_issue` veto — or
5424/// `server_error` when the user could not be read.
5425async fn native_login_post_auth<C: Connection + Clone>(
5426    state: &web::Data<AppState<C>>,
5427    req: &HttpRequest,
5428    tenant_id: Uuid,
5429    organization_id: Uuid,
5430    subject: &axiam_federation::native_login::NativeSubject,
5431) -> Result<(), &'static str> {
5432    let user = state
5433        .user_repo
5434        .get_by_id(tenant_id, subject.user_id)
5435        .await
5436        .map_err(|e| {
5437            tracing::error!(
5438                error = %e,
5439                tenant_id = %tenant_id,
5440                "native sign-in could not read the user for login.post_auth"
5441            );
5442            "server_error"
5443        })?;
5444    match state
5445        .auth_service
5446        .intercept_federated_login_post_auth(
5447            tenant_id,
5448            organization_id,
5449            &user,
5450            client_ip(req).as_deref(),
5451            crate::extractors::client_info::user_agent(req).as_deref(),
5452        )
5453        .await
5454    {
5455        Ok(()) => Ok(()),
5456        // The only error the gate produces is its refusal; anything else is
5457        // refused the same way rather than read as an allow.
5458        Err(_) => Err(NATIVE_POST_AUTH_DENIED),
5459    }
5460}
5461
5462/// The `federation.native_login` audit record of one request (step 9).
5463///
5464/// What it records: the client, the provider (the resolved one once known;
5465/// until then the request's name for it, bounded), the outcome, a reason
5466/// code, and whether the sign-in provisioned the user. What it never records:
5467/// the ID token, the nonce, or anything derived from either.
5468struct NativeLoginAudit<'a> {
5469    tenant_id: Uuid,
5470    client_id: &'a str,
5471    requested_provider: Option<&'a str>,
5472    ip_address: Option<String>,
5473}
5474
5475impl NativeLoginAudit<'_> {
5476    async fn refused<C: Connection + Clone>(
5477        &self,
5478        state: &AppState<C>,
5479        subject: Option<&axiam_federation::native_login::NativeSubject>,
5480        reason_code: &str,
5481    ) {
5482        tracing::info!(
5483            target: "axiam::audit",
5484            event = NATIVE_LOGIN_AUDIT_ACTION,
5485            tenant_id = %self.tenant_id,
5486            client_id = %self.client_id,
5487            outcome = "refused",
5488            reason = reason_code,
5489            "native sign-in refused"
5490        );
5491        self.append(state, subject, AuditOutcome::Failure, reason_code, None)
5492            .await;
5493    }
5494
5495    async fn accepted<C: Connection + Clone>(
5496        &self,
5497        state: &AppState<C>,
5498        subject: &axiam_federation::native_login::NativeSubject,
5499        scopes: &[String],
5500    ) {
5501        tracing::info!(
5502            target: "axiam::audit",
5503            event = NATIVE_LOGIN_AUDIT_ACTION,
5504            tenant_id = %self.tenant_id,
5505            client_id = %self.client_id,
5506            outcome = "accepted",
5507            user_id = %subject.user_id,
5508            federation_config_id = %subject.provider_id,
5509            jit_provisioned = subject.newly_provisioned,
5510            "native sign-in"
5511        );
5512        self.append(
5513            state,
5514            Some(subject),
5515            AuditOutcome::Success,
5516            "accepted",
5517            Some(scopes),
5518        )
5519        .await;
5520    }
5521
5522    async fn append<C: Connection + Clone>(
5523        &self,
5524        state: &AppState<C>,
5525        subject: Option<&axiam_federation::native_login::NativeSubject>,
5526        outcome: AuditOutcome,
5527        reason_code: &str,
5528        scopes: Option<&[String]>,
5529    ) {
5530        let accepted = outcome == AuditOutcome::Success;
5531        let (actor_id, actor_type) = match subject {
5532            Some(s) if accepted => (s.user_id, ActorType::User),
5533            _ => (Uuid::nil(), ActorType::System),
5534        };
5535        let metadata = serde_json::json!({
5536            "client_id": self.client_id,
5537            "outcome": if accepted { "accepted" } else { "refused" },
5538            "reason_code": reason_code,
5539            // Caller-supplied until resolved, so bounded like a client id.
5540            "provider": subject
5541                .map(|s| s.provider_id.to_string())
5542                .or_else(|| self.requested_provider.map(truncate_bytes_on_char_boundary)),
5543            "provider_name": subject.map(|s| s.provider_name.as_str()),
5544            "provider_kind": subject.map(|s| s.provider_kind),
5545            "provider_inherited": subject.map(|s| s.inherited),
5546            "user_id": subject.map(|s| s.user_id),
5547            "external_subject": subject.map(|s| s.external_subject.as_str()),
5548            "newly_provisioned": subject.is_some_and(|s| s.newly_provisioned),
5549            "granted_scopes": scopes.map(|s| s.join(" ")),
5550        });
5551        if let Err(e) = state
5552            .audit_repo
5553            .append(CreateAuditLogEntry {
5554                tenant_id: self.tenant_id,
5555                actor_id,
5556                actor_type,
5557                action: NATIVE_LOGIN_AUDIT_ACTION.into(),
5558                resource_id: subject.map(|s| s.provider_id),
5559                outcome,
5560                ip_address: self.ip_address.clone(),
5561                metadata: Some(metadata),
5562            })
5563            .await
5564        {
5565            // Never propagated: a sign-in must not fail because the audit sink
5566            // is unavailable (T-15-04).
5567            tracing::error!(
5568                error = %e,
5569                tenant_id = %self.tenant_id,
5570                "failed to write the {NATIVE_LOGIN_AUDIT_ACTION} audit row"
5571            );
5572        }
5573    }
5574}
5575
5576/// `invalid_client` returns 401 with a `WWW-Authenticate` header per
5577/// RFC 6749 §5.2.  Although the token endpoint uses `client_secret_post`,
5578/// RFC 6749 §5.2 still requires the 401 response to include
5579/// `WWW-Authenticate` indicating the authentication scheme.
5580/// Whether this request came from something that will *render* the answer.
5581///
5582/// Only an explicit `text/html` in `Accept` counts. A missing header, `*/*`
5583/// (curl's default) or an API client's `application/json` all keep the JSON
5584/// body byte-for-byte, so nothing that integrates against AXIAM today can
5585/// observe this.
5586fn prefers_html(req: &HttpRequest) -> bool {
5587    req.headers()
5588        .get(actix_web::http::header::ACCEPT)
5589        .and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok())
5590        .is_some_and(|accept| {
5591            accept
5592                .split(',')
5593                .any(|part| part.trim().starts_with("text/html"))
5594        })
5595}
5596
5597/// The five characters that turn text into markup.
5598///
5599/// Written out rather than pulled from a crate because the set is closed and
5600/// the reason it exists should be visible at the point of use: an
5601/// `error_description` is not always AXIAM's own prose — `par_form_error`
5602/// forwards actix's message, which quotes a field name the caller chose.
5603fn escape_html(raw: &str) -> String {
5604    let mut out = String::with_capacity(raw.len());
5605    for c in raw.chars() {
5606        match c {
5607            '&' => out.push_str("&amp;"),
5608            '<' => out.push_str("&lt;"),
5609            '>' => out.push_str("&gt;"),
5610            '"' => out.push_str("&quot;"),
5611            '\'' => out.push_str("&#39;"),
5612            _ => out.push(c),
5613        }
5614    }
5615    out
5616}
5617
5618/// An authorization-endpoint refusal, rendered for whoever asked.
5619///
5620/// # Why the authorization endpoint gets its own
5621///
5622/// `/oauth2/authorize` is the one OAuth2 endpoint a **person** arrives at.
5623/// When a refusal cannot be redirected to the client — RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1 for a
5624/// bad `redirect_uri` or `client_id`, and every `request_uri` failure, where
5625/// the redirect target is precisely what could not be resolved — the
5626/// specification says the server "SHOULD inform the resource owner of the
5627/// error". A `Content-Type: application/json` body informs a developer reading
5628/// a browser window; it does not inform a resource owner.
5629///
5630/// It is also what a certification reviewer is looking at. Five OIDF modules
5631/// end in REVIEW with instructions of the form "it must show an error page
5632/// saying the request_uri is invalid - upload a screenshot of the error page",
5633/// and the screenshot they were being handed was a raw JSON object.
5634///
5635/// # What it does not do
5636///
5637/// It does not change a single byte for a non-browser caller, and it does not
5638/// invent content: the page renders the same `error` and `error_description`
5639/// the JSON carries, escaped, and nothing else. In particular it echoes no
5640/// `state`, no `redirect_uri` and no `request_uri` — the rule
5641/// [`logged_out_page`] states and for the same reason, since those are
5642/// attacker-supplied strings and this page is served from AXIAM's own origin.
5643/// The code a `request_uri` refusal carries **when it is reported to the relying
5644/// party**, as opposed to rendered for the person in front of the browser.
5645///
5646/// OIDC Core §3.1.2.6 defines `invalid_request_uri` for exactly this state, and
5647/// the OIDF suite asserts it by name: `EnsureInvalidRequestUriError` accepts
5648/// that code and no other, so `invalid_request` — which is what RFC 6749 leaves
5649/// you with and what [`ParService::consume`](axiam_oauth2::par) produces —
5650/// fails two FAPI 2.0 modules the moment the refusal starts reaching the client
5651/// at all. It is also the more useful of the two: a relying party told
5652/// `invalid_request` has been handed a description of a client bug that did not
5653/// happen, while `invalid_request_uri` says the recoverable thing — push again
5654/// and restart.
5655///
5656/// **Narrow on purpose**, and narrow in the same place `is_request_uri_gone` is
5657/// narrow: `request_uri was not issued to this client` is a different failure
5658/// and keeps its own answer, exactly as it does on the login-hop return leg.
5659///
5660/// Returns `None` when the refusal is not about a spent handle, meaning "report
5661/// it as it is". Nothing here changes what a request with no registered
5662/// `redirect_uri` is answered — that is still
5663/// [`authorize_error_response`] rendering the original error.
5664fn request_uri_error_for_client(e: &OAuth2Error) -> Option<OAuth2Error> {
5665    axiam_oauth2::par::is_request_uri_gone(e).then(|| {
5666        OAuth2Error::InvalidRequestUri(
5667            "the pushed authorization request is unknown, expired, or has already been \
5668             used (a request_uri lives 60 seconds and may be spent once); push it again \
5669             and restart the authorization request"
5670                .into(),
5671        )
5672    })
5673}
5674
5675/// Report a `request_uri` refusal to the relying party when the request named a
5676/// `redirect_uri` that client registered, and to the person otherwise.
5677///
5678/// The client is looked up *here* rather than passed in because the caller is
5679/// past [`resolve_authorize_principal`] and holds no registration: this path is
5680/// reached with a principal already resolved, and the pushed request that would
5681/// have carried the client's parameters is precisely what could not be read.
5682/// One repository read, on a refusal path only.
5683///
5684/// A lookup that fails is not a licence to redirect. Anything other than a
5685/// client whose registration lists the exact `redirect_uri` asked for falls
5686/// through to [`authorize_error_response`], which is the answer this endpoint
5687/// gave before the redirect existed.
5688async fn refuse_request_uri_to_client<C: Connection + Clone>(
5689    state: &AppState<C>,
5690    http_req: &HttpRequest,
5691    tenant_id: Uuid,
5692    client_id: &str,
5693    redirect_uri: Option<&str>,
5694    state_param: Option<&str>,
5695    e: &OAuth2Error,
5696) -> HttpResponse {
5697    let Some(uri) = redirect_uri else {
5698        return authorize_error_response(http_req, e);
5699    };
5700    let Ok(client) = state
5701        .oauth2_client_repo
5702        .get_by_client_id(tenant_id, client_id)
5703        .await
5704    else {
5705        return authorize_error_response(http_req, e);
5706    };
5707    if any_redirect_uri_matches(&client.redirect_uris, uri) {
5708        let mapped = request_uri_error_for_client(e);
5709        build_error_redirect(
5710            uri,
5711            mapped.as_ref().unwrap_or(e),
5712            state_param,
5713            &issuer_config(http_req, state),
5714        )
5715    } else {
5716        authorize_error_response(http_req, e)
5717    }
5718}
5719
5720fn authorize_error_response(req: &HttpRequest, e: &OAuth2Error) -> HttpResponse {
5721    if !prefers_html(req) {
5722        return build_oauth2_error_response(e);
5723    }
5724    let status = match e {
5725        OAuth2Error::InvalidClient(_) => actix_web::http::StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED,
5726        OAuth2Error::ServerError(_) => actix_web::http::StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR,
5727        _ => actix_web::http::StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,
5728    };
5729    let code = escape_html(e.error_code());
5730    let description = escape_html(&e.error_description());
5731    HttpResponse::build(status)
5732        .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
5733        .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"))
5734        .content_type("text/html; charset=utf-8")
5735        .body(format!(
5736            "<!doctype html><html lang=\"en\"><head><meta charset=\"utf-8\">\
5737             <meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width,initial-scale=1\">\
5738             <title>This authorization request cannot be completed</title></head>\
5739             <body><h1>This authorization request cannot be completed</h1>\
5740             <p>{description}</p><p><small>Error code: <code>{code}</code></small></p>\
5741             <p>Nothing has been shared with the application that sent you here. \
5742             Return to it and start again.</p></body></html>"
5743        ))
5744}
5745
5746pub(crate) fn build_oauth2_error_response(e: &OAuth2Error) -> HttpResponse {
5747    let status = match e {
5748        OAuth2Error::InvalidClient(_) => actix_web::http::StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED,
5749        OAuth2Error::ServerError(_) => actix_web::http::StatusCode::INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR,
5750        _ => actix_web::http::StatusCode::BAD_REQUEST,
5751    };
5752    let mut builder = HttpResponse::build(status);
5753    builder
5754        .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
5755        .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"));
5756    // RFC 6749 §5.2: 401 responses MUST include WWW-Authenticate
5757    if status == actix_web::http::StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED {
5758        builder.append_header(("WWW-Authenticate", "Bearer realm=\"axiam\""));
5759    }
5760    builder.json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
5761        error: e.error_code().to_string(),
5762        error_description: e.error_description(),
5763    })
5764}
5765
5766// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
5767// Tests (B3: JWKS caching headers wired through the actix handler)
5768// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
5769//
5770// The full RFC-conformance/e2e coverage for `/oauth2/jwks` lives in
5771// `tests/oauth2_*.rs`. These handler-level tests are deliberately narrow:
5772// they call `jwks::<C>` directly against `AppState::for_test` (no DB
5773// migrations, no running `App`/router) since the handler does not touch the
5774// database at all -- just enough actix machinery (`HttpRequest`,
5775// `web::Data`) to prove the `If-None-Match` -> 304 wiring and the
5776// `Cache-Control`/`ETag` headers actually reach the HTTP response. The
5777// cache/ETag/304 *logic itself* (RFC 7232 comparison, rotation, malformed
5778// keys, etc.) is exhaustively covered by `axiam_oauth2::jwks_cache`'s own
5779// unit tests -- this module does not re-test that logic, only the plumbing.
5780#[cfg(test)]
5781mod jwks_handler_tests {
5782    use actix_web::http::header::{CACHE_CONTROL, ETAG, IF_NONE_MATCH};
5783    use actix_web::test::TestRequest;
5784    use surrealdb::Surreal;
5785    use surrealdb::engine::local::Mem;
5786
5787    use super::*;
5788
5789    // Test-only Ed25519 keypair with no real-world value. nosemgrep
5790    const TEST_PUBLIC_KEY_PEM: &str = concat!(
5791        "-----BEGIN PUBLIC KEY-----\n",
5792        "MCowBQYDK2VwAyEAcweT2rPwpUxadO56wIhW1XBoMF63aWOE2UMAVsRudhs=\n",
5793        "-----END PUBLIC KEY-----"
5794    );
5795
5796    async fn test_state() -> AppState<surrealdb::engine::local::Db> {
5797        let db = Surreal::new::<Mem>(()).await.expect("in-memory db");
5798        let auth_config = AuthConfig {
5799            jwt_public_key_pem: TEST_PUBLIC_KEY_PEM.into(),
5800            ..AuthConfig::default()
5801        };
5802        AppState::for_test(db, auth_config)
5803    }
5804
5805    /// **T0.3, server side.** The `return_to` check that resolves rather than
5806    /// parses.
5807    ///
5808    /// `login_hop::validate_return_to` refuses these on spelling; this one
5809    /// refuses them on where they *resolve to*, which is the property that
5810    /// survives an edit to the first. `Url::join` is what turns
5811    /// `//evil.example` into another host and normalises `..` away, so the two
5812    /// checks fail for genuinely independent reasons.
5813    #[actix_web::test]
5814    async fn t0_3_return_to_must_resolve_onto_an_origin_this_deployment_owns() {
5815        let db = Surreal::new::<Mem>(()).await.expect("in-memory db");
5816        let state = AppState::for_test(
5817            db,
5818            AuthConfig {
5819                oauth2_issuer_url: "https://iam.example.com".into(),
5820                ..AuthConfig::default()
5821            },
5822        );
5823
5824        assert!(return_to_is_on_this_deployment(
5825            &state,
5826            "/oauth2/authorize?client_id=oa_1&axiam_login_hop=1",
5827            axiam_oauth2::login_hop::AUTHORIZE_PATH,
5828        ));
5829
5830        for hostile in [
5831            "https://evil.example/oauth2/authorize?x=1",
5832            "//evil.example/oauth2/authorize?x=1",
5833            "/oauth2/authorize/../../admin/users?x=1",
5834            "/api/v1/users?x=1",
5835            "/login?return_to=%2F",
5836            // Resolves to the right path on the wrong host — the case only a
5837            // resolving check can see.
5838            "https://iam.example.com.evil.test/oauth2/authorize?x=1",
5839        ] {
5840            assert!(
5841                !return_to_is_on_this_deployment(
5842                    &state,
5843                    hostile,
5844                    axiam_oauth2::login_hop::AUTHORIZE_PATH
5845                ),
5846                "must refuse {hostile}"
5847            );
5848        }
5849    }
5850
5851    /// The two checks are applied in series and the builder is bound by both:
5852    /// what `build_return_to` emits is what this accepts.
5853    #[actix_web::test]
5854    async fn the_return_to_the_server_builds_passes_the_origin_check() {
5855        let db = Surreal::new::<Mem>(()).await.expect("in-memory db");
5856        let state = AppState::for_test(
5857            db,
5858            AuthConfig {
5859                oauth2_issuer_url: "https://iam.example.com".into(),
5860                ..AuthConfig::default()
5861            },
5862        );
5863        let built = axiam_oauth2::login_hop::build_return_to(
5864            "response_type=code&client_id=oa_1&tenant_id=00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001",
5865        )
5866        .expect("a return_to");
5867        assert!(return_to_is_on_this_deployment(
5868            &state,
5869            &built,
5870            axiam_oauth2::login_hop::AUTHORIZE_PATH
5871        ));
5872    }
5873
5874    #[actix_web::test]
5875    async fn jwks_returns_200_with_cache_control_and_etag_when_no_if_none_match() {
5876        let state = web::Data::new(test_state().await);
5877        let req = TestRequest::default().to_http_request();
5878
5879        let resp = jwks(req, state).await;
5880
5881        assert_eq!(resp.status(), actix_web::http::StatusCode::OK);
5882        assert_eq!(
5883            resp.headers().get(CACHE_CONTROL).unwrap(),
5884            "public, max-age=300"
5885        );
5886        assert!(resp.headers().get(ETAG).is_some());
5887    }
5888
5889    #[actix_web::test]
5890    async fn jwks_returns_304_when_if_none_match_echoes_current_etag() {
5891        let state = web::Data::new(test_state().await);
5892
5893        // First request: discover the current ETag.
5894        let first_req = TestRequest::default().to_http_request();
5895        let first_resp = jwks(first_req, state.clone()).await;
5896        let etag = first_resp
5897            .headers()
5898            .get(ETAG)
5899            .expect("etag present")
5900            .to_str()
5901            .expect("etag is ascii")
5902            .to_string();
5903
5904        // Second request: echo it back via If-None-Match.
5905        let second_req = TestRequest::default()
5906            .insert_header((IF_NONE_MATCH, etag.as_str()))
5907            .to_http_request();
5908        let second_resp = jwks(second_req, state).await;
5909
5910        assert_eq!(
5911            second_resp.status(),
5912            actix_web::http::StatusCode::NOT_MODIFIED
5913        );
5914        assert_eq!(second_resp.headers().get(ETAG).unwrap(), etag.as_str());
5915        assert_eq!(
5916            second_resp.headers().get(CACHE_CONTROL).unwrap(),
5917            "public, max-age=300"
5918        );
5919    }
5920
5921    #[actix_web::test]
5922    async fn jwks_returns_200_when_if_none_match_does_not_match() {
5923        let state = web::Data::new(test_state().await);
5924        let req = TestRequest::default()
5925            .insert_header((IF_NONE_MATCH, "\"stale-etag-from-before-rotation\""))
5926            .to_http_request();
5927
5928        let resp = jwks(req, state).await;
5929
5930        assert_eq!(resp.status(), actix_web::http::StatusCode::OK);
5931    }
5932}
5933
5934// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
5935// B5 — Pushed Authorization Requests (RFC 9126)
5936// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
5937
5938/// Form body accepted by `POST /oauth2/par`.
5939///
5940/// These are the ordinary authorization-request parameters, plus the client
5941/// credentials that make the push attributable. `request_uri` and `request`
5942/// are modelled only so that they can be refused: RFC 9126 §2.1 forbids
5943/// pushing a `request_uri`, because a chained push would let the second
5944/// request inherit the first's authentication, and AXIAM accepts no request
5945/// object on any carrier (`request_not_supported`).
5946#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, utoipa::ToSchema)]
5947pub struct PushedAuthorizationRequest {
5948    pub client_id: String,
5949    /// Optional since SEC-093: a client registered for `tls_client_auth` or
5950    /// `private_key_jwt` has no secret to present here, and presenting one no
5951    /// longer authenticates it.
5952    pub client_secret: Option<String>,
5953    /// RFC 7521 §4.2 — `private_key_jwt` client authentication at PAR.
5954    pub client_assertion: Option<String>,
5955    pub client_assertion_type: Option<String>,
5956    pub response_type: String,
5957    pub redirect_uri: String,
5958    pub scope: Option<String>,
5959    pub state: Option<String>,
5960    pub code_challenge: Option<String>,
5961    pub code_challenge_method: Option<String>,
5962    pub nonce: Option<String>,
5963    // X7.1 — pushed alongside the original seven, because PAR is the only
5964    // carrier a `require_par` client has.
5965    pub prompt: Option<String>,
5966    pub max_age: Option<String>,
5967    pub acr_values: Option<String>,
5968    pub claims: Option<String>,
5969    pub id_token_hint: Option<String>,
5970    pub login_hint: Option<String>,
5971    pub display: Option<String>,
5972    pub ui_locales: Option<String>,
5973    pub claims_locales: Option<String>,
5974    /// RFC 9126 §2.1 — the one authorization parameter a client may NOT push.
5975    ///
5976    /// Modelled so that it can be refused. Leaving it off the struct made
5977    /// `serde` drop it silently and the endpoint answer `201` to a request the
5978    /// specification says must be rejected:
5979    ///
5980    /// > The `request_uri` authorization request parameter is one exception,
5981    /// > and it MUST NOT be provided.
5982    ///
5983    /// Refusing matters beyond conformance. A `request_uri` accepted here would
5984    /// be a pushed request that names another pushed request, and the second
5985    /// would inherit the client authentication of the first — a chain whose
5986    /// authenticated origin is a different request from the one finally
5987    /// presented at the authorization endpoint.
5988    pub request_uri: Option<String>,
5989    /// RFC 9101 `request` — a request object by value. AXIAM does not accept
5990    /// one on either carrier (X7 G12), and this field exists so that it can be
5991    /// **refused** here with the code the authorization endpoint already gives
5992    /// it, `request_not_supported`.
5993    ///
5994    /// Leaving it off the struct had serde drop it and the endpoint answer
5995    /// `201`, which made the two carriers disagree about the same parameter —
5996    /// and the disagreement was not cosmetic (T23.1.1). RFC 9126 §3 lets a
5997    /// client push a request object, and RFC 9101 §6.3 tells it the server
5998    /// will use only the object's parameters; a client that put `max_age=0`
5999    /// or `prompt=login` inside one, beside the bare required fields, had
6000    /// those security-bearing parameters silently discarded, so they never
6001    /// reached the `fapi2` gate that refuses them or the honour lane that
6002    /// acts on them. Refusing the object is the only answer that does not
6003    /// tell the client a guarantee was applied when it was not.
6004    pub request: Option<String>,
6005    /// RFC 9449 §10 — the JWK thumbprint of the key the client will prove
6006    /// possession of at the token endpoint.
6007    ///
6008    /// One of the two carriers §10.1 requires an AS supporting both PAR and
6009    /// DPoP to accept; the other is a `DPoP` header on this same request. When
6010    /// both arrive they must agree, and the handler refuses the request when
6011    /// they do not.
6012    pub dpop_jkt: Option<String>,
6013    /// T21.3 / RFC 8707 §2 — the target service this request is for.
6014    ///
6015    /// Pushed alongside the parameters above it, for the reason they are: PAR
6016    /// and the query string are two carriers of one request, and for a
6017    /// `require_par` client PAR is the only carrier there is. Validated here,
6018    /// where the client is authenticated, so an `invalid_target` reaches the
6019    /// client as a protocol error rather than surfacing in a browser after a
6020    /// sign-in the user should never have been asked for.
6021    pub resource: Option<String>,
6022    /// G-B (#817) — AXIAM's provider hint, pushed like `login_hint`; see the
6023    /// authorization endpoint's `axiam_idp_hint`. Never refused: a hint that
6024    /// names nothing is answered as no hint.
6025    pub axiam_idp_hint: Option<String>,
6026}
6027
6028/// `POST /oauth2/par` success body (RFC 9126 §2.2).
6029#[derive(Debug, Serialize, utoipa::ToSchema)]
6030pub struct PushedAuthorizationResponse {
6031    /// Opaque, single-use, `urn:ietf:params:oauth:request_uri:`-prefixed.
6032    pub request_uri: String,
6033    /// Seconds until the `request_uri` expires.
6034    pub expires_in: i64,
6035}
6036
6037/// Render a malformed PAR body as an OAuth2 error object (RFC 9126 §2.3).
6038///
6039/// # Why this exists
6040///
6041/// `web::Form` rejects a body it cannot deserialize *before* the handler runs,
6042/// and actix's default rendering is `400` with a `text/plain` body — for a
6043/// missing `redirect_uri`, literally:
6044///
6045/// ```text
6046/// Parse error: missing field `redirect_uri`.
6047/// ```
6048///
6049/// RFC 9126 §2.3 says the PAR endpoint's error response is the token
6050/// endpoint's: a JSON object carrying `error` and optionally
6051/// `error_description`. A client cannot act on prose, and a conformance suite
6052/// reports it as "Pushed Authorization did not return a JSON object" — which
6053/// is what two FAPI 2.0 modules did.
6054///
6055/// The description is deliberately actix's own message: the caller already
6056/// controls every byte of the body being described, so naming the field it got
6057/// wrong tells an attacker nothing and saves an integrator an afternoon.
6058///
6059/// Wired at the route rather than inside the handler because the handler is
6060/// never reached — see `crate::server`'s `/par` resource.
6061/// The message `serde` produces for a repeated field, for the one field this
6062/// server has to answer for specially (T21.3 / D1).
6063///
6064/// Pinned by [`the_duplicate_field_message_is_what_serde_still_says`] so that
6065/// a `serde` upgrade which reworded it fails a test rather than silently
6066/// reverting the error code below to `invalid_request`.
6067const DUPLICATE_RESOURCE_MESSAGE: &str = "duplicate field `resource`";
6068
6069/// Answer a repeated `resource` parameter with `invalid_target` (D1), or
6070/// `None` when the failure is anything else.
6071///
6072/// # Why a string comparison, and why that is not load-bearing
6073///
6074/// RFC 8707 §2 lets `resource` repeat; D1 of the MCP plan accepts one value
6075/// and refuses a second, because AXIAM's access token carries a single `aud`
6076/// (`AccessTokenClaims::aud`) and there is no honest way to serve two targets
6077/// with one string.
6078///
6079/// The refusal itself is **not** implemented here. `serde_urlencoded`
6080/// deserializes a repeated key into a non-sequence field by failing, so a
6081/// second `resource` never reaches a handler at all: the request is refused
6082/// before any of this runs, and it is refused whatever this function returns.
6083/// That is the fail-closed property, and it holds by construction — there is
6084/// no path on which two values are silently narrowed to one.
6085///
6086/// What this function buys is the *code*: a client that repeats the parameter
6087/// is told `invalid_target`, which is what RFC 8707 defines and what a client
6088/// can act on, instead of a `400` describing a deserializer. If the string
6089/// ever stops matching, the answer degrades to that `400` — still a refusal,
6090/// merely a less useful one.
6091fn duplicate_resource_response(message: &str) -> Option<HttpResponse> {
6092    message.contains(DUPLICATE_RESOURCE_MESSAGE).then(|| {
6093        build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidTarget(
6094            "resource may be given at most once: AXIAM issues a token for one target service              (RFC 8707 section 2). Start a separate authorization for each resource"
6095                .into(),
6096        ))
6097    })
6098}
6099
6100/// `QueryConfig` error handler for `/oauth2/authorize` (T21.3 / D1).
6101///
6102/// Answers a repeated `resource` with `invalid_target`, and **delegates every
6103/// other failure to actix unchanged** — `err.into()` is precisely what actix
6104/// does when no handler is registered, so a malformed query that fails for any
6105/// other reason gets byte-for-byte the response it got before this existed
6106/// (I1).
6107///
6108/// The refusal is not redirected, and cannot be: this runs before the handler,
6109/// so no client has been looked up and no `redirect_uri` has been validated.
6110/// RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1 is explicit that an error may only be redirected once the
6111/// redirect URI is known good, so a direct response is the only correct answer
6112/// here.
6113pub fn authorize_query_error(
6114    err: actix_web::error::QueryPayloadError,
6115    _req: &HttpRequest,
6116) -> actix_web::Error {
6117    match duplicate_resource_response(&err.to_string()) {
6118        Some(body) => actix_web::error::InternalError::from_response(err, body).into(),
6119        None => err.into(),
6120    }
6121}
6122
6123/// `FormConfig` error handler for `/oauth2/token` (T21.3 / D1).
6124///
6125/// The token endpoint's twin of [`authorize_query_error`], and it keeps the
6126/// same discipline: only a repeated `resource` is answered differently, and
6127/// every other body actix could not deserialize produces exactly today's
6128/// response (I1). The token endpoint has never had a form error handler, so
6129/// this is the first one, and it is deliberately not an opportunity to make
6130/// every malformed body render as JSON — that would be a behaviour change for
6131/// clients that have been getting the `text/plain` answer for years.
6132pub fn token_form_error(
6133    err: actix_web::error::UrlencodedError,
6134    _req: &HttpRequest,
6135) -> actix_web::Error {
6136    match duplicate_resource_response(&err.to_string()) {
6137        Some(body) => actix_web::error::InternalError::from_response(err, body).into(),
6138        None => err.into(),
6139    }
6140}
6141
6142pub fn par_form_error(
6143    err: actix_web::error::UrlencodedError,
6144    _req: &HttpRequest,
6145) -> actix_web::Error {
6146    // T21.3 / D1 — a repeated `resource` gets RFC 8707's own code. Everything
6147    // else keeps the `invalid_request` this endpoint has answered since B5;
6148    // the body was already JSON here, so this arm changes one member of a
6149    // response shape that is otherwise untouched.
6150    let body = duplicate_resource_response(&err.to_string()).unwrap_or_else(|| {
6151        build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest(err.to_string()))
6152    });
6153    actix_web::error::InternalError::from_response(err, body).into()
6154}
6155
6156/// `POST /oauth2/par` — RFC 9126 (B5).
6157///
6158/// Accepts an authorization request over a direct, client-authenticated POST
6159/// and returns an opaque `request_uri` to put in the browser redirect instead
6160/// of the parameters. What travels through the user agent is then a random
6161/// string that cannot be edited into meaning something else.
6162///
6163/// **Authenticated, unlike `/oauth2/device_authorization`.** That is the whole
6164/// point: the parameters stop travelling through the browser, and the ones
6165/// that arrive are attributable to a client that proved it holds the secret.
6166///
6167/// Answers `201`, not `200` — RFC 9126 §2.2 specifies Created, and the
6168/// response names a resource that did not exist before the call.
6169#[utoipa::path(
6170    post,
6171    path = "/oauth2/par",
6172    tag = "oauth2",
6173    params(TenantQuery),
6174    request_body(
6175        content_type = "application/x-www-form-urlencoded",
6176        content = PushedAuthorizationRequest,
6177    ),
6178    responses(
6179        (status = 201, description = "Pushed authorization request stored",
6180         body = PushedAuthorizationResponse),
6181        (status = 400, description = "OAuth2 error", body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
6182        (status = 401, description = "Client authentication failed",
6183         body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
6184    ),
6185)]
6186pub async fn pushed_authorization_request<C: Connection + Clone>(
6187    http_req: HttpRequest,
6188    tenant_query: web::Query<TenantQuery>,
6189    form: web::Form<PushedAuthorizationRequest>,
6190    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
6191) -> HttpResponse {
6192    // W8 / RFC 6749 §5.2 — the challenge is decided from the request,
6193    // before the handler runs, and applied to whatever 401 it produces.
6194    let challenge = client_auth_challenge(&http_req);
6195    with_client_auth_challenge(
6196        pushed_authorization_request_inner(http_req, tenant_query, form, state).await,
6197        challenge,
6198    )
6199}
6200
6201/// See [`token_inner`] for why this split exists.
6202async fn pushed_authorization_request_inner<C: Connection + Clone>(
6203    http_req: HttpRequest,
6204    tenant_query: web::Query<TenantQuery>,
6205    form: web::Form<PushedAuthorizationRequest>,
6206    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
6207) -> HttpResponse {
6208    let tenant_id = tenant_query.into_inner().tenant_id;
6209    let req = form.into_inner();
6210
6211    // RFC 9126 §2.1-2, answered before anything else is looked at.
6212    //
6213    // Ahead of client authentication deliberately, and it costs nothing: the
6214    // refusal names a parameter the caller sent rather than anything about the
6215    // client, so it is not an oracle, and a request that cannot be honoured
6216    // whoever sent it should not first consume an authentication.
6217    if req.request_uri.is_some() {
6218        return build_oauth2_error_response(&OAuth2Error::InvalidRequest(
6219            "request_uri must not be provided to the pushed authorization request endpoint \
6220             (RFC 9126 §2.1)"
6221                .into(),
6222        ));
6223    }
6224
6225    // X7 G12 on the second carrier (T23.1.1). A request object by value gets
6226    // the answer `/oauth2/authorize` gives it, and for the same reason: AXIAM
6227    // reads no request object, so accepting one would mean serving the
6228    // request without the parameters it carried. Decided by the same
6229    // classifier the authorization endpoint uses, so a blank template value
6230    // is "no object" on both carriers rather than on one. Before client
6231    // authentication for the reason the `request_uri` refusal above is: it
6232    // names a parameter the caller sent and nothing about the client.
6233    if let Some(object) = classify_request_object(req.request.as_deref(), None) {
6234        return build_oauth2_error_response(&object.into_error());
6235    }
6236
6237    // T21.5 — before client authentication, as at the token endpoint.
6238    crate::cimd::materialise_if_cimd(&state, &http_req, tenant_id, &req.client_id).await;
6239
6240    // One client-authentication path in the codebase, shared with the token
6241    // endpoint, rather than a second one to keep correct — and since SEC-093
6242    // that path honours the registered `token_endpoint_auth_method`. PAR is
6243    // the sharpest instance of the old bug: FAPI 2.0 §5.3.1.1 requires strong
6244    // client authentication AND requires PAR, so a FAPI deployment whose PAR
6245    // endpoint accepted a shared secret was both an authentication downgrade
6246    // and a conformance failure.
6247    let ctx = match token_request_context(&http_req) {
6248        Ok(ctx) => ctx.with_assertion(
6249            req.client_assertion.as_deref(),
6250            req.client_assertion_type.as_deref(),
6251        ),
6252        Err(response) => return *response,
6253    };
6254    let client = match state
6255        .oauth2
6256        .token_service
6257        .authenticate_client(
6258            tenant_id,
6259            &req.client_id,
6260            req.client_secret.as_deref(),
6261            &ctx,
6262        )
6263        .await
6264    {
6265        Ok(client) => client,
6266        Err(e) => return build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
6267    };
6268    // D-17 (T23.1.5) — the profile's client-authentication rule at request
6269    // time, after authentication and before anything is pushed: a `fapi2` row
6270    // edited to a shared-secret method is refused here as at the token
6271    // endpoint. Only this rule, not `enforce_token_request` whole: the DPoP
6272    // proof that would ask for is verified further down, after this point.
6273    if let Err(e) = axiam_oauth2::fapi::enforce_client_authentication(&client) {
6274        return build_oauth2_error_response(&e);
6275    }
6276    // Cloned because `client.client_id` is moved into the pushed request
6277    // below, and the §10.1 refusal between here and there wants to name the
6278    // client it is refusing.
6279    let client_id_for_log = client.client_id.clone();
6280
6281    // Keyed by the authenticated client and counted AFTER authentication, for
6282    // the same reasons as the token exchange: PAR always carries credentials
6283    // so there is a real identity to key on, per-IP would collapse a whole
6284    // deployment behind one NAT into a single bucket, and counting before
6285    // authentication would let an unauthenticated caller burn a real client's
6286    // allowance.
6287    if !state.shared_rate_limit.check_at(
6288        &format!("oauth2_par:{}:{}", tenant_id, client.client_id),
6289        chrono::Utc::now(),
6290        state.rate_limit_cfg.par_per_min,
6291    ) {
6292        return HttpResponse::TooManyRequests()
6293            .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
6294            .json(OAuth2ErrorResponse {
6295                error: "slow_down".into(),
6296                error_description: "PAR rate limit exceeded for this client".into(),
6297            });
6298    }
6299
6300    // RFC 9449 §10.1 — resolve the DPoP key this authorization binds to.
6301    //
6302    //   Both mechanisms MUST be supported by an authorization server that
6303    //   supports PAR and DPoP.  If both mechanisms are used at the same time,
6304    //   the authorization server MUST reject the request if the JWK Thumbprint
6305    //   in dpop_jkt does not match the public key in the DPoP header.
6306    //
6307    // Deliberately after client authentication and after the rate limit.
6308    // Verifying a proof *records its `jti`*, so doing it earlier would let an
6309    // unauthenticated caller write to the replay table at will — and the
6310    // refusals below describe a key, which is a fact about the caller and not
6311    // about the client registration, so nothing is leaked by the wait.
6312    //
6313    // The header alone binds just as firmly as the parameter alone: §10.1 says
6314    // the server "MUST further behave as if the contained public key's
6315    // thumbprint was provided using dpop_jkt". That is the whole reason this
6316    // resolves to a single value rather than carrying both onward — by the
6317    // time the token endpoint asks, there is only one right answer and no
6318    // benefit in it having to reconstruct which carrier produced it.
6319    let proof_jkt = match verify_dpop_header(&http_req, &state, tenant_id).await {
6320        Ok(verified) => verified.map(|v| v.jkt),
6321        Err(response) => return *response,
6322    };
6323    let dpop_jkt = match (req.dpop_jkt.as_deref(), proof_jkt.as_deref()) {
6324        (Some(param), Some(proof)) if param != proof => {
6325            tracing::debug!(
6326                client_id = %client_id_for_log,
6327                "PAR carried a dpop_jkt naming a different key from the DPoP proof; refusing"
6328            );
6329            return *dpop_error_response(
6330                "invalid_dpop_proof",
6331                "dpop_jkt does not match the key of the DPoP proof on this request \
6332                 (RFC 9449 §10.1)",
6333                None,
6334            );
6335        }
6336        // Either they agree, or exactly one arrived, or neither did. In all
6337        // three the parameter wins where it exists and the proof supplies the
6338        // binding where it does not — which is the same value in the agreeing
6339        // case, so the arms need not be distinguished.
6340        (Some(param), _) => Some(param.to_owned()),
6341        (None, proof) => proof.map(str::to_owned),
6342    };
6343
6344    match state
6345        .oauth2
6346        .par_service
6347        .push(axiam_oauth2::par::PushedRequest {
6348            tenant_id,
6349            client_id: client.client_id,
6350            response_type: req.response_type,
6351            redirect_uri: req.redirect_uri,
6352            scope: req.scope,
6353            state: req.state,
6354            code_challenge: req.code_challenge,
6355            code_challenge_method: req.code_challenge_method,
6356            nonce: req.nonce,
6357            prompt: req.prompt,
6358            max_age: req.max_age,
6359            acr_values: req.acr_values,
6360            claims: req.claims,
6361            id_token_hint: req.id_token_hint,
6362            login_hint: req.login_hint,
6363            display: req.display,
6364            ui_locales: req.ui_locales,
6365            claims_locales: req.claims_locales,
6366            dpop_jkt,
6367            resource: req.resource,
6368            axiam_idp_hint: req.axiam_idp_hint,
6369        })
6370        .await
6371    {
6372        Ok(resp) => HttpResponse::Created()
6373            .append_header(("Cache-Control", "no-store"))
6374            .append_header(("Pragma", "no-cache"))
6375            .json(PushedAuthorizationResponse {
6376                request_uri: resp.request_uri,
6377                expires_in: resp.expires_in,
6378            }),
6379        Err(e) => build_oauth2_error_response(&e),
6380    }
6381}
6382
6383// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
6384// B5 — RP-Initiated Logout 1.0
6385// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
6386
6387/// Query parameters accepted by `/oauth2/end_session`.
6388#[derive(Debug, Deserialize, utoipa::IntoParams)]
6389pub struct EndSessionQuery {
6390    pub tenant_id: Uuid,
6391    /// A previously-issued ID token identifying the session to end. The only
6392    /// *authenticated* statement of which session and client this is about.
6393    pub id_token_hint: Option<String>,
6394    /// Where to send the browser afterwards. Honoured only on the identified
6395    /// client's allow-list, by exact match.
6396    pub post_logout_redirect_uri: Option<String>,
6397    /// Echoed verbatim on a redirect that actually happens. Never interpreted.
6398    pub state: Option<String>,
6399    /// Fallback identification when no `id_token_hint` is supplied.
6400    pub client_id: Option<String>,
6401}
6402
6403/// `GET`/`POST /oauth2/end_session` — OIDC RP-Initiated Logout 1.0 (B5).
6404///
6405/// Ends the session named by `id_token_hint` and notifies every client that
6406/// participated in it (Back-Channel Logout 1.0), then either redirects to an
6407/// allow-listed `post_logout_redirect_uri` or renders AXIAM's own logged-out
6408/// page.
6409///
6410/// # The redirect is the whole security surface
6411///
6412/// This endpoint is reachable without authentication by design — a user whose
6413/// session has already expired must still be able to complete a logout — so an
6414/// unvalidated `post_logout_redirect_uri` would be an open redirect on an
6415/// unauthenticated endpoint. It is honoured **only** on exact match against
6416/// the identified client's `post_logout_redirect_uris`.
6417///
6418/// A non-matching URI does not fail the request: the session is still ended
6419/// and AXIAM renders its own page. Refusing to log a user out because their RP
6420/// sent a bad parameter would be the wrong failure — they asked to log out.
6421///
6422/// # Why an unverifiable hint cannot end a session
6423///
6424/// Without a verifiable `id_token_hint` there is nothing to identify but the
6425/// browser's own OP-session cookie. It deliberately does **not** fall back to
6426/// "end every session for the subject named in an unverified parameter": that
6427/// is a denial-of-service primitive handed to anyone who knows a user id.
6428///
6429/// # Without a hint `sid`: the cookie, one hop away (T23.1.8, P23W1-10)
6430///
6431/// The browser's OP cookie is `Path=/oauth2/authorize` (and
6432/// `/t/{tenant_id}/oauth2/authorize`), so it never reaches this endpoint: the
6433/// handler could expire it but not read it, and the session row it named
6434/// survived every logout that carried no `sid` (F4 residual P23W1-10). Such a
6435/// request is therefore answered with a `302` to the `/logout` sub-path of the
6436/// authorization endpoint it came through, which RFC 6265 §5.1.4 path-match
6437/// *does* send the cookie to (`end_session_at_cookie_path`). That hop
6438/// reads the cookie, revokes the one row it names in this tenant, clears every
6439/// copy and continues exactly as this handler would have. The continuation
6440/// carries only what this handler would have used — `post_logout_redirect_uri`,
6441/// `state`, the effective `client_id` and (on the deployment-wide path) the
6442/// tenant — and the hop validates it exactly as here, so it is no open
6443/// redirect. The bounce sets no cookie: a removal on this response would be
6444/// applied before the browser follows it, and the hop would find nothing.
6445///
6446/// # No confirmation prompt
6447///
6448/// RP-Initiated Logout 1.0 §2 permits one, and a prompt is the mitigation for
6449/// logout CSRF. We take the other side deliberately: a forced logout is a
6450/// nuisance, not a privilege escalation, and a prompt shown on every logout is
6451/// trained away within a week. The cookie hop does not move that line: a
6452/// forged navigation here already cleared every cookie this browser holds, and
6453/// revoking the row those cookies named changes nothing anyone but that browser
6454/// can observe — which is why the hop does **not** fan out back-channel logout.
6455/// Telling every relying party is reserved for a request that proves which
6456/// session it means with a signed hint.
6457#[utoipa::path(
6458    get,
6459    path = "/oauth2/end_session",
6460    tag = "oauth2",
6461    params(EndSessionQuery),
6462    responses(
6463        (status = 302, description = "Session ended; redirected to an allow-listed URI — \
6464                                      or, with no id_token_hint sid, to \
6465                                      /oauth2/authorize/logout, which can read the OP \
6466                                      browser-session cookie and ends the session it names"),
6467        (status = 200, description = "Session ended; AXIAM's logged-out page"),
6468        (status = 400, description = "OAuth2 error", body = OAuth2ErrorResponse),
6469        (status = 500, description = "The session could not be fully ended; cookies \
6470                                      cleared, no redirect"),
6471    ),
6472)]
6473pub async fn end_session<C: Connection + Clone>(
6474    req: HttpRequest,
6475    query: web::Query<EndSessionQuery>,
6476    state: web::Data<AppState<C>>,
6477) -> HttpResponse {
6478    let q = query.into_inner();
6479    let tenant_id = q.tenant_id;
6480    // T21.6 — `None` on the deployment-wide endpoint. Only the logout token's
6481    // `iss` reads it; which session is ended and where the browser may be sent
6482    // afterwards are decided exactly as before.
6483    let tenant_issuer =
6484        crate::middleware::tenant_path::binding_of(&req).map(|binding| binding.issuer().to_owned());
6485
6486    // 1. Identify the session and client. The hint is a *signed* statement of
6487    //    both; `client_id` is an unauthenticated parameter and is only a
6488    //    fallback for choosing the redirect allow-list.
6489    let hinted = q.id_token_hint.as_deref().and_then(|t| {
6490        axiam_oauth2::logout::decode_id_token_hint(t, &state.auth_config.jwt_public_key_pem)
6491    });
6492
6493    let (session_id, hint_client_id, subject_id) = match hinted {
6494        Some(h) => (h.session_id, Some(h.client_id), h.subject_id),
6495        None => (None, None, None),
6496    };
6497
6498    // 2. When both are present and disagree, refuse rather than resolve in
